Internal control boards are hidden, and the module images are mirrored from Bambu Studio.
- **New "Safety" tab in Printer Settings (X2D / P2S), mirroring Bambu Studio's Safety Options.** On printers that expose safety options (currently the X2D and P2S), a **Safety** tab now sits next to Print Options with two live read/write controls: **Open Door Detection** (Off / Notification / Pause printing — what the printer does when the enclosure door is opened mid-print) and **Idle Heating Protection** (automatically stops heating after 5 minutes of idle). On these models the open-door control moves here from Print Options, exactly as Bambu Studio does. Open Door Detection is also fixed on the other models that have it: its choices are now the correct **Off / Notification / Pause** (previously mislabelled Off / Pause / Halt) and it sends the correct printer command.
- **Printer Settings toggles now freeze with a "waiting" spinner until the printer confirms, and the dialog updates in place.** Print Options and the Safety tab talk to the printer over MQTT, which has a noticeable round-trip delay — previously a control stayed clickable during that window, so the same toggle could be fired several times before the first even landed. Each control now disables itself and shows a small spinner the instant you click it, releasing once the printer's confirmation arrives, and the value refreshes in the open dialog instead of the dialog closing and reopening. This also rounds out the Print Options ↔ Bambu Studio parity: the legacy **"build plate position detection"** toggle now shows on the models that use it (A1 / A1 mini), and the build-plate and "store sent files on external storage" toggles are sent over the correct MQTT commands so they actually apply on the printer.
- **A paused print that ran out of filament now names the slot the printer is actually waiting for.** The firmware's own runout message says to reload "the same AMS slot", which is wrong whenever **AMS Filament Backup** is on: the printer will not re-accept the depleted slot and has already advanced to the next compatible one. BamDude now reads the printer's target and previous slot during the pause and highlights them on the AMS graphic — amber (with a small ↓ marker) on the slot that needs filament, red on the slot that ran out — while the HMS error dialog spells both out in words ("Filament ran out in AMS-A · Slot 2. The printer is now waiting for compatible filament in AMS-A · Slot 3"). Where the slot genuinely can't be pinned down (an ambiguous multi-AMS setup), it says so and points you at the printer screen rather than naming the wrong slot. Covers regular AMS, AMS-HT and the external spool.
- **Assigning a spool to an AMS slot is now confirmed against the printer instead of being fire-and-forget.** Assigning from Inventory, or configuring a slot from the printer card, previously reported success the moment the command was published — whether or not the tray accepted it. A silently-dropped assignment never surfaced anywhere, and because a print only deducts from the spool on the exact tray it pulls from, it also silently recorded no filament usage. BamDude now reads the AMS telemetry back afterwards and tells you the outcome: **loaded** when the tray echoes the filament you assigned, a **warning** when the filament landed but its flow calibration (K-profile) did not, and **not confirmed** if the tray hasn't reported it after about 30 seconds. If the printer goes quiet it simply stays silent rather than inventing a failure. Works for regular AMS, AMS-HT and external slots.
- **The pile-up, nozzle-clumping, air-printing and displacement toggles now actually apply on the printer** — they were being published under BamDude's internal names instead of the wire names the firmware expects, so toggling them had no effect. They now use the correct MQTT module names. The last three Bambu Studio options are also wired up: the **3-mode Nozzle Clumping** switch (Auto / On / Off) on printers that use the newer smart variant, the **sensor-based Air-print detection** on models that surface it in Print Options (e.g. A2L), and the standalone **AI monitoring** toggle on printers with basic AI but no per-type detections. (These three appear only on hardware that reports them, which none of the local test printers do, so they're implemented to Bambu Studio's spec but not yet field-verified.)

### Changed

- **The optional slicer-API sidecar now pins current slicer versions: OrcaSlicer 2.4.2 and Bambu Studio 2.7.1.62.** The `slicer-api/` compose stack shipped with OrcaSlicer 2.3.2 (March) and Bambu Studio 2.7.1.57. Both pins move to the latest official releases of each; the Bambu Studio 2.8.x line is public beta and was deliberately left out of the default. If you already run the sidecar, your own `slicer-api/.env` still wins — bump `ORCA_VERSION` / `BAMBU_VERSION` there and rebuild to pick the new ones up (a fresh Bambu Studio download is ~220 MB). Nothing changes for installs that don't use the sidecar.
- **Camera streaming can now be pointed at ffmpeg explicitly via a new `FFMPEG_PATH` setting.** The X1 / X2 / H2 / P2 series stream over RTSP, which needs ffmpeg to decode (the A1 / P1 chamber-image protocol doesn't). When ffmpeg is installed but not on the running service's PATH — most commonly a fresh Windows install whose PATH change hasn't reached an already-open shell — the camera connects but shows no frames. Set `FFMPEG_PATH` (in `.env` or the environment) to the ffmpeg binary and BamDude uses it directly, skipping the PATH search entirely. Left unset, behaviour is unchanged — PATH and the usual install locations are searched exactly as before. The Docker image and native installers already bundle ffmpeg, so this is typically only needed for local Windows development.
- **Print-history archives now colour each filament from the spool you actually loaded — per slot, from the moment the print starts.** The colour circles on an archive card/row are taken from the inventory spool assigned to each AMS slot rather than the colour baked into the sliced file, so a part printed from a spool you've curated as pure black no longer shows the slicer's near-black grey. Each slot is handled independently: a slot with a loaded spool uses that spool's colour, a slot without one keeps its sliced colour — previously it was all-or-nothing, so a single unmatched slot discarded every spool colour for the whole print. The colour is written when the print starts (from your built-in inventory) and refined when it finishes; prints tracked through Spoolman keep getting their spool colours on completion as before. This also feeds the archive colour filter and the Statistics colour-distribution graph.
- **Each archive now records the real print duration and how close the slicer's estimate was, and your existing history has been backfilled.** The actual print time (finish minus start) and the estimate-accuracy percentage were previously recalculated on every page load; they're now stored on the archive itself and filled in for all past prints, so the archive list and Statistics read them directly — lighter to load and consistent everywhere. What you see is unchanged (the same actual duration and accuracy shown on each print); this is groundwork that also lets prints be sorted/filtered by estimate accuracy later. Values are recorded only for completed prints, exactly as before.
- **Slimmed the app bundle: the Add-ons tab's device thumbnails and the printer thumbnails now load as static images on demand instead of being packed into the JavaScript.** The accessory pictures (AMS, exhaust fan, …) are served from files on the server, and the printer's own picture in the Add-ons tab reuses the very same image as its printer card (no duplicate copies). Nothing changes on screen — only what ships inside the app.

### Fixed

- **A slow file transfer to the printer is no longer retried on top of itself.** When sending a print, if the upload took longer than the FTP timeout BamDude gave up and immediately started a second attempt — while the first one was still uploading. Bambu printers accept roughly one FTP session at a time, so the second attempt collided with the first and both failed, turning a merely slow transfer into a failed print dispatch. BamDude now waits for the in-flight transfer to actually finish before deciding, and if it completes during that window the send simply succeeds. A genuinely stuck transfer still fails as before, and downloads have worked this way since 0.3.x — uploads had been left out.
- **Switching off an accessory smart plug no longer knocks its printer offline.** Any plug linked to a printer was treated as that printer's power supply, so turning off a chamber filter, an enclosure light or a filament dryer marked the printer offline and left its state unknown — which also stopped the queue from sending it any work. Plugs now carry a **"This plug powers the printer"** switch (on by default, so nothing changes for existing setups); turn it off on accessories. Only the printer's own plug marks it offline when switched off, and only that plug is switched on to wake the printer for a queued print.
- **REST smart plugs now record energy per print and over a date range.** BamDude filed whatever a REST plug reported as a *daily* figure and never had a running total, so those plugs showed no energy on the print archive and a blank figure for any date range — both need a counter that only goes up, which a daily value resetting at midnight can't provide. REST plugs now have a separate **Lifetime Energy path**; fill it in and per-print energy, energy cost and the Stats energy figures all start working. Tasmota and Home Assistant plugs were unaffected.
- **Swapping the filament on the external spool now clears the old inventory assignment.** Changing the spool in the external slot left the previously-assigned inventory spool linked to it, so the slot kept showing — and deducting from — the wrong spool. The check that unlinks a stale assignment only ran when something changed inside an AMS unit, and the external spool isn't one, so an external-only swap never triggered it. It now watches the external slot's own identity (material, colour, tag) and re-runs the check on a change, ignoring the fill percentage so it doesn't fire on every update during a print. **And when an assignment is unlinked, open browser tabs are told immediately** — previously the database was already correct but every open tab kept rendering the old spool until an unrelated refresh, which looked exactly like the fix not working.
- **A print routed to a differently-typed spool is now filed under the material it actually used.** If you mapped a slot in the Print dialog to a loaded spool of a different material than the plate was sliced for — a PLA slice sent to the only loaded PETG spool — the archive still said PLA, even though the PETG spool was correctly debited. That misfiled the run in the archive card, the material filter and the Statistics material graphs. The archive now takes each slot's material from the spool that actually fed it, exactly as it already did for colour, on both the built-in inventory and Spoolman paths. Slots with no matched spool keep their sliced material, so a mixed print stays accurate rather than losing the resolved ones.
- **Pushover Emergency priority was rejected by Pushover itself.** Setting a Pushover provider to priority 2 (Emergency) made every notification fail: Pushover *mandates* a `retry` (how often to re-alert, minimum 30 s) and an `expire` (when to stop, maximum 3 hours) for Emergency alerts, and BamDude never sent them, so the message was refused before it left the app. Priority 2 now works — two optional fields, **Emergency Retry** and **Emergency Expire**, appear on the Pushover provider *only when priority is set to 2*, default to 60 s / 1 hour, and are clamped to Pushover's legal range. They're sent only at priority 2, since Pushover ignores them otherwise.
- **Starting AMS drying now tells you whether it actually started.** Pressing Start used to close the dialog silently, and the printer's acknowledgement was taken as proof — but an ack only means the command was *taken*. If the AMS then refused it for a reason BamDude couldn't see beforehand, nothing said so and the spool sat there wet. You now get a **"Drying command sent"** confirmation immediately, and if the unit hasn't actually begun within 30 seconds — or reports an error — a warning says the printer accepted the command but the AMS never started drying. Stopping drying confirms too. Each AMS is watched separately, so starting a cycle on a second unit no longer loses the first unit's verdict, and a unit still cooling down from a previous cycle is not mistaken for one that started.
- **AMS drying on a P1P / P1S can only be started at the printer, so BamDude no longer offers it.** Bambu's own P1 manual is explicit: "P1S connected AMS drying functions may only be controlled from the P1S screen." The firmware takes the drying command, answers *success*, and then discards it — which is why a P1S with an AMS 2 Pro sat at zero dry status no matter how many times Start Drying was pressed. **The control stays where it is**: the flame button remains on the printer card, disabled, with a tooltip explaining that drying on this printer is screen-only — the point is to learn *where* to dry, not to watch the button quietly disappear. A cycle you started at the printer still shows in BamDude with its live countdown, because reading the state was never the problem; only the Stop button goes away, since a P1 ignores stop exactly as it ignores start. Automatic and ambient drying skip P1 printers for the same reason, and both drying endpoints now refuse with a clear message instead of reporting a success the printer never delivered. This also covers P1 printers stored under their internal model code (C11 / C12), which previously weren't gated at all.
- **Moving the build plate no longer runs with the printer's travel limits switched off.** Every bed-jog BamDude sent wrapped the move in an `M211 S0` / `M211 S1` pair meant to bypass the soft endstops — and the UI sent that on *every* jog, not just the one the warning dialog asked about. Worse, `M211 S<number>` is not a command Bambu's firmware actually has: in its dialect a bare `M211 S` means "remember the current endstop state" and `M211 R` means "restore it", so what BamDude sent most likely did nothing useful at all while the move ran unprotected. Bambu's own sliced start G-code turns the soft endstops **off** and mostly never turns them back on, which is why Bambu Studio explicitly re-enables them before every manual move. BamDude now sends exactly the same sequence Bambu Studio does — remember the endstop state, enable all three axes, move, restore — so a manual bed move is clamped at the travel limit the way the printer's own touchscreen clamps it. The per-model direction handling for A1 / A1 Mini bed-slingers is unchanged.
- **The "printer is not homed" dialog no longer offers to move anyway.** That button was the one that drove the plate with the limits disabled. It is gone; **Auto Home** is now the way forward, exactly as in Bambu Studio, which refuses a manual move outright while the axis is not homed. Once homing finishes, jogging works normally for the rest of the session. The jog panel also carries a standing note that travel limits may still not stop a manual move — the printer reports no axis position, so BamDude cannot clamp it either.
- **Editing a project from the Projects list no longer wipes its tags, due date or priority.** The edit dialog is shared between the list and the project page and fills itself from whichever project it is handed — but the list payload never carried those three fields, so opening the dialog from a project card showed an empty tags box and an empty due date, and saving quietly wrote the dialog's default priority over a stored **High** or **Urgent**. The list now carries them. Clearing a field works too: emptying tags or a due date and saving now actually clears it, where before the change was silently dropped and the old value came back. Project templates now also carry their target part count, which the same dialog edits.
- **Backups taken on PostgreSQL now carry the full database schema.** The portable SQLite copy inside each backup ZIP was rebuilt with only column names and types — required fields, default values, relationships, uniqueness rules and binary columns were all dropped. Restoring such a backup onto a SQLite install copies that weakened schema onto the live database, and it cannot be repaired afterwards, so newly-created records (a printer, a user group, a macro, a saved setting) could be written without their creation timestamp and then broke the pages that list them. The portable copy is now produced from the real schema definition, identical to what a native SQLite install has. **Re-take your backups after upgrading** — an existing backup file still carries the old, weakened schema. PostgreSQL restores and SQLite-to-PostgreSQL migration were never affected.
- **An unwritable backup folder is now explained instead of reported as "[Errno 30]".** Scheduled backups to a NAS share could run for months and then stop, failing with a read-only-filesystem error while the same folder was perfectly writable from your own shell — because BamDude's systemd service runs sandboxed and treats every path outside its install, data and log folders as read-only. Nothing about the folder itself was wrong, so no permission check could ever find it. BamDude now tests the folder with a real write when you save the path and when the backup card opens, names which of the possible causes it is, and hands you the exact command to fix it with your own path already filled in. A failed scheduled run reports the same explanation, and the last failure reason is now shown next to the run instead of a bare "(failed)". Reinstalling no longer silently removes a writable path you added to the service by hand — the installer backs the old service file up and carries those paths forward. In Docker, a backup folder that was never mounted into the container is now flagged: it looks writable but the backups vanish when the container is recreated, and the compose snippet that mounts it properly is shown.
- **Mass actions on the Filament tab: tick the rows you mean, then act on them.** Each row in the table now has a checkbox, with one in the header for everything on the page and one per group that takes all its members. Once anything is ticked a toolbar appears with **Edit · Print labels · Reset usage · Archive** (or **Restore** on the Archived tab) **· Delete**. Bulk **archive, restore and delete are new** — previously the only mass action was editing, and it worked on *whatever the filter happened to show* rather than on a set you chose. That behaviour is still one click away as **“Select all N matching the filter”** in the toolbar, but it is now something you ask for rather than something that happens by default, so a Delete can no longer land on rows you never looked at. Destructive actions confirm first, and the selection clears itself whenever the filter, search or tab changes so the count can never describe a list you are no longer looking at.
- **All of this works in Spoolman mode too**, where mass editing was previously impossible — none of the endpoints existed. When some rows fail (Spoolman is a separate service that can be briefly unreachable) you now get an honest “7 done, 2 failed” instead of a success message, and the selection is kept so you can retry the rest.
- **The bulk-edit dialog no longer asks you to choose the spools twice.** It used to open with its own checkbox list, because it *was* the selection mechanism; now that selection lives on the page, that list is read-only — it still shows exactly which spools are about to change, which is the last thing standing between you and editing the wrong ones.
- **Skip Objects showed the wrong plate's parts on a multi-plate file — and skipping one could cancel the wrong part.** In a 3MF containing several plates each plate numbers its objects independently, and BamDude was reading the first plate's list no matter which plate was actually printing. The names looked plausible, so the mistake was invisible: you would ask to skip one part and the printer would drop whichever part happened to carry that number on the running plate. The object list is now always read from the plate being printed, on every path that loads it — print start, reloading from the archive, and re-fetching from the printer.
- **You can now skip a part by clicking it on the build-plate preview.** The numbered markers over the plate picture were previously just labels; clicking one now asks to skip that part, the same as its row in the list below. Parts already marked to be skipped are not clickable, and clicking a marker no longer opens the enlarged plate view by accident.
- **A folder's README now sits beside the files instead of on top of them.** The markdown panel rendered as a full-width block above the file grid, so on a folder with any real documentation the model cards were pushed below the fold. It is now a collapsible right-hand rail on wide screens, stacking above the list only on narrow ones. Collapse it and it shrinks to a thin strip you can reopen with one click — and that choice is remembered across folders and reloads. Folders without a markdown file still show nothing at all.
- **A folder's README no longer disappears when its external folder is rescanned.** Rescanning an external folder deleted the database record for any file type the scanner does not itself index — a `README.md` being the one people hit — even though the file was sitting there untouched. The scan now checks whether the file is actually gone from disk before removing its record, and markdown is indexed like the other file types.
- **Visiting the login page while already signed in now takes you to the app.** A valid session that landed on `/login` — most often the address bar autocompleting to it — was shown the sign-in form anyway, which looked like BamDude had forgotten the session even though every request was working. Two-factor and single-sign-on flows are untouched.
- **The print-progress toast fits on a phone again.** Installed as a PWA on an iPhone the toast is wider than the screen, so it ran off the left edge and could not be read. It is now capped to the screen width and positioned clear of the home indicator and the landscape notch.
- **BamDude can now be bound to a specific address in Docker via `HOST`.** Useful when a reverse proxy on the same host fronts the app and you want to listen on loopback only (`HOST=127.0.0.1`). The default is unchanged, and the native installers already had this as `BIND_ADDRESS`.
- **“Prefer lowest remaining filament” now also applies when you print from the Print dialog.** The setting was only ever honoured by AutoQueue. Any print sent the ordinary way — Print → pick a printer → Add to queue — pinned its own slot assignment first, and that assignment is intentionally never second-guessed later (so a mapping you set by hand is never overwritten), which meant the setting was quietly skipped on the path most people actually use. The dialog's auto-match now drains the emptiest compatible spool first, on every path it feeds: the single-printer panel, the per-plate mapping, and the multi-printer fan-out. It only ever breaks a **tie** — an exact colour match still beats a nearly-empty spool of the wrong colour, spools on the wrong extruder are still excluded, and a spool the printer cannot measure is used only when nothing measurable fits. The rule is the same one the automatic queue already used, so both now agree.
- **Multi-colour jobs get their colour stripe back in the queue timeline.** A job printed in more than one filament stores its colours as a list, and the timeline fed that list to the browser as if it were a single colour — which is not valid, so the stripe simply vanished on exactly the jobs where it is most useful. Each colour now gets its own band in the stripe.
- **Scheduled backups keep the right hour on Windows.** Windows ships no timezone database, and the embedded Python in the Windows installer did not carry one either, so a user who set `TZ` to a real zone got the daily backup at the wrong wall-clock hour with only a line in the log. The timezone data is now installed alongside BamDude on Windows. Linux, macOS and Docker were never affected.
- **The AMS history chart's tooltip follows your theme again.** It was still painted from two hardcoded colours while the rest of the dialog moved to theme variables, so on a non-default background it did not match.
- **The AMS backup indicator now opens a view of which slots actually back each other up.** Clicking the backup badge on a printer card used to jump to the AMS settings dialog; it now opens a dedicated **AMS Filament Backup** window showing the on/off switch plus a ring for every backup pair — each ring naming the slots in the order the printer will rotate through them when the printing slot runs dry. Pairing is strict on purpose: two slots are shown as backups only when they carry the **same Bambu filament profile in the same colour**, because that is what the printer's own firmware requires — three PETG spools in different colours will not cover for each other. Slots with a hand-entered filament and no profile never pair. On dual-extruder printers each ring is marked **R** or **L**, since the printer cannot switch across extruders. Full AMS settings are still one click away on the gear button next to the badge.
- **While a print runs, each AMS slot it actually draws from is now marked on the printer card.** A small **P1 / P2 / P3** tag appears in the corner of every slot tile the running job is mapped to, naming which of the print's filament slots comes from there. It is the quickest way to catch a job that was queued for “any printer of this model” and landed on one whose filaments do not match what was sliced — previously there was no way to check that once the print had started. The tag shows while printing or paused and disappears when the printer goes idle.
- **A printer on two networks no longer leaks its second address to the slicer, past the virtual printer.** A virtual printer rewrites the printer's IP in the status it mirrors to Bambu Studio so that sending a file lands on BamDude rather than on the printer directly. It only rewrote the one address BamDude itself connects on — so a printer with both Wi-Fi and Ethernet active (X1C, H2D Pro) still advertised the other one, and the slicer could take it and upload straight to the printer, bypassing the queue, the archive and everything else the virtual printer exists for. Every active interface is now pointed at the virtual printer; inactive ones are left alone so the slicer can still see which links are down. **The rewrite also recovers by itself now:** it used to be set up once, when the connection to the printer was first established, so if the printer's address was not yet known at that moment — a fresh boot, a slow DHCP lease, a printer addressed by hostname before DNS answered — it stayed switched off until something forced a reconnect, which for an idle printer might be never. It is now re-checked continuously, and when it comes online the already-mirrored status is corrected too.
- **Operators and Viewers get MakerWorld back.** Browsing and importing from MakerWorld are permission-gated, and those two permissions were only ever handed out to **newly created** installs — on any database created before they were introduced, including every Bambuddy database imported into BamDude, the Operators and Viewers groups simply never received them and hit "permission denied". An upgrade now grants them: Operators can browse and import, Viewers can browse, exactly as a fresh install has always done. Administrators were never affected. Groups you created yourself are left untouched — their permissions stay yours to set.
- **The archive “Add to Project” menu no longer closes the moment you scroll it.** With more than a handful of projects the submenu scrolls, and any wheel turn, arrow key or scrollbar drag inside it shut the whole context menu — it treated its own internal scrolling as a page scroll. Only page scrolling dismisses the menu now. Two long-standing annoyances in the same menu are fixed with it: projects are listed **alphabetically** instead of in database order, and once there are more than five a **search box** appears at the top of the list — type to filter, Enter picks the first match.
- **Configuring an AMS slot no longer wipes the K-profile the printer is actually using.** The Configure Slot dialog only listed flow-calibration profiles whose filament matched the chosen slicer preset exactly. A spool assigned under, say, "Generic PLA" but calibrated on the printer under a custom profile therefore showed an empty profile list — and because Save needs nothing but a preset, saving sent "no profile" to the printer and **cleared the binding**, while the printer card's hover-card kept showing the profile that was there a moment ago. The dialog now always includes the profile the slot is currently bound to, so it is pre-selected and survives a save. Two related list problems are fixed with it: profiles are matched by name when the preset carries no resolvable filament id — which is every **Orca Cloud** preset, whose list was previously always empty — and a slot with nothing bound no longer has an unrelated profile injected into it.
- **The connection diagnostic no longer reports an unfixable problem for "Store sent files on external storage".** Install step 4 is verified by reading the option straight off the printer — but several models have an SD card slot and no reachable way to switch it on: they never report support for it, so Bambu Studio / OrcaSlicer never draw the toggle, and the P1P / P1S have no screen to set it from either. Those printers were shown a permanently red check pointing at instructions that lead nowhere, which also dragged the whole diagnostic down to "problems". The check is now **skipped** on them, with an explanation of why, instead of failing. Which models can't reach the toggle is resolved from Bambu Lab's own per-model data plus what the printer itself reports (currently P1P, P1S, A2L and X1E), so a firmware update that starts exposing the option re-enables the check by itself. Printers where the setting *is* reachable — X1C, X2D, P2S, the H2 family — still get the same actionable failure as before, and any printer reporting the option **on** still passes.
- **H2C prints sometimes landed in the archive with no filament, layer count or thumbnail.** Those figures are read out of the sliced `.3mf` that BamDude pulls off the printer when a print starts, and on the H2C that download intermittently failed: its encrypted FTP connection negotiated TLS 1.3, which trips the same fault in the printer's FTP server that the P2S and X2D are already capped to TLS 1.2 for. When the file can't be pulled the print falls back to an archive without slice data, hence the empty card. The H2C — including printers reporting themselves as `O1C` or `O1C2` — now gets the same cap. Existing empty cards can be filled in from the archive's **Retry download** action.
- **The finish photo no longer shows the empty plate after an automatic plate swap.** Some printers (notably the A1 Mini) never report the internal "end" stage, so the finish photo fell back to being taken at the moment the print is marked finished — which the printer only announces *after* the End G-code has already parked, swapped or cleared the plate. BamDude now keeps a rolling snapshot taken while the print is still running (refreshed at most every 25 seconds, and once more on the final layer) and uses it whenever the photo has to fall back to the completion moment. Because the snapshot is tied to layer changes it stops updating the instant printing ends, so you get the finished print rather than the aftermath. Snapshots are only taken when finish photos are enabled, are never carried over between prints, and are skipped while you're watching the live camera — or while the finish photo is already being taken — so neither the stream nor the photo itself is disturbed.
- **The virtual printer now shows the real print's progress in Bambu Studio / OrcaSlicer.** When a virtual printer is bound to a real printer, the slicer's Device tab used to show it as permanently idle — no stage, no percentage, no layer count, no time remaining — even while that printer was mid-print. It now mirrors the printer's live progress, so you can watch the job from the slicer you sent it from. The Send button stays enabled throughout, so you can still queue the next job while the current one runs, and the progress view steps aside for a moment whenever you send something new so the upload isn't disturbed. Printer errors are still reported on BamDude's own printer card rather than as a slicer pop-up.
- **Total Print Time no longer counts the hours BamDude was offline.** When BamDude reconnects to a printer and closes out a print that had finished while it was down, the print used to be recorded as ending *at the moment of reconnection* — so a backend restart after a weekend outage silently added the whole gap to that print's duration, and hundreds of phantom hours to Statistics. Such a recovered print is now recorded with its expected duration instead (or, when the slicer estimate is unknown, contributes nothing rather than a made-up figure), and recovered prints are excluded from the estimate-accuracy averages so they can't skew them toward a perfect score nobody measured. A print recovered as *failed* now also says so in its failure reason, making it clear its end time is a reconstruction.
- **A print recovered after a disconnect now gets its time and filament figures back.** Filling those figures in from the print's own 3MF was silently doing nothing, because the file was looked for in the wrong place — so every recovered print was left blank. It now finds the file, and reads the figures from the plate that was actually printed instead of always from the first plate of a multi-plate project.
- **An active print on H2/X-series printers is no longer falsely marked "completed" (and duplicated) when the printer's MQTT connection reconnects.** On an X2D — and any H2-family printer (H2D, H2D Pro, H2C, H2S, P2S) — a running job could flip to "completed" on every reconnect (a stale-connection recovery, or a backend restart), leaving a stuck duplicate archive record for the same print. The cause: these printers report the running file under a generic internal path (`/data/Metadata/plate_1.gcode`) instead of the sliced filename, so the startup reconciliation sweep couldn't recognise the still-running job as *ours* and closed it as "finished while we were away". Reconciliation now also matches on the print's name (the printer reports it reliably), so an actively-printing job on these models is left alone. Other printer models (P1S, A1, etc.) were unaffected and behave exactly as before.
- **The floating "Report a bug" button no longer overlaps the pagination on the Inventory page.** The fixed bottom-right bug-report bubble sat on top of the pagination's right-hand controls — the "last page" arrow in particular — so it was hard to click when the table (or cards) filled the screen. The Inventory pagination now keeps clearance on the right in both table and card views, so its navigation buttons stay fully clickable.
- **Corrected the internal printer-model code table so P1P / P1S and X1 Carbon / X1 are no longer mislabelled.** BamDude's fallback map for slicer "sliced-for" model codes had `C11` / `C12` pointing at X1 Carbon / X1, but per Bambu's own configuration those codes are actually P1P / P1S, and the real X1 Carbon / X1 codes (`BL-P001` / `BL-P002`) were missing entirely. In practice this stayed dormant for connected printers (they identify by full model name), but it could mislabel the "sliced for" model on an archived P1-series job and, when a printer was matched by code rather than name, mis-gate a couple of capabilities (e.g. treat a P1P as if it had an ethernet port or a door sensor). The table now matches Bambu's configuration, and the equivalent front-end map was corrected the same way.
- **The Printer Settings → Printer Parts dialog now shows nozzles correctly on every model.** On dual-nozzle printers (X2D, H2D, H2D Pro, H2C) the dialog previously showed two nozzles with no way to tell them apart — the X2D even showed them completely unlabelled. They're now clearly marked **Left** and **Right** (matching the printer card and Bambu Studio, with the left nozzle in the left column). Single-nozzle printers (P1S, A1, A1 mini, P2S, …) no longer render a phantom empty second nozzle — the stray "Type —, Diameter —, Flow —" block is gone, leaving just the printer's one real nozzle.
- **Print Options now match what each printer actually supports — and their values are read correctly.** The tab's visible options used to come from a rough per-model guess, so some printers were offered toggles their firmware doesn't have while others were missing ones they do: the X2D, for example, showed none of its AI detections (spaghetti, pile-up, nozzle-clumping), foreign-object or displacement detection, or purge-air, while A1 / P1S could surface toggles they don't expose. BamDude now resolves each option's availability the same way Bambu Studio does — from the capability flags the printer reports live — so the list matches the printer (and Bambu Studio) on every model. Several values were also decoded wrongly and are fixed: AI-detector **sensitivity** (Low / Medium / High) was reading the neighbouring detector's bits, and foreign-object, printed-part-displacement, snapshot and build-plate-alignment states weren't being read at all. "Store sent files on external storage" now appears only where the firmware supports it (shown on X2D, hidden on P1S / A1).

- **The Docker image now ships the Tailscale CLI, so the Virtual Printer's Tailscale panel can finally work in Docker.** BamDude reads your tailnet IP and MagicDNS name by asking the local `tailscale` command — but that command was never installed in the image, so in Docker the VP card reported Tailscale as unavailable no matter what you did, including mounting the daemon socket exactly as the log hint asked. The CLI is now part of the image (the daemon still runs on your host, not in the container), and `docker-compose.yml` documents the optional `/var/run/tailscale/tailscaled.sock` mount that connects the two. Nothing changes if you don't use Tailscale — without the mount the card simply shows it as unavailable, as before. Native installs were always fine (they use the host's own CLI).

- **Queueing several plates at once now maps each plate on its own, instead of one mapping decided by a hidden panel.** Ticking more than one plate hid the Filament Mapping panel but did not stop the dialog sending a mapping — it fell back to the whole file's filament list, the union of every plate. Tray assignment is stateful, so where plate 1 prints red on slot 1 and plate 2 prints red on slot 2, slot 1 claimed the only red spool and slot 2 fell through to a type-only match on whatever else was loaded. That single mapping then went out with **every** plate, and the queue uses a stored mapping verbatim — so a plate could print in the wrong colour, decided by a panel you never saw. Each selected plate now gets its own panel (named after the plate), its own tray overrides, and its own mapping on its own queue item. Fanning several plates across several printers ships no mapping at all, so the scheduler maps each plate against the printer it actually picks. Alongside it: a manual tray pick no longer survives a change of printer (a tray number means a different spool on a different machine), the "not enough filament" check now follows what each plate actually dispatches and sums the demand per spool (60 g left doesn't cover two plates of 40 g even though it covers either one), the per-printer tray editor no longer appears for a multi-plate fan-out where its choices were collected and then discarded, and a plate whose filaments can't be read is now named and blocks Print instead of being queued unmapped.

- **Connecting Orca Cloud is now a code you approve, not a URL you paste.** The old sign-in opened a browser tab, redirected you to a `localhost` address that failed to load, and asked you to copy that URL back into BamDude. OrcaSlicer has since shipped a proper pairing API, so connecting is: click **Connect**, approve a short code in your Orca Cloud settings, done. No redirect, no paste, and it works from a LAN address, from `localhost`, or behind a reverse proxy — all cases the old redirect-based flow struggled with. BamDude asks for read-only access (`sync:read`). Anyone connected through the old flow reconnects once.

- **An expired Bambu Cloud sign-in is now detected and named, instead of silently reporting "connected" forever.** BamDude decided whether your cloud sign-in was alive by stamping a 30-day expiry every time it *loaded* the stored token — re-derived from the current moment on every request — so the check could never fail. The Profiles page said "connected" indefinitely while every cloud call was being rejected, and you saw Bambu's own bare "Please login." wherever a feature happened to surface it. Bambu is now the authority: the status endpoint asks Bambu whether the token is still accepted (cached five minutes so the several components polling it don't each pay a round-trip), and a genuine rejection is recorded once and shared, so cloud profiles, MakerWorld, slicer presets and firmware checks all agree at the same moment. The Profiles page now says explicitly that the sign-in expired, rather than just quietly showing the login form again. Crucially, only Bambu's documented expiry answer counts as expiry — a stray 401 (a region- or endpoint-specific refusal, a Cloudflare interstitial, a brief blip) is treated as transient and leaves you signed in, and an unreachable Bambu is reported as "unknown", never as expired, so a cloud outage can't sign you out of a working session.
- **Cloud filament-preset lookups no longer fill the log with warnings for an expected answer.** Many official Bambu presets are only addressable with a printer-variant suffix (`GFSL05` exists solely as `GFSL05_07 @BBL A1`) and personal presets belong to whichever Bambu account sliced the file, so a lookup miss is routine — BamDude already resolves both from local presets. Those misses now log at debug level; a rejected token, a server error or a connection failure still logs as a warning, because those are real faults.

- **New "Use the file's built-in settings" option when slicing a 3MF.** Server-side slicing always applied your picked printer/process/filament profiles, which override whatever the designer configured inside the file — so a MakerWorld model set up for 5 walls came out at your profile's default 2. The Slice dialog now offers a checkbox to slice the file exactly as its author set it up, with walls, infill and filament coming from the file. It appears only when the file actually carries embedded settings **and** the selected printer matches the design's target model, because honouring another model's settings would place the model on the wrong bed. Ticking it locks the profile dropdowns and the bed-type picker, since none of them are used on that path. Everything else slices exactly as before.
- **Filament profiles are now judged by the printers they're actually built for, not just by their name.** Slicing for a P2S could fail with `filament preset Bambu PLA Basic @BBL X1C 0.2 nozzle (slot 1) is not compatible with printer Bambu Lab P2S` — naming a profile shown nowhere in the dialog. The dialog reads a profile's printer scope from its compatible-printers list and falls back to reading the printer out of its name, but that list isn't shipped by every source and names like "Overture PLA Matte @0.2" carry no model at all. Orca Cloud profiles now carry their own list, the same-name bridge shares a known list with copies that lack one, and the name reader understands the two remaining shapes — the full `@Bambu Lab H2D 0.4 nozzle` form user-saved presets get, and a bare `@<size>` tag, which can rule a printer out but is never taken as proof of a match. Nozzle sizes are compared numerically, so "0.20" and "0.2" are no longer treated as different. Separately, when a multi-plate job substitutes filaments into slots a plate doesn't use, it now copies from the plate's lowest **used** slot instead of slot 1 — slot 1 is itself unused on plenty of plates, and anchoring there could propagate another printer's profile into every unused slot and block the slice.

- **P1 / A1 chamber cameras no longer come up black and stay that way for ~20 minutes.** These printers accept only one camera connection at a time, and BamDude was opening a second before the first had closed — so the printer kept feeding a socket nobody was reading, and the live viewer got nothing until the printer's own keepalive reaped the dead one. Three things fed it, all fixed: a viewer joining an already-running stream now gets the last frame immediately instead of waiting for the next one (that wait never fired the browser's load event, so the stall detector reconnect-looped and caused the churn); a replacement stream now waits for the previous socket to actually close before dialling the printer; and stopping a stream keeps its slot reserved while it tears down, instead of leaving a gap that a page reload could slip a second connection through. The page also now needs two consecutive bad readings before reconnecting, so one blip during startup no longer tears down a stream that was about to deliver frames.
- **A stuck camera stream can no longer hang for hours.** After an RTSP read timeout the cleanup killed the stalled ffmpeg and then waited for it to exit with no time limit — and a killed ffmpeg blocked on a dead network socket can take arbitrarily long to be reaped (12 hours in the reported case). The stream sat parked in that wait while every new viewer attached to it and received nothing. All three places that waited are now bounded to two seconds, including the Stop button (which would otherwise hang the very request you pressed to recover) and the background janitor (whose hang would disable the safety net entirely). An unreaped process is abandoned and cleaned up on the next sweep.

- **The Cam Wall now has its own address, and can run on a screen with no login.** It used to be a toggle on the Printers page, so it couldn't be bookmarked, linked, or pinned to a wall-mounted display. It now lives at `/camwall`. Signed in, it's the wall as it was. For a TV or a Raspberry Pi with no login, create a token under Settings → API Keys with the **Cam Wall** scope and open `/camwall?token=…`. That mode is deliberately narrow: it serves only what a tile draws — name, connection, state, progress — and never the printer's serial number, IP address, or the name of the file being printed. Archived printers don't appear; printers in maintenance mode still do.
- **A kiosk Cam Wall can be configured from its URL.** A wall-mounted screen is awkward to configure — you can't open devtools on a TV to set a browser setting — so `?maxLive=`, `?interval=` and `?status=` now set how many tiles stream live, how often the rest refresh, and whether the status overlay shows. Out-of-range values fall back to the defaults rather than leaving you with a wall you can't fix from the same URL, and a kiosk never writes those settings back, so opening a kiosk link once won't overwrite your own wall preferences. A token wall also hides the settings popover — there's nobody standing at it.

- **The OBS streaming overlay works again when you're not signed in.** Everything the overlay draws is behind authentication, so a browser without a session — which is exactly what OBS is — rendered a blank page. (An incognito window failed the same way; remote access was never the cause.) Create a token with the new **Streaming Overlay** scope and add `?token=…` to the overlay URL. It's a separate scope from Cam Wall on purpose: the overlay names the file on screen, which a Cam Wall token is trusted never to reveal, so folding them together would have quietly widened every wall token already handed out.

- **A2L printers: the AMS Lite's slots now load, deduct and map correctly.** The A2L reports its 4-slot AMS Lite under an internal unit number that doesn't line up with the rest of its own telemetry, so BamDude read loaded slots as empty, credited filament usage to the wrong spool (or to none at all), and refused to link those slots to Spoolman. The unit is now normalised on the way in, so the AMS card shows the real slot contents, usage is deducted from the spool actually printing, Spoolman links work, and prints dispatch with the slot mapping the printer expects. The unit is labelled **AMS Lite**. Only the A2L's Lite unit is affected — every other printer and AMS type is untouched. (An A2L with a *second* AMS attached alongside the Lite is not yet supported and logs a warning.)
- **An AMS-HT no longer loses its spool a few seconds after the printer powers on.** The single-tray high-temp dry box reports its *loaded* tray with the same status code a regular 4-slot AMS uses for "slot emptied", so the brief status update the printer sends at power-on was misread as a spool removal and wiped the HT slot's filament type, RFID data and spool assignment. HT units are now exempt from that heuristic; genuinely removing an HT spool still clears the slot, and regular AMS units behave exactly as before.
- **A non-RFID spool in a standard AMS now shows "?" instead of "Empty".** A spool with no readable tag is reported by the AMS with no filament type at all — structurally identical to a truly empty slot — so the slot rendered as empty while Bambu Studio correctly showed it as present-but-unidentified. BamDude now uses the same authoritative "a spool is physically here" signal Studio does, so a present-but-unconfigured slot reads **"?"** (click Configure to tell it what's loaded) and only a genuinely empty slot reads **Empty**. Printers that already reported these slots correctly are unaffected.
- **Configuring an AMS slot with a built-in filament now sticks instead of reverting.** Picking a built-in / generic filament in the **Configure AMS Slot** dialog sent the printer a filament id with no matching profile id, which the firmware treats as a half-finished configuration: it showed the new material for a moment and then snapped back to whatever the slot had before. The profile id is now filled in from the filament you picked, so the choice holds. Custom and cloud presets, which already carried their own profile id, are unchanged.
- **Non-0.4mm nozzles are now handled in AMS slot configuration and guarded at dispatch.** The **Configure AMS Slot** filament picker was hardwired to 0.4mm, so a 0.6 (or 0.2 / 0.8) machine could only assign 0.4 profiles to its trays; it now offers the profiles for the nozzle actually installed, resolved per AMS unit on dual-nozzle printers. Separately, sending a file sliced for one nozzle size to a printer with a different nozzle installed used to fail on the printer with a cryptic "Failed to get AMS mapping table" error after the whole upload. The queue now catches the mismatch before uploading and fails the item with an actionable message. It only ever blocks on a *positive* mismatch — a file with no recorded nozzle size, or a printer that hasn't reported its nozzles, dispatches exactly as before, and on dual-nozzle printers a match against either hotend passes.
- **The AMS slot popup no longer covers the dialog it just opened.** On a touch device, tapping **Configure** (or Assign / Unassign Spool, or an "Open in Inventory" link) on an AMS slot left the slot popup standing on top of the dialog it had opened, with both layers on screen at once — the popup is only dismissed by the pointer leaving it, and a tablet never sends that after the tap. The popup now closes itself before opening a dialog or navigating away. Actions that report progress inside the popup — RFID re-read, Load, Unload, Copy UUID — are unchanged.
- **Queue dispatch reliability on mixed and multi-user farms.** A cluster of dispatch-time fixes: a start-print sent to a printer that had *just* gone busy no longer risks the firmware cancelling the running job — the item is deferred and retried once the printer is genuinely idle, instead of being marked failed. A print color-matched from a Virtual Printer to a real printer now actually uses the AMS slot it resolved, instead of shipping the VP's stale "no AMS" flag and aborting at layer 0 against the empty external spool. An unresolved AMS mapping (a rare frontend status-load race that saved `[-1]`) is now recomputed from the printer's live trays at dispatch rather than being silently treated as an external spool and printing against an empty feed. And stopping a print while its printer is offline now closes out the linked archive (cancelled) instead of leaving it stuck showing "printing" forever.
- **More queue reliability on mixed and multi-plate farms.** A G-code file sliced for one model can no longer be queued to — or moved onto — a printer it can't run on: queue create and edit reject the mismatch with a clear message (the X1/X1C/X1E/P1P/P1S series interchange within their family, everything else is exact-match, and files without slice metadata are never blocked). Multi-plate prints queued with **Force color match** no longer make every plate wait for every colour in the batch — each plate now only demands the colours its own slots print. A printer that accepts a file but never actually starts is retried a few times and then failed with an actionable message ("check the printer's screen… confirm its SD card is readable"), instead of re-uploading and re-waiting forever and hogging an upload slot the rest of the farm needs. The scheduler also re-checks the queue within a few seconds after a dispatch instead of always waiting the full interval, so a batch fans out promptly. And the queue list stops re-parsing each 3MF three times on every poll — per-plate metadata is cached by file revision, so a busy queue with several dashboards open is far lighter.
- **Large PostgreSQL printer farms no longer exhaust the database connection pool.** On a farm with dozens of printers, two or three people opening the dashboard at once could saturate the pool — after which unrelated requests hung for up to 30 seconds or failed, and even an unauthenticated probe took ~25 seconds to answer. Several things fed it: the pool was fixed at a small size, every authenticated request checked out two connections instead of one, and many operations that talk to a printer over FTP / camera / SMTP kept their connection open, sitting idle, for the whole slow network call. Now the pool is sized for farms by default and tunable via `DB_POOL_SIZE` / `DB_MAX_OVERFLOW` / `DB_POOL_TIMEOUT` / `DB_POOL_RECYCLE` / `DB_POOL_USE_LIFO` (with pre-ping + recycle so a server-dropped connection self-heals instead of erroring a request); each request makes a single checkout; and the file manager, printer storage, camera stream/snapshot, timelapse scan/attach, cloud filament lookups, the preheat heat-soak wait, progress/error notifications, the finish-photo capture, and the print-start 3MF download all release their connection before the slow work. Four bare FTP helpers gained an overall timeout so a saturated printer can't pin a caller indefinitely, and email sends now run off the event loop with a timeout. A new `GET /api/v1/system/db-pool` reports live pool gauges for diagnostics. SQLite installs are unaffected (their pool was already generous).

### Security

- **Closed a permissions gap where an account limited to its *own* archives could still modify — or slice and print — another user's files.** In a multi-user setup, an account scoped to "own" archive/library access (rather than "all") could still act on other users' items by their internal id: favouriting, editing project-page metadata, deleting a timelapse or photo, replacing the source 3MF or Fusion (.f3d) file on someone else's archive, and slicing or running a print pipeline from another user's model or archive (with the sliced result then attributed to and downloadable by the requester). Slice-job status polling had no owner scoping at all. Every one of these paths now enforces per-item ownership — a restricted account gets the same "not found" it already got when *viewing* another user's item, while accounts with full ("all") access and administrators are unaffected. Single-admin farms, and setups where Operators keep BamDude's default full read access, were never exposed to the cross-account case.
- **Printer FTPS and MQTT connections now declare TLS 1.2 as their explicit minimum.** The floor previously came from the host's OpenSSL build rather than from BamDude, so Docker installs were already floored at TLS 1.2 but a bare-metal or appliance install could in principle negotiate the obsolete TLS 1.0 / 1.1 with a printer that offered them. Every Bambu printer speaks TLS 1.2, so this changes nothing on the wire — it just states the floor instead of inheriting it. Certificate handling is unchanged (printers use self-signed certificates).
- **Updated bundled dependencies to patched releases** — the DOMPurify HTML sanitiser and brace-expansion clear known advisories, and the SQLAlchemy / pip install floors were raised so a fresh install can't resolve to a version the app fails to start on. No behaviour change.

## [0.4.7b4] - 2026-07-21

Beta pre-release continuing toward the 0.4.7 stable. Image: `ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.7b4` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.7b4` (beta channel — `:latest` still tracks 0.4.6). A follow-up polish + fix pass on the b3 **Archived Printers** and inventory work: archived printers now read as **"Printer N (Archived)"** consistently across Statistics and the Archives page (and sink to the bottom of those lists), with printer lists sorted by name; **quick-add spool creation** numbers lots from the value you enter and keeps sequential-lot bundles as distinct cards; and the spool dialog's **PA Profile** tab no longer counts — or fails to save — K-profiles bound to archived printers.

### Changed

- **Archived printers now read as "Printer N (Archived)" everywhere their history appears, and printer lists sort by name.** A retired printer used to show up inconsistently — Statistics fell back to a bare "Printer N" (it excluded archived printers) while the Archives page still showed its full name. Now an archived printer is labelled the same way — `Printer N (Archived)` — across Statistics, the Archives list/grid/calendar, and the Archives printer filter, so a decommissioned printer reads clearly no matter where its past prints surface. Alongside it, printer ordering is now alphabetical by name: the Queue view, the Statistics per-printer breakdowns and bar chart, the Archives printer filter, and the Maintenance page's printer list, selection chips, and history filter are all sorted by name — with archived printers sunk to the bottom of the Statistics lists and Archives filter.

### Fixed

- **Quick-add spool creation now numbers lots from the value you type, and sequential-lot copies stay as separate cards.** In "Quick Add (Stock)" mode the **Lot** field is shown again (it was hidden behind an auto-increment checkbox), and with "Auto-number lots" ticked the copies are numbered **from your starting value** — enter lot 5 with quantity 3 and you get lots 5, 6, 7 (an empty lot still starts at 1). The inventory grouping key now includes the lot too, so a bundle created this way shows as distinct cards instead of collapsing into a single aggregate row.
- **The spool dialog's PA Profile tab no longer counts K-profiles from archived printers, and saving such a spool no longer fails.** The tab badge counted every K-profile still stored on the spool — including ones bound to printers you've since archived, which the profile list (correctly) already hides — so it could read higher than your active printer count. It now counts only profiles on non-archived printers, matching the list. Separately, saving a spool that still carried an archived printer's K-profile used to abort the profile save with a warning (it couldn't resolve the retired printer); it now skips those stale entries and saves the rest.

## [0.4.7b3] - 2026-07-12

Beta pre-release continuing toward the 0.4.7 stable. Image: `ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.7b3` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.7b3` (beta channel — `:latest` still tracks 0.4.6). The headline is **Archive a printer** — soft-retire a sold or decommissioned printer so it disappears from the whole app (Printers page, pickers, queues, dispatch, scheduler, MQTT) while its print history is kept, restorable from Settings → Printing. Alongside it: the Print dialog now **remembers your Preheat & heat-soak choice per printer model**, the **Heater History window shows the live "Current" reading** with a heating-state thermometer (now on non-H2 printers too), and the **multi-plate print preview** no longer 404s when a plate name contains spaces.

### Added

- **Archive a printer instead of deleting it.** A printer you've retired — sold, decommissioned, swapped out — can now be *archived* rather than deleted: it vanishes from the Printers page, every printer picker, queues, dispatch, the scheduler, and MQTT, **while its full print history is kept**. Archiving is blocked while a print is running and cancels the printer's pending queue items. Restore an archived printer (or delete it for good) under **Settings → Printing → Archived printers**. This is distinct from Maintenance Mode, which only parks a printer temporarily and keeps its card visible.
- **The Print dialog now remembers your Preheat & heat-soak choice per printer model.** The per-print Preheat override (Inherit / On / Off) and its optional chamber-target join the print-option toggles that were already saved per model — so they're persisted when you print, queue, or auto-queue a job and restored the next time you open the dialog for that model. Admins can also pre-set or edit them per user (and for the virtual-printer system fallback) under **Settings → Print → Saved Print Options Profiles**.

### Fixed

- **The print preview (cover) on the printer card and queue card now shows for multi-plate prints whose plate name contains spaces.** The printer firmware reports the running job's name with spaces collapsed to underscores (e.g. a plate named "Modular Panels rear" comes back as "Modular_Panels_rear"), but the archive keeps the original spaced filename — so the cover endpoint's exact filename match missed it and returned 404, and the card fell back to a placeholder box even though the archive and its thumbnail existed. The match now normalises spaces and underscores on both sides, the same way the 3MF download flow already does.
- **The Heater History window's "Current" reading is now live, not a stale history sample.** The big "Current" value (and its target) was pulled from the last *recorded* history point, which only refreshes about once a minute, so it lagged behind the real-time temperature on the printer card (e.g. the card showed 254 °C while the window showed 256 °C). It now reads the same live MQTT value as the card, and a thermometer icon in front of the number fills and glows when that heater is actively on (empty outline when idle) — the same indicator the printer card uses.
- **The bed heater indicator now works on non-H2 printers too.** The bed's live heating state was only derived for the H2 series, so on every other model the bed thermometer never reflected whether the bed was actively heating. It's now computed for all printers (heating = a target is set and the current temperature is still below it).

## [0.4.7b2] - 2026-07-08

Beta pre-release continuing toward the 0.4.7 stable. Image: `ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.7b2` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.7b2` (beta channel — `:latest` still tracks 0.4.6). This cycle ports the upstream Bambuddy **v0.2.4.8 → v0.2.4.9** changes — the headline additions are **Slicer Pipelines** (save a slice setup once, then slice-and-queue any library file or archive with model-class fanout and a live per-copy runs dashboard) and **Preheat & heat-soak** before queued prints (per-filament chamber targets from the loaded AMS + airduct control) — alongside new **API-key management scopes** and a broad fix pass: correct Spoolman accounting across an AMS-backup switch, smart-plug power kept on across print restarts, virtual-printer upload integrity (`--loop asyncio` + a ZIP-completeness gate), HMS "Ignore and Resume" that truly ignores, scannable QR on 203 dpi thermal labels, app-wide light-theme contrast, and more.

### Added

- **Preheat & heat-soak before queued prints — give engineering filaments the warm bed and chamber they need for adhesion and warp control.** When enabled, the scheduler heats the bed (and, on chamber-equipped printers, the chamber) and holds at temperature *before* each queued print starts — the heat-soak ABS/ASA/PA/PC need but that Bambu's own firmware won't wait for (it silently ignores `M191`), running while the printer is otherwise idle between upload and start. The chamber target is worked out per print from the loaded AMS filament types (a built-in map — PLA/PETG 0°C, ABS/ASA 45°C, PA/PC 50°C, PA-CF 55°C — that you can fully edit under **Settings → Printing → Preheat & Heat Soak**), taking the hottest across loaded slots so a mixed PA+PLA job soaks for the PA. Three hardware tiers are handled automatically: active chamber heater (H2C/H2D/H2D Pro/H2S/X2D/X1E) drives `M141` and waits on the chamber sensor; chamber-sensor-only machines (X1C/P2S) wait for radiant warm-up; sensorless machines (P1S/P1P/A1) hold on a timer. On models with a cooling/heating airduct flap the flap is flipped to match — heating mode (closing the exhaust) for engineering filaments, cooling for PLA — since the firmware won't switch it for you. Configurable max-wait and soak duration; per-print override (Inherit/On/Off + an optional explicit chamber target) in the Print dialog's options. Off by default — existing installs see no change. Ported from upstream Bambuddy #1468.
- **Slicer Pipelines — save a slice setup once, then slice-and-queue any file with a single click.** A *pipeline* bundles the four picks you normally make in the Slice dialog (printer preset, process preset, filament preset per AMS slot, and bed type) together with a dispatch target and a copy-fanout strategy, so a repeatable job stops being a re-pick-everything chore. Save one straight from the Slice dialog ("Save as pipeline"), then **Run with pipeline** from a library file, an archive, or the Slice dialog: BamDude slices the source once and enqueues however many copies you asked for onto the target. The target can be a **specific printer** or a whole **printer-model class** (e.g. "any X1C") — class targets are handed to the existing auto-queue distributor, so copies balance across your matching printers exactly like any other model-assigned job. A pre-flight check flags mismatches (target offline/disabled, AMS filament type/colour differs, missing slot) before you commit, with a "Run anyway" escape hatch. A new **Pipelines tab on the Print Queue page** tracks every run live — per-copy status, cancel an in-flight run, retry just the failed copies, and clear the log — and manage/rename/delete pipelines under **Settings → Pipelines**. Gated by three new permissions (`pipelines:read` / `:write` / `:run`); Operators get all three, Viewers get read. Ported from upstream Bambuddy #1425, adapted to BamDude's two-tier queue.
- **API keys can now be scoped to manage projects, archives, and maintenance.** Three new per-key permissions — `can_manage_projects` (create/curate projects + batch-add archives), `can_manage_archives` (create/edit/delete print archives, *excluding* the destructive purge), and `can_manage_maintenance` (log maintenance, reset counters, edit intervals + the maintenance-type catalog) — let home-automation integrations drive those surfaces via API key without handing over broader printer control or settings access. Existing keys keep all three **off** (no silent scope widening); grant them per key under **Settings → API Keys**. Ported from upstream Bambuddy #1893 / #1888 / #1832.
- **Spool labels now scan reliably on low-resolution and black-and-white thermal printers.** The QR code on the small 40 × 30 mm box label rendered too densely to print on 203 dpi thermal heads — the modules bled together and wouldn't scan. Two fixes, applied adaptively so every template benefits with no configuration: the QR now has a 12 mm minimum size (the 40 × 30 label goes ~7.5 → 12 mm, roughly doubling the dots per module at 203 dpi) while still capped by the label's height and width, and label QR codes now use `ERROR_CORRECT_L` instead of `M` (same payload, fewer and chunkier modules — a label doesn't need damage-level error recovery). A new **Monochrome (black & white printer)** checkbox in the label print dialog drops the colour swatch (a useless grey block on a B&W printer) and widens the text column, with the colour still carried by the hex-code line. Ported from upstream Bambuddy #1870.

### Fixed

- **Prints on printers with a Filament Track System (FTS) now get fed from the right spool.** The FTS accessory routes any AMS slot to either nozzle, so the per-nozzle slot filter — correct on fixed dual-nozzle machines — wrongly stranded prints on the "wrong nozzle" slot when an FTS was installed. Dispatch now skips that filter when an FTS is present (#2186).
- **Cancelling a queued print while it's being dispatched now actually cancels it.** A narrow race let the scheduler flip a just-cancelled item back to "printing" from a stale snapshot and dispatch it anyway; the status flip is now an atomic compare-and-set that bails if the item was cancelled or removed in the meantime (#1853).
- **Multi-material prints that genuinely use both nozzles now map each filament to the correct nozzle.** A single-active-nozzle shortcut could collapse a real multi-group (dual-nozzle) slice onto one nozzle when the slicer under-reported the second nozzle; the shortcut now only applies to true single-group slices and otherwise honours the slicer's per-filament nozzle groups (#1825).
- **Auto-drying no longer cuts a running dry short.** The AMS humidity reading is noisy while the dryer heats, so a mid-dry humidity re-check could stop a legitimate manual or preset dry ~30 min in. A running dry now always runs to its set duration; it's only ever interrupted when a print is dispatched to that printer (#1892).
- **The finish photo now captures the actual print, not the aftermath.** On printers that skip the internal "end" stage (e.g. A1 Mini), the finish photo was taken only after the print's End G-code had already run — parking the toolhead, swapping, or clearing the plate. It's now captured the moment the last layer starts, while the print is still on the bed (#1867).
- **The first-layer photo no longer fires ~30 minutes early.** Some models (e.g. P1S) tick the layer counter during pre-print calibration, which triggered a "first layer done" notification with a photo of an empty plate. The notification now waits until the printer is genuinely printing (#1837).
- **The Windows installer now starts on a fresh Windows 10 that never had the Visual C++ runtime.** The bundled embeddable Python omits `vcruntime140_1.dll`/`msvcp140.dll`, which greenlet needs; without them the database layer failed to start and the dashboard refused connections while the service showed "running". Those runtime DLLs now ship inside the installer.
- **The macOS native installer no longer needs `sudo`.** Homebrew refuses to run as root and a root-created virtualenv can't be managed by the per-user launchd agent, so the old root-based flow broke halfway through. macOS now installs rootless under your home directory and refuses to run under `sudo` with guidance; the Linux systemd install is unchanged.
- **Spoolman usage is now split correctly when a print switches to an AMS backup spool mid-run.** For a single-material print that ran a slot dry and continued on its AMS-backup tray, Spoolman charged the entire print to the origin spool (pushing it past its starting weight) and the remain-percentage pass then double-counted the backup. Usage is now attributed to each spool for the portion of the print it actually fed — matching how the built-in spool inventory already accounted for it (#1793).
- **A smart plug no longer cuts power when a print restarts, and queue auto-off honors each plug's own cooldown.** Restarting a print (e.g. from the printer's screen) while a delayed power-off was pending could kill power mid-print; the pending off is now always cancelled when a print starts, and every auto-off path re-checks that the printer isn't actively printing before switching off. Auto-off after a queue job also now follows each plug's configured delay / temperature-threshold instead of a fixed cooldown (#1890).
- **Virtual-printer uploads no longer arrive truncated.** Native (non-Docker) installs launched the server without `--loop asyncio`, so `uvicorn[standard]` auto-selected uvloop, whose TLS layer could silently drop the tail of a 3MF sent to the virtual printer — the job was acked as complete but the file was corrupt. All install paths now force the asyncio loop (matching the Docker image), and the FTP server additionally validates that an uploaded 3MF is a complete ZIP before acknowledging it, rejecting a truncated transfer with an actionable error instead of archiving a broken job (#1896).
- **The bind-interface list now populates on macOS.** The virtual-printer / discovery network-interface picker only worked on Windows and Linux; macOS (and BSD) returned an empty list. Non-Linux platforms now enumerate interfaces via `psutil` (#af867c03).
- **The virtual-printer MQTT broker recovers from stuck clients.** A slow or dead consumer whose write buffer stopped draining could wedge the broker; it now evicts a client on drain-timeout and tightens TCP keepalive so dead peers are detected promptly (#1872).
- **Camera passthrough now uses the right port for A1/P1 printers behind a virtual printer.** The VP camera proxy hardcoded the RTSP port (322); A1/P1 series use the chamber-image protocol on port 6000, so their streams didn't come through. The proxy now picks the port from the target printer's model (#1868).
- **External-camera snapshots in a non-JPEG format now display.** A snapshot fetched from a user's external camera URL in PNG/WebP was passed through unchanged into a JPEG-labelled stream and failed to render; such frames are now transcoded to JPEG (#1902).
- **Re-slicing a 3MF now keeps its soluble-support (PVA) setup.** When a source 3MF configured to print supports in a separate PVA/soluble material was re-sliced, the support-material choice was dropped and supports came out in the model material. The support settings (enable, support/interface filament) now carry through the re-slice, and support materials show on the archive card of an unsliced source 3MF instead of being hidden (#1881).
- **A rejected slice now shows the real reason, and auto-pick never fills in a filament preset that's wrong for the printer.** When the slicer refused a slice for a content reason — an object off the bed, or a filament preset bound to a different printer model — you got a generic "input preset file is invalid" placeholder, and for 3MF sources BamDude silently retried with the file's *original* printer, masking the problem. The slicer's actual diagnostic (e.g. `filament preset Generic PLA @BBL H2C (slot 1) is not compatible with printer Bambu Lab A1`) is now surfaced instead, and such a rejection no longer falls back to the wrong printer. Separately, the Slice dialog's automatic per-slot filament pick now hard-skips any preset incompatible with the chosen printer whenever a compatible one exists — previously a strong colour match could let a wrong-printer preset win and then poison every unused slot — only falling back to a mismatch when nothing compatible is available (#1851).
- **The wrong-plate "Ignore and Resume" HMS action now actually ignores the fault.** It previously issued a plain resume, so a wrong-plate (or similar) fault re-paused the printer 1–2 seconds later; it now sends the firmware's dedicated ignore command that suppresses the fault's next re-check while resuming, matching Bambu Studio (#1869).
- **HMS action buttons render as real buttons, show a per-button spinner, and no longer report a false failure on a transient re-pause.** The buttons relied on a dynamic hover class that Tailwind never generated (so they looked inert), and the acknowledgement check keyed off state fields that can round-trip PAUSE→PAUSE, occasionally surfacing a spurious error even though the printer accepted the action; acknowledgement is now detected from the firmware's actual response (#1869).
- **HMS faults that aren't in the catalog but carry firmware actions now surface.** An unrecognised fault code that the printer attached actionable options to was hidden by the display filter — exactly the fault a user most needs to act on; such faults now appear (with their action buttons and a generic description) instead of being dropped (#1840).
- **Fetching or deleting a single cloud slicer setting no longer fails.** The Bambu Cloud slicer-setting API requires a `version` query parameter that the singular GET and DELETE calls were omitting, so they returned HTTP 400; both now send it (#1815).
- **A transient network hiccup at startup no longer logs you out.** When the app validated your saved session on load and hit a temporary error (server briefly unreachable, a 5xx), it discarded the valid session and forced a re-login; it now retries a few times and only clears the session on a genuine authentication rejection (#1889).
- **The live-updates WebSocket no longer hammers the server when it can't authenticate.** If the connection token was refused because the session was unauthenticated (or a user lacks the `websocket:connect` permission), the client retried in a tight loop; it now stops on an auth rejection and only keeps retrying for transient network failures. The stream-overlay page's live connection, which silently fell back to polling under the auth gate, was fixed the same way.
- **Light theme is readable again for status text, badges, and borders.** Many components were styled dark-theme-first, so error/warning/info/success text (and their chips and borders) used shades that washed out on the light theme's white surfaces. Every such element now carries a proper light-theme colour alongside its dark one, across the whole app — dark theme is unchanged, and genuinely dark surfaces (log console, terminal views) were deliberately left as-is (#1909).

### Security

- **Updated the router dependency to clear reported advisories.** `react-router` was raised to 7.18.1, clearing a batch of advisories flagged against the 7.x line (open redirect via protocol-relative URLs, XSS, denial-of-service, and CSRF).
