Nearly all of them only affect React Router's server-side rendering and data APIs, which BamDude — a client-side single-page app served as a static bundle — does not use; the bump keeps the dependency audit clean and closes the one client-relevant item (open redirect) regardless.

## [0.4.7b1] - 2026-07-06

Beta pre-release for testing ahead of the 0.4.7 stable. Image: `ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.7b1` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.7b1` (beta channel — `:latest` still tracks 0.4.6). This cycle ports the upstream Bambuddy v0.2.4.7 → v0.2.4.8 changes on top of a large security-hardening pass — headline additions include the **Camera Wall**, **AMS Filament Backup** awareness, **heater temperature history**, actionable **HMS** remediation buttons, queue **batch grouping / drag-reorder / ETA sort**, **Bambu A2L** support, a **native Windows `.exe` installer**, library **user tags**, opt-in **continue drying while printing**, server-side plate thumbnails, and Spoolman tracking for no-3MF "Untitled" prints.

### Security

- **Printer access codes are no longer exposed to read-only users.** The printer list and detail API used to return each printer's `access_code` — the MQTT credential — to anyone with read access, including Viewers and read-scoped API keys. A read-only user could harvest those codes and talk to the printers' MQTT directly, sidestepping BamDude's permission checks. The code is now returned only to callers who can actually edit printers (`printers:update` — Administrators and Operators); everyone else gets the printer without it. Nothing else changes — names, models, serials, and camera settings are still visible to read-only dashboards, and the edit dialog (which never displayed the stored code, only accepted a new one) is unaffected.
- **Dependency security hardening.** Floor-pinned several runtime dependencies to their patched releases: DOMPurify 3.4.11 (GHSA-cmwh-pvxp-8882), cryptography 48.0.1, python-multipart 0.0.31 (three multipart-parser CVEs), Starlette 1.3.1 (two CVEs), and pydantic-settings 2.14.2; plus a handful of transitive build/runtime deps (form-data, js-yaml, markdown-it, ws) nudged to their fixed versions. The frontend build toolchain also moved to Vite 8, and the tracked production bundle was rebuilt on it.
- **Per-item read permissions for archives, the queue, and the library.** Read access now comes in two flavours — "own" (see only what you created) and "all" (see everyone's) — matching how edit/delete already worked. Previously a custom role granted a bare `archives:read` (or `queue:read` / `library:read`) could read, download, and queue-print *every* user's items, even ones it had no business seeing. Requests for another user's item now return "not found" rather than exposing it. **This does not change what your operators see:** the built-in Operators and Viewers groups keep farm-wide visibility (everyone sees all prints, queues, and files) — BamDude is a shared farm. The tighter "own only" default applies only to custom roles you build from the granular permissions; grant them the "read all" variant to restore farm-wide visibility. Operators also gain the Orca Cloud sign-in permission so the slice dialog's Orca Cloud preset picker works for them.
- **User and group management is now strictly admin-only.** Creating, editing, or deleting a user or a group — and adding or removing group members — now requires an actual admin account, not merely the matching `users:*` / `groups:*` permission. Previously an operator granted one of those permissions through a custom group could use it to promote themselves (or a new account) to admin. Admin here means a real admin: either the legacy admin role or membership in the Administrators group. As an extra safeguard, a system group's permission set (the Administrators group in particular) can no longer be emptied or rewritten, even by an admin, so the app can't be locked out through the group editor. Read endpoints are unchanged — operator dashboards that list users or groups keep working with just the read permission.
- **Starting and stopping a queued print now respect item ownership.** The queue's start and stop API endpoints now follow the same "own vs all" rule as cancel: a user with only own-scope queue permission can start or stop their *own* queued prints, while all-scope covers everyone's. Previously the start endpoint let any own-scope holder start *another* user's queued item through the API, and the stop endpoint required full all-scope so it rejected an operator stopping even their own item. Unattributed items (e.g. uploaded straight from a slicer to a virtual printer) stay claimable by the first person who starts them. Alongside this, two queue-integrity hardening fixes: two prints added to the same printer's queue at the exact same moment can no longer land on a duplicate position (on PostgreSQL), and a malformed drag-reorder request that would leave two items sharing a position is now rejected instead of producing an ambiguous order.
- **Updated a bundled network dependency to clear security advisories.** `aiohttp` (pulled in transitively by the web-push notification library) was floor-pinned to ≥ 3.14.0 to clear CVE-2026-34993 and CVE-2026-47265; BamDude's own use of it (external camera streaming) was verified unaffected by the update.
- **Closed additional path-traversal gaps and added a regression backstop.** A few filesystem paths were still built from names that can originate from an untrusted source — a filename reported by the printer over FTP/MQTT when recovering a print's 3MF, and the archive folder name derived from a user-supplied upload filename during slice-to-archive — without a containment check. All now route through the same resolve-and-verify guard used elsewhere, so a crafted name can no longer write outside the data directory. A new test walks the request and service code and fails the build if any future path join of a variable to a directory isn't either guarded or explicitly annotated, so this class of bug stays closed.
- **Hardened the virtual printer's slicer-facing ports.** The MQTT broker now rate-limits failed logins per source IP (five failures in 60 seconds blocks further attempts until the window clears), so the 8-character access code can't be brute-forced by anything that can reach the port over LAN/Tailscale/a tunnel. Access-code checks on both the MQTT and FTP sides now run in constant time (closes a timing side-channel), the access code is no longer written to debug logs, and FTP uploads are capped at 4 GiB and streamed straight to disk — a runaway or malicious upload can no longer exhaust the host's memory or disk.
- **API keys are now strictly confined to their granted scopes (upstream advisory GHSA-r2qv-8222-hqg3, CVSS 9.9 critical).** Previously any valid API key could reach almost every endpoint — starting and stopping prints, reordering the queue, reprinting archives, deleting other users' library files, and reading every resource — regardless of which permission checkboxes were ticked on it. Each key is now limited to exactly the scopes it was granted; anything outside them (settings, user/key administration, resource deletion, network scan) is refused. Two new scopes were added — **Manage Library** (upload/rename/delete your own library files + MakerWorld import) and **Manage Inventory** (spool/catalog/forecast writes) — so file and inventory access can be granted without handing over queue control. On upgrade, existing keys inherit these two new scopes from their current "Manage Queue" setting, so a queue-enabled key keeps working while a read-only key gains nothing.
- **The real-time event stream now requires authentication.** The WebSocket that pushes live printer status, print progress, archive, and inventory events is served outside the HTTP auth layer, so anyone who could reach the web port could subscribe to every event in the system. It now requires a short-lived token minted for the signed-in user (or an API key with read access); unauthenticated connections are refused before any event is sent. The browser handles this transparently.
- **Closed a set of path-traversal file-access holes (upstream fix for a private report).** Importing a crafted project archive could write files anywhere the server could write (via absolute or `..` paths inside the archive); the archive-photo endpoint — which is unauthenticated so `<img>` tags can load it — could be tricked into serving any file on disk; and a malicious/compromised printer's timelapse filename could land bytes outside the archive folder. All three now route through a containment check that rejects any path escaping its intended directory.
- **Updated a bundled authentication dependency to clear security advisories.** PyJWT raised to ≥ 2.13.0 (fixes four advisories flagged against the previous line); BamDude's token handling was verified unaffected by the update's behavioural changes.
- **Audited upstream advisory GHSA-6mf4-q26m-47pv (fail-open authentication bypass, CVSS 9.8 critical) — BamDude is not affected.** In upstream Bambuddy, optional authentication could be tricked into treating a database error as "auth is disabled" and waving unauthenticated requests through every protected endpoint (the proof-of-concept exhausts the process's file descriptors to force the error). BamDude's authentication is **always-on** and carries no such "is auth enabled?" probe that can fail open — when the system can't verify state it denies the request (the auth gate answers `401`, the setup gate `503`), never grants it. Added regression tests that pin this fail-closed contract so the anti-pattern can't slip back in.
- **Pinned FastAPI below 0.136.0 to keep a flagged dependency out of the build.** FastAPI 0.136.x declares an undocumented optional `fastar` package in its `[standard]` extra, flagged as a supply-chain risk (MAL-2026-4750). BamDude never installs that extra, so it was never exposed, but holding to the 0.135.x line removes the flagged version from the dependency tree entirely (and lets the CI audit suppression for it be dropped).

### Added

- **Spoolman now tracks filament for "Untitled" prints that have no 3MF.** When you print an unsaved Bambu Studio project, the printer keeps no `.gcode.3mf` on its storage (the job reports as "Untitled"), so BamDude falls back to a metadata-only archive — and until now Spoolman saw no weight change for that print, silently under-counting the spool. BamDude now snapshots each AMS slot's remaining % at print start and, at completion, writes the consumed grams to the matching Spoolman spool from the drop in remaining % multiplied by the spool's curated reference weight — the same AMS remain-delta approach the built-in inventory already uses (and, like it, ignoring the printer's unreliable per-slot weight readout). It only applies to slots the 3MF didn't already account for, so a normal print is unchanged and no slot is double-counted; it skips a slot whose spool was swapped mid-print, and needs a Spoolman spool assigned to that slot.
- **A dedicated "AI Failure Detection" notification event.** Obico's AI failure alerts (spaghetti / print-failure detection) used to pigg-back on the generic "Printer Error" event, so you couldn't subscribe to AI alerts without also getting every HMS/AMS hardware error, and vice-versa. AI failure detection is now its own event with its own message template: email/ntfy/Discord/webhook/etc. providers get a separate toggle for it, and Telegram chats get it as a separate item in the per-chat event picker. It's opt-in (off by default). The alert carries the printer, job name, confidence score, and the action taken (notify / pause / pause + power off).
- **Finish photos are now embedded directly in completion and failure emails.** When a notification email's template includes the `{finish_photo_url}` variable and a finish photo was captured, the photo is now inlined into the email body (as a proper multipart/related image) instead of only appearing as a link — so it shows inline in your mail client. Emails without a photo, or templates without the variable, are unchanged.
- **Admin-configurable maximum session lifetime.** Settings → Users now has a **Session Policy** card that caps how long a login stays valid before you have to sign in again (presets 24 hours / 7 days / 30 days, or a custom value up to 30 days). It defaults to 30 days, matching the existing "remember me" behaviour, so nothing changes until you lower it — handy for shared or public farm terminals where you want sessions to expire sooner. The ceiling applies to new logins and takes effect on existing sessions at their next automatic token refresh.
- **SSO autologin and an option to disable local username/password login.** Each OIDC provider gains an **Autologin** toggle (Settings → Users → OIDC): when set, the login page redirects unauthenticated visitors straight to that provider on load, so a browser hitting BamDude lands in your identity provider's sign-in instead of the local form. Only one provider can carry the flag, and it only fires while that provider is enabled. A companion **Disable local username/password login** switch (same tab) turns off the local credentials form entirely, leaving only SSO — useful when every operator signs in through your IdP. It's guarded against lockout: you can't disable local login unless at least one OIDC provider is enabled, and you can't disable it if your own account has no OIDC link (so you can't strand yourself). LDAP is unaffected (it keeps its own toggle). If SSO ever breaks, set `BAMDUDE_LOCAL_LOGIN=true` on the server to force the local form back on (or visit `/login?fallback=local` to skip the autologin redirect for one visit) — the documented recovery path. A disabled local login always returns the same generic "incorrect username or password" error, so it never advertises that the form is off.
- **Bambu Lab A2L support.** The A2L (a linear-rail 3D printer with an integrated cutter/plotter head, internal code `N9`, serials beginning `26A19`) is now a recognised model: pick it from the Add/Edit Printer dropdown (new "A2 Series" group), and it gets the correct capabilities everywhere — single FDM extruder (not dual-nozzle, so AMS routing stays on the main slot), no Ethernet (Wi-Fi only), the low-rate chamber camera (no RTSP), firmware update tracking, and a printer-card image. A virtual A2L is also available in the Add Virtual Printer dropdown for Bambu Studio testing. The firmware release-history page isn't published by Bambu yet, so update checks show no versions until it goes live.
- **Unknown printer models now self-identify in the logs.** The first status message from any printer logs the model-identity fields the firmware reports (`dev_model_name`, `dev_product_name`, etc.) once, at INFO level, so a support bundle from a brand-new or unrecognised model discloses its internal code without a debug build. If Bambu renames those fields, the log falls back to dumping the available field names so the model is still traceable.
- **"Send All plates" from your slicer now queues every plate.** When you send a multi-plate 3MF to a virtual printer with Bambu Studio's "Send all plates", BamDude now adds one queue item per plate (in plate order) instead of a single item for the whole file — so each plate prints as its own job and the queue shows them individually. Single-plate sends are unchanged.
- **Per-virtual-printer auto-print G-code injection toggle.** A virtual printer in Queue or Auto-queue mode gains a **G-code injection** switch (on the VP card) that opts its jobs into the per-model start/end G-code snippets (Settings → Printers → G-code Snippets) — for auto-eject / plate-clear rigs (Farmloop, SwapMod, AutoClear, Printflow 3D). It's off by default, so existing snippet users don't suddenly start injecting into Studio-Send jobs after upgrading, and it's a no-op unless snippets exist for the target model. Flipping it takes effect immediately (the running VP restarts to pick it up).
- **Support bundles now include each printer's raw status snapshot.** The downloadable support bundle adds a redacted `push-status/printer-N.json` per connected printer (model, firmware, and the raw MQTT status the printer reports). This is the ground-truth data needed to diagnose per-model AMS and filament-slot quirks. Private details are stripped before it's written — filenames, cloud/task IDs, and the printer's LAN IP addresses are removed, and the whole file is run through the same name/serial/access-code sanitizer as the log. The snapshot is only included when debug logging is enabled, and the bundle downloads to your machine before you choose to share it.
- **Orca Cloud profile sync — slice and configure AMS slots with your Orca Cloud presets.** Profiles now has an **Orca Cloud** tab beside Bambu Cloud: sign in to your Orca Cloud account (Google / Apple / GitHub or email + password) and browse your synced printer, process, and filament profiles with the same search + filters as the Bambu Cloud view. Once connected, Orca Cloud presets appear as a fourth tier in the slice dialog and the AMS-slot filament picker, ranked above local, Bambu Cloud, and standard presets (they carry full metadata inline, so filament type and colour pre-fill for free). The slice dialog shows a separate status banner per cloud so you can tell at a glance which one needs attention. Sign-in uses a paste-based flow: a tab opens to Orca's sign-in page, and you paste the resulting `localhost` callback URL back into BamDude — a workaround for Orca's Supabase project only allowing localhost redirects (upstream feature request OrcaSlicer/OrcaSlicer#14028). Requires the new **orca_cloud:auth** permission.
- **Discover printers on a different subnet (behind a router or on another VLAN).** Automatic discovery uses SSDP multicast, which doesn't cross routers — so a printer on a different network segment than the server was invisible. The Add Printer dialog now always shows a subnet picker with a **Custom subnet…** option that reveals a CIDR field (e.g. `10.1.1.0/24`); picking it runs an active unicast scan of that subnet instead of SSDP. The last custom subnet you entered is remembered. The FTP (990) and MQTT (8883) ports must be reachable across the routing boundary.
- **The connection diagnostic now catches two more common setup mistakes.** Running a printer's connection diagnostic adds two checks: **Store sent files on external storage (install step 4)** — reads the printer's own report of the setting and fails if it's off, since without it every archived print is missing its thumbnail and slicer metadata; and **Printer is publishing status** — the MQTT broker can accept your connection even when the serial number is wrong or mis-cased (the report topic is case-sensitive), which shows up as empty AMS / no filaments / no K-profiles in the slicer. The diagnostic waits up to 10 seconds for the printer's first status report and shows a live elapsed-seconds counter so the wait doesn't look hung.
- **A banner on the Archives page when prints are archived without thumbnails.** If a recent print was archived through the no-3MF fallback (the printer's SD card didn't have the `.gcode.3mf`, usually because "Store sent files on external storage" is off in the slicer), the Archives page now shows a one-time, dismissible banner explaining how to fix it. This catches the slicer-only variant of the setting that the printer never reports, so the connection diagnostic can't see it.
- **CSV import / export for the inventory page.** Two new buttons on the inventory header let you export the whole local inventory to a CSV file and bulk-add spools back from one. Export downloads a date-stamped `bamdude_inventory_YYYYMMDD.csv` (one row per active spool); import first shows a preview table that marks every row valid / error / skipped — and flags where a colour was filled in from the catalogue or a matching spool already exists — before anything is written, then a confirm click saves only the valid rows. Headers are case- and spacing-tolerant, only `material` is required, and the same validation as the manual add-spool form applies. Local inventory only — in Spoolman mode the buttons are disabled with a hint pointing at Spoolman's own CSV tools.
- **Separate "Open in Slicer" preference.** The slicer used for in-app slicing (the API/sidecar slicer) and the one the desktop **Open in Slicer** button hands files to are now independent settings. A new **Settings → Slicer → Open in Slicer** dropdown lets you, for example, slice through the Bambu Studio sidecar but open files locally in OrcaSlicer (or vice versa). It defaults to **Same as API slicer**, so existing setups are unchanged until you pick a different one.
- **Native Windows installer (`.exe`).** BamDude now ships a self-contained Windows installer built with Inno Setup: download `bamdude-windows-setup.exe` from the Releases page and run it — no Python or Node install required on the target machine. It bundles an embedded Python distribution, the pre-built frontend, and a static ffmpeg; installs to `C:\Program Files\BamDude`; keeps your data and logs outside the program folder (`C:\ProgramData\BamDude\{data,logs}`, preserved on uninstall by default); and registers BamDude as an NSSM-supervised Windows Service that starts on boot and serves `http://localhost:8000`. A Start Menu shortcut opens the web UI. (Replaces the earlier PowerShell install script, which couldn't survive environment drift across Windows hosts.)
- **New "System" theme option that follows your operating system.** Alongside Dark and Light, the theme can now be set to **System** — the app matches your OS's light/dark preference and switches automatically when the OS does. The sidebar theme button cycles Dark → Light → System, and **Settings → Appearance** has a matching three-button selector. Your per-mode style, background, and accent choices are preserved and applied to whichever mode the system resolves to.
- **Experimental H2D Pro support in the Add Virtual Printer dropdown.** The H2D Pro now advertises its own model code (distinct from the H2D) so Bambu Studio recognises it. The codes are transcribed from the model reference and not yet confirmed against a live H2D Pro — if your slicer doesn't recognise the virtual printer, let us know.
- **Refresh button on the slice dialog's preset list.** The slice dialog caches the cloud and bundled preset listings for a few minutes; if you delete or rename a preset in Bambu Studio / Bambu Handy it could take a while to disappear from the dropdown. A new **Refresh** control fetches the latest listings immediately. Importing or deleting a local profile in Settings also refreshes the slice dialog's presets right away.
- **Transparent / clear filament is now a first-class colour.** The spool colour editor has a new **Clear** quick-swatch (and the hex field accepts an 8-digit `RRGGBBAA` value) so you can mark a spool as transparent. Clear spools render as a checkerboard swatch throughout the app instead of an invisible or solid-black chip.
- **Look up a spool by its RFID tag over the API.** A new `GET /api/v1/inventory/spools/by-tag` endpoint returns a single spool matched on its `tray_uuid` and/or `tag_uid`, so an external NFC/inventory integration can dedupe a scan without pulling the whole inventory. Matching is format-tolerant (hex-normalised, case-insensitive), archived spools are excluded unless `include_archived=true`, and the endpoint is readable with a Manage-Inventory API key.
- **Managed storage-locations catalog for the inventory.** Instead of typing a free-text storage location on every spool, you now pick from a managed list. A new **Locations** button on the inventory header opens a catalog where you create, rename, and delete storage spots (shelves, drawers, dryboxes); the spool form's storage field becomes a dropdown (with an inline "create new" option), and the inventory filter and deep-links work off the catalog. Renaming a location updates every spool assigned to it. Your existing free-text locations are migrated into the catalog automatically on first launch, and in Spoolman mode the distinct locations from Spoolman are imported and kept in sync. (Editing storage locations needs the "edit inventory" permission.)
- **Reorder forecasting now groups spools by colour.** The forecast treats each colour of a filament as its own line — a Bambu PLA Matte you stock in five colours now shows as five rows, each with its own runway and per-colour reorder settings, instead of lumping every colour of the same material/brand into one. New material and brand filters, a dedicated **Spools** count column, and a clearer multi-series usage chart round out the panel. Your existing per-SKU lead-time and safety-margin overrides carry over to the matching colour rows on first load.
- **Choose whether unknown RFID spools are auto-added to inventory.** A new **Settings → Filament Tracking → Auto-add unknown RFID spools** toggle (on by default, so nothing changes unless you opt out) controls what happens when a printer reads a filament tag BamDude has no spool for. With it off, instead of silently creating a spool, BamDude pops a confirmation card — with the colour and material pre-filled — so you can add it to inventory or ignore it. Useful if you pre-create inventory entries by hand and don't want duplicates. Slots with no RFID tag at all never prompt.
- **User-authored tags for library files.** Label library files with your own cross-cutting tags ("toys", "kid-safe", "PETG-only") that cut across the folder tree. A new **Tags** button manages the catalog; a tag filter rail narrows the list to files carrying *all* the selected tags; and the multi-select toolbar gains a **Tag** action that bulk-adds, removes, or replaces tags across the selected files. These are separate from the automatic format/provenance badges the app already computes on each file. Managing the catalog needs the manage-all-library permission; a Manage-Library API key can drive them too.
- **File Manager: sort folders by recent activity, search inside subfolders, and per-folder description panels.** Three File Manager improvements: (1) the folder sidebar gains a **sort control** — keep the alphabetical order or switch to **By recent activity** so a folder that just got a new file bubbles to the top, with an asc/desc toggle; both the choice and direction are remembered per browser and apply at every nesting level. Activity tracks when a file was last added or changed inside a folder, not just when the folder was renamed. (2) When you select a folder and type in the search box, results now include matches from **every subfolder underneath it** (an "Including subfolders" hint appears) instead of only the folder's direct contents. (3) If a folder contains a Markdown file (`README.md` / `description.md`, or the first `.md` otherwise), its rendered contents show in a collapsible **description panel** above the file list — a read-only preview so you can document a folder in place; folders without a `.md` show nothing. As part of this, a folder's file count in the Project- and Archive-detail views now excludes files sitting in the trash, matching the main file listing.
- **Build plate type is now shown on queue items and in the print dialog.** On a farm with many plates it wasn't obvious which physical plate a queued job needs — the archive card showed the plate type but the queue and the scheduling dialog didn't. Each pending queue item now carries a build-plate icon (hover for the name), and the print dialog shows the selected plate's type next to the plate picker. Multi-plate 3MFs are handled per-plate: the value re-reads the file for the plate you actually picked instead of assuming every plate uses the first plate's bed, so a file mixing (say) Textured PEI and Engineering plates labels each one correctly.
- **Sort the Printers page by ETA.** The sort dropdown gains an **ETA** option so the printer finishing next sits on top — handy for staging the next job's filament ahead of time. Order is: printers currently printing with a known remaining time (soonest first), then printers that just started before an ETA is known, then idle, then offline. Uses the same remaining-time the per-card ETA already shows, so there's no extra load.
- **Group, collapse, and drag-reorder queued prints.** A printer's pending queue gains three ways to tame a long list: **select two or more items and "Group as batch"** to bundle them under one collapsible group (with an **Ungroup** action to disband it again); **collapse a batch** to a single row so it stops eating the whole card (the collapsed/expanded state is remembered per batch); and **drag a row by its grip handle** to reorder it. Grouping and collapse work in every view; drag is offered when the queue is expanded with no collapsed batches in the way, and the existing up/down/top/bottom buttons remain available everywhere. Grouping needs the "manage the whole queue" permission.
- **Per-printer Maintenance Mode.** Put a printer "out of service" without deleting it — from a printer's ⋮ menu (or the Edit dialog) toggle Maintenance Mode, and it drops out of queue dispatch, the scheduler, auto-drying and metrics, and disconnects from MQTT until you turn it back on. The card shows an amber "Maintenance" pill and panel with an Exit button so it stays visible but plainly parked. Toggling it on mid-print asks for confirmation first (since it stops progress tracking for the running job). Requires the "edit printers" permission.
- **Per-filament humidity thresholds for auto-drying and alarms.** Settings → Filaments now has a table where you set the humidity level (%) that triggers auto-drying and the hourly humidity alarm *per filament type* (PLA, PETG, TPU, ABS, ASA, PA, PC, PVA) plus a default for everything else. Multi-material AMS units resolve to the strictest (lowest) threshold across their loaded spools — so a Nylon spool that wants dry air isn't ignored just because it shares the unit with PLA. Leave a row blank to fall back to the global "fair" threshold; both the scheduler and the alarm notifier use the same resolution so they stay in lockstep.
- **The AMS drying badge now shows the filament and target temperature.** Instead of a bare "Drying · 11h 35m left", the badge reads "Drying · PETG @ 65°C · 11h 35m left" — the actual cycle you started. BamDude caches the filament + temperature it sent (Bambu only echoes the countdown on the wire) and falls back to the loaded tray's type + recommended drying temp when no record exists (e.g. drying started before a restart).
- **Continue drying while printing (opt-in).** A new **Settings → Print Queue → Continue drying while printing** toggle (off by default) keeps AMS auto-drying running through a print instead of stopping it the moment a job dispatches. It only engages on hardware Bambu confirms supports "Print While Drying" — H2D (firmware 01.03.00.00+), H2C / H2S / P2S / H2D Pro (01.02.00.00+), X2D / A2L (01.01.00.00+), and X1C (01.11.02.00+); other models (A1, A1 Mini, P1P/P1S, X1, X1E) are left out and unchanged. While a print runs, the drying temperature is automatically capped 5 °C below the idle preset (never below 40 °C) to protect spools inside the hot enclosure. With the toggle off, drying still yields to prints exactly as before.
- **Heater temperature history (nozzle / bed / chamber).** The Printers page now records each printer's heater readings once a minute and charts them over time — click the chart icon in a printer's temperature row to open a Heater History window with nozzle, second-nozzle (dual-nozzle H2D), bed and chamber traces, target overlays, current/avg/min/max stats and 6h/24h/48h/7d ranges. It mirrors the existing AMS humidity/temperature history, so long prints and pre-heat behaviour are now reviewable after the fact. Data is retained 30 days by default (configurable) and auto-pruned. Visible to the Operators and Viewers groups; also readable via read-scoped API keys.
- **AMS Filament Backup status on the printer card + smarter "prefer lowest".** Each printer card's Filaments section now shows an AMS Filament Backup badge (on / off / unknown) at a glance; clicking it opens AMS settings to change it. More importantly, the "prefer lowest remaining filament" dispatch option now respects the backup state: when AMS Filament Backup is **off**, the printer won't auto-switch between same-material spools mid-print, so preferring the lowest spool would risk stranding the job when it runs out — BamDude now skips prefer-lowest for that printer and matches normally. With backup on, prefer-lowest works as before. Applied consistently across both dispatch paths (scheduler and auto-queue).
- **Camera Wall — one live grid of every printer's camera.** The Printers page has a new view toggle (**Cards / Cam wall**) that swaps the printer cards for a responsive grid of camera tiles, so you can watch the whole farm at a glance. To stay light on a Raspberry Pi, only on-screen tiles stream live and only up to a cap you set (default 4); the rest fall back to periodic snapshots (default every 8s), and off-screen tiles pause entirely — all reusing the existing shared camera fan-out, so extra viewers don't spin up extra transcoders. Each tile shows an offline chip when the printer is disconnected, an optional status overlay (off / state chip only / full with progress, layer, and time-left), and an HMS-error badge; clicking a tile opens that camera in your preferred window or embedded viewer. Max-live count, snapshot interval, and overlay level are remembered per browser. Also fixes a bug where closing one camera viewer could freeze another viewer's stream of the same printer — a viewer now only tears the stream down when it's the last one watching. Needs the "view cameras" permission.
- **Actionable buttons on the HMS error dialog.** When a printer reports an HMS error, the error dialog now shows the same remediation buttons the printer's own screen offers — Resume, Stop, Ignore & Resume, "Filament extruded, continue", Stop Drying, Turn off the Fire Alarm, and so on — mapped from Bambu's own catalog per printer model. Clicking one sends the matching command straight to the printer over MQTT (the command shapes are the plain ones BambuStudio uses, verified on a live H2D so the firmware doesn't silently reject them), then the dialog confirms the printer actually acted before closing. Which buttons appear depends on the specific error code; unknown codes still show just the description and the wiki link as before. Needs the "control printers" permission. Refresh the bundled catalog with `python scripts/update_hms_actions.py`.

### Removed

- **Slicer preset bundle (`.bbscfg`) import has been removed.** The feature let you upload a Bambu Studio "Printer Preset Bundle" to the slicer sidecar, but Bambu Studio's `.bbscfg` export strips the system process and filament presets — so importing a bundle left you without process presets and slicing quietly fell back to the settings embedded in the model, and the bundle mode also hid the standard preset tier behind a constrained dropdown. It's gone end to end; the Settings panel now shows a short notice explaining the removal and the alternatives. Server-side slicing now always resolves presets in the order Imported → Orca Cloud → Bambu Cloud → built-in standard. (Importing individual OrcaSlicer/Bambu Studio preset files — `.json`, `.orca_filament`, `.bbscfg`, `.zip` — into your local profiles is a separate feature and is unaffected.)

### Changed

- **Bulk-edit spools list now shows each spool by its display-name template.** The spool picker in "Bulk edit spools" labelled each row with a fixed brand + material + colour join, ignoring the **Settings → spool display name** template that the inventory table and every other spool list already follow. It now synthesises the same name from your template, so the labels match the rest of the app.
- **The Assign Spool dialog's Spoolman list now uses your spool display-name template too.** In Spoolman mode, the spool picker labelled rows with a fixed brand + material + variant join while the local-inventory list right above it already used your template. Both lists now render names the same way.
- **File Manager: "All Files" now shows only your own uploaded files, with a new "External" sidebar entry for linked folders.** If you linked a NAS or other external folder that auto-imported hundreds of files, they used to drown your own uploads in "All Files" with no way to filter them out. "All Files" now lists managed-storage files only (its meaning before external folders existed), and a new **External** entry — shown only when at least one external folder is linked — holds the combined view across every linked folder. Clicking an individual folder in the tree is unchanged. To get the old combined-everything view, click **External** once.
- **Deleting an archive now handles its queued prints properly.** If you delete an archive that still has prints queued from it (a "Send All" archive can back several — one per plate), the delete confirmation now tells you how many queued prints will go with it, and those pending queue items are removed instead of being left behind as dead "cancelled" rows. If any of those prints is currently running, the delete is blocked with a clear message asking you to stop the print first — so a live print never loses the archive it needs for its filament/plate record.
- **The inventory "Reset usage" action is now called "Reset counter".** The old name suggested it would wipe the spool's used-grams to 0, but it only zeroes the displayed **Total Consumed** counter — the spool's remaining weight is untouched. The button, confirmation dialogs, and tooltips now say "Reset counter" and spell out that remaining weight isn't changed. (The underlying API endpoints were renamed to match, from `…/reset-usage` to `…/reset-consumed-counter`.)
- **The slice dialog's preset list is simpler and more predictable.** Presets are now grouped and ranked in one consistent order — your imported/local presets first, then Orca Cloud, then Bambu Cloud, then the built-in standard presets — and the list no longer hides a preset from one source just because another source has one with the same name (every tier shows its own). The "you're signed out" banner that used to sit at the top of the cloud sections is gone; sign-in state is already clear from the preset picker itself. AMS-slot preset lists gained matching source badges so you can tell at a glance which tier a profile came from.
- **The Queue timeline now shows only committed schedules.** Timeline bars are limited to what's actually going to run next — the active print, prints pinned to a scheduled time, and ASAP prints queued behind an active print. Staged (manual-start) jobs, blocked/waiting jobs, and ASAP jobs on an idle printer are no longer drawn, so the timeline reads as a real forecast instead of everything that's merely parked in the queue.

### Fixed

- **Archive and library cards now show a thumbnail for prints sliced through the Bambu Studio / OrcaSlicer docker sidecar.** Both slicer CLIs skip the plate preview when invoked headlessly with `--slice --export-3mf`, so files with no embeddable preview — STEP/STP uploads, preview-less 3MF sources, and the 2nd-and-later plates of a multi-plate slice — landed in the archive/library with a blank card. BamDude now renders the missing plate thumbnails server-side from the sliced 3MF's own model (an isometric Bambu-green view matching the rest of the library thumbnails) and injects them before the file is written. This complements the existing STL-source preview injection, which still supplies the higher-fidelity plate 1 for library-STL slices — the new pass only fills the blanks that path leaves. Best-effort: a render failure never fails the slice, it just leaves the card without a picture as before.
- **The Archives list no longer errors out when an older print row has no creation date.** Prints adopted from a pre-fork Bambuddy database, or copied across a SQLite ↔ PostgreSQL restore, could carry a missing `created_at`, which made the whole archives API return an error and left the page unable to load. Those rows are now backfilled on startup (to the print's completion time, then its start time, then the current time) and the list tolerates a missing date, so a single legacy row can't break the page again.
- **The Docker install script no longer fails with a permission error on `/opt/bamdude`.** Running the documented `curl … | bash` Docker installer as a normal user aborted immediately because it tried to create the root-owned default install directory without elevation. It now creates the directory with `sudo` only when needed (and hands ownership back to you so you can edit `docker-compose.yml` and `.env` afterwards) — custom install paths under your home directory still install without a password prompt.
- **In-app "Install Update" on Windows installer installs now offers the installer instead of failing.** On a machine set up with the native Windows `.exe` installer, clicking Install Update failed with "Could not find git executable" — the installer has no bundled git and no git repository to update from. BamDude now detects installer-based Windows installs and shows a "Download installer for vX.Y.Z" link instead: download it and re-run the installer to update (your data, settings, and printers are preserved). Docker, Home Assistant, Linux/macOS, and Windows git-clone dev installs are unchanged.
- **False camera-port warning on the A1, A1 Mini, and P1 series is fixed.** The connection diagnostic always probed the RTSPS camera port (322), but A1 / A1 Mini / P1P / P1S stream over the chamber-image protocol on port 6000 and never open 322 — so the camera check always warned even when the camera worked fine. The diagnostic now probes the correct port for each model and labels the check accordingly.
- **MakerWorld import now works for API-key callers whose owner has a Bambu Cloud login.** Importing or resolving a MakerWorld model through an API key (e.g. a browser extension or Home Assistant automation) returned "requires a Bambu Cloud login" even when the key's owning account had a valid stored Bambu Cloud session. The MakerWorld routes now resolve the key owner's cloud token, so those calls succeed and the imported library file is attributed to the key's owner. Keys without cloud access still get the same "requires a Bambu Cloud login" response as before.
- **Prints sent to a virtual printer now keep the print options you picked in Bambu Studio.** When you sent a file to a virtual printer, the workflow toggles from the slicer's Send dialog — bed levelling, flow (dynamics) calibration, timelapse, first-layer inspection, and AMS use — were silently dropped, so the queued job always fell back to BamDude's default settings instead of your choices. The virtual printer was matching the slicer's options against the wrong internal key, so every field was lost on every send. It now correlates them correctly, waits a little longer (up to 5 s) for the slicer's options to arrive on slower/wireless setups, and — if they land just after the job was queued — retroactively applies them to the still-pending queue item. Additionally, on the H2C's dual-nozzle rack, the slicer's per-filament nozzle pick is now carried through to the printer, so a hardened-nozzle or standard-nozzle assignment prints on the nozzle you chose instead of the firmware guessing.
- **Auto-print G-code injection now works on the P1S (and any firmware that stops at the executable-block marker).** End snippets were appended to the very end of the plate G-code, but Bambu firmware ignores anything after the `; EXECUTABLE_BLOCK_END` marker — so on a P1S the injected auto-eject / plate-clear moves silently never ran, leaving the finished part on the bed and blocking the queue. End snippets are now spliced in just before that marker (after the printer's own machine-end sequence), with the old end-of-file behaviour kept as a fallback for slicers that don't emit the marker. Two companion fixes make the injected file loadable on the P1S: its `.gcode.md5` checksum sidecar is recomputed to match the modified G-code (a stale hash was rejected with HMS 0500-4003 "unable to parse"), and every file's original ZIP compression is preserved on rewrite so the P1S's preview parser can still read the embedded thumbnails.
- **Reprint / print with quantity > 1 and G-code injection now injects every copy.** When you ticked G-code injection on a reprint or a library print with quantity > 1, the setting was silently dropped and no copy was injected. All copies now carry the injection flag and go through the scheduler, so every one gets the start/end snippets — the whole batch clears the plate as intended. Single-copy prints and jobs with injection off are unchanged.
- **Signing in now returns you to the page you were trying to reach.** If you opened a deep link (say a specific archive or the queue) while logged out, you were bounced to the login page and then always dropped on the dashboard afterwards — losing where you meant to go. The original destination is now preserved through both the regular login and the full OIDC/SSO round-trip, so you land where you started. Redirect targets are validated to be same-origin in-app paths, so the feature can't be abused for an open redirect.
- **The sidebar now shows Archives, Queue, and Files for granular read-only roles.** After read permissions were split into "own" and "all" variants, a custom role (or API key) granted only the granular tier — e.g. `archives:read_own` without the legacy `archives:read` — could open those pages by URL but didn't see their sidebar entries, because the nav check still looked only for the old flag. The nav now accepts any of the three read tiers, so a granular-only role gets its menu items. The built-in Operators/Viewers groups were unaffected (they keep the legacy flag); this fixes custom roles built on the new permissions.
- **User-print email templates are labelled clearly in the template list.** The four per-user print-email templates now read "User Print Started Email" / "Completed Email" / "Failed Email" / "Stopped Email" in Settings → Notifications → Message Templates, matching their descriptions, so they're no longer confusable with the broadcast print events.
- **Printer-offline notifications now actually fire.** The "printer went offline" notification event existed and could be configured, but nothing ever triggered it — a printer dropping off the network sent nothing. It now fires once when a printer transitions from connected to disconnected (from either a network/MQTT drop or a smart-plug power-off), after a 60-second debounce so a brief reconnect blip stays silent; reconnecting within the window cancels the pending alert.
- **A reprint started right after an MQTT reconnect no longer corrupts its own state.** When a printer reconnected mid-job and a reprint had begun, the reconnect-time reconciliation could mistake the still-running reprint for a stale one — falsely marking it complete, advancing its queue item to idle, and arming the "waiting for plate clear" gate while the print was still going. Adopted archive rows now track the current run's task id, so the reconciler sees a match instead of a ghost and leaves the running print alone.
- **The finish photo is no longer dropped when a print finishes via the fallback path.** On the FINISH-state completion path, the photo capture and the completion handler were dispatched back-to-back and raced: the handler could look for the photo before the ~15-second camera grab had stored it, then start its own capture that collided with the still-running grab (Bambu printers allow only one camera client), so the completion notification arrived without the photo despite one having been taken. The handler now waits for the capture to finish (or time out) before proceeding, so the photo lands reliably.
- **Completion notifications and archive cards now show only the printed plate's filament, not the whole project's.** For a multi-plate 3MF, the per-slot filament breakdown (e.g. "PLA: 12.3g | PETG: 5.0g") was summed across every plate in the file, even though the print — and its archive — is a single plate. The headline weight and time were already scoped to the printed plate; the per-slot list now matches them. Applies to prints archived from now on (existing archive records are unchanged).
- **The File Manager's drag-and-drop overlay no longer gets stuck.** If you started dragging files onto the File Manager and then cancelled — pressing Escape, dragging back out of the browser window, or releasing outside the page — the "drop to upload" overlay could stay on screen until you refreshed. It now clears in all three cases.
- **API keys with the Manage Library scope can now curate library files.** Such a key could upload and rename/delete its own files, but got a 403 ("administrative operation") when renaming, deleting, or moving any file — even though the scope is meant for full library management. Because API keys have no per-user identity, the all-ownership variants are the only ones that can ever work for a key, so they now ride the Manage-Library scope (matching how the Queue scope already behaves). Purging the library (which bypasses the recycle-bin window) still requires an admin.
- **Assign-spool picker now shows the spool note on touch devices.** The note attached to a spool was only visible as a hover tooltip, so it was invisible on phones and tablets. It now renders as an inline muted line under the spool weight in both the built-in and Spoolman pickers.
- **Assign to AMS keeps your custom Bambu Cloud filament preset instead of falling back to Generic.** When a spool carried a cloud user/shared preset (PFUS/PFCN), the AMS-change re-sync path could discard the slicer setting and reset the slot to "Generic <Material>". The custom preset id is now preserved, so the slicer loads your actual preset.
- **Deleting a local slicer profile removes it from the list immediately.** The deleted preset lingered until the list refetched in the background, and a quick second click could open a delete dialog that then 404'd. The row now disappears the instant the delete succeeds.
- **Chamber-fan badge no longer shows on printers that have no chamber fan.** Open-frame models (P1P, A1, A1 Mini, A2L) were still displaying a chamber-fan status pill stuck at 0%. The pill now appears only on models that physically have a chamber exhaust fan.
- **"Rotate spool during drying" is disabled when a tray in that AMS is loaded.** The firmware locks the whole AMS while any tray is threaded and rejects a rotate request outright — the option is now greyed out (with a tooltip) in that state and never sent, instead of silently failing.
- **Dual-nozzle external spools show L / R inside the colour circle.** On H2D / H2C the two external feeds now label the colour circle directly with L / R instead of a separate caption line below it, matching the AMS slot layout.
- **AMS Filament Backup mid-print spool switch no longer credits all filament to the second spool.** On the P1 series the firmware pushes `total_layer_num = 0` at end of print, which wiped the cached layer total BamDude uses to split filament usage across a mid-print AMS-Backup spool change — so the whole print's weight landed on the spool that finished the job. The layer total is now guarded against that zero push (and reset cleanly at print start), and the usage split falls back through the last known layer count, or an equal split, instead of dumping everything on the last spool.
- **AMS active-tray highlight no longer sticks on slot 1 during external-spool prints (H2S).** When a print used only external spools (every AMS slot mapped out), the printer still reported the physical slot as "in use", so the active-tray highlight stayed stuck on AMS slot 1. BamDude now recognises the all-external mapping and points the highlight at the external spool instead. Prints that use AMS, or mix AMS and external, are unaffected.
- **Editing a spool no longer shows the wrong slicer preset name.** After changing a spool's slicer preset via bulk edit, opening that spool in the single-spool editor could show the *old* preset's name in the preset field, even though the correct preset was actually selected ("Selected: …" showed the right one). The field now resolves the name from the spool's saved preset itself, so it always matches. Bulk edit also now stores the new preset's display name alongside the code, so the name can't drift out of date for anything else that reads it (e.g. label fallbacks).
- **Cloud and Orca Cloud presets no longer get rejected by the slicer engine.** Bambu Cloud ships presets with a `type` of "printer"/"print" and often an empty `from` field, which the Bambu Studio / Orca CLI's settings loader rejects (error −5) — so slicing with a cloud preset could fail outright. BamDude now pins those fields to the values the CLI accepts before handing the preset over, so cloud and Orca Cloud presets slice reliably.
- **A1 and A1 Mini were swapped in one internal model lookup.** A printer identified by its internal code (rather than its serial number) could be labelled A1 when it was an A1 Mini and vice-versa. Every other place that names these codes already had it right; this one table was the lone outlier. Most A1-family detection uses the serial prefix and was unaffected, but the mislabel is now fixed and locked with a regression test.
- **Finish photos now show the finished print, and no longer interfere with prints you didn't record.** Bambu firmware lowers the bed the instant a print reports complete, so the live-camera "finish photo" often captured the bed already dropped. BamDude now watches the printer's own progress and grabs the photo the moment it reaches end-of-print filament unloading — after the toolhead parks but before the bed drops — which frames the print correctly without changing anything about how the print runs. **Important fix:** the previous approach forced timelapse recording on for every print to have a video to pull the frame from; on filament profiles sliced with Timelapse Type = Smooth, that silently re-enabled the per-layer toolhead wipe/park moves you had opted out of by unchecking Timelapse in the slicer — so the toolhead parked off the part every layer on prints you never meant to record. Your Timelapse choice in the send dialog is now respected exactly. If you *did* enable timelapse, the finish photo is still pulled from the recording's last frame (best framing, and it's your video to keep). Live-camera capture remains the fallback. Note: prints started directly on the printer's touchscreen, Bambu Handy, or Bambu Studio's Send still use the live-camera photo.
- **Configure AMS Slot no longer lists filament profiles from other printer models.** When picking a profile for an AMS slot, presets compiled for a different printer model used to leak into the list — including ones whose model appears at the start of the name (e.g. "X1C eSUN PETG…") rather than as a Bambu Studio `@` suffix, which slipped past the old filter entirely. The list now recognises the model whether it's in the suffix, the long-form name, or the body of the preset name, and also honours an imported local preset's own compatible-printers list. Profiles that can't be classified stay visible (fail-open), and your currently-saved preset is always shown.
- **The "force colour match" checkbox now appears when scheduling against a specific printer.** Previously the per-filament force-colour-match toggle only showed up when queuing to the any-printer / auto-queue; picking a specific printer in the re-print or schedule dialog hid it. It's now available in specific-printer mode too, with the same per-slot layout as the auto-queue path.
- **Filament colour names in the specific-printer override panel now match the rest of the app.** The specific-printer scheduling panel showed the raw base material name where every other panel resolves the Bambu Studio sub-brand colour (e.g. "Charcoal"). It now uses the same colour-catalogue lookup, so the label reads consistently. Colour names still come only from the colour catalogue — no hardcoded tables.
- **AMS slot cards no longer show the previous spool's profile name after a swap.** When a new spool was assigned to an AMS slot — by RFID auto-assign, manual assignment, or Spoolman sync — the slot card could keep displaying the profile name of the spool that used to be there. The stored slot-to-profile mapping is now updated on every one of those paths, so the card always reflects the spool currently loaded.
- **Dual-nozzle printers can now assign AMS slots from either extruder.** In the re-print and schedule dialogs, the per-filament slot dropdown used to hide every AMS slot wired to a different extruder than the one the slicer picked — so on an H2D with AMS on both sides you couldn't route a filament to the "other" side even when you'd deliberately loaded it there. Every loaded slot is now offered; the L/R badge still shows the slicer's plan, and the printer validates the mapping when the print starts.
- **Turning off Flow Calibration or Nozzle Offset Calibration now actually skips it.** Unchecking these in the re-print / schedule dialog was accepted and sent to the printer, but the printer still ran both stages at the start of the print — the "off" value BamDude sent (`2`) meant "skip the explicit pass but still run the calibration stage", not "don't calibrate". BamDude now sends the value (`0`) that removes the stage entirely, matching what Bambu Studio sends when you uncheck them, verified on H2D firmware 01.x. (Vibration compensation is a separate firmware-side stage that still runs regardless — not fixable from here.)
- **Downloading a file from an archive no longer doubles its extension.** In some setups the downloaded file could come out named `…​.gcode.3mf.3mf`. This only happened when the browser couldn't read the server's suggested filename (e.g. behind a reverse proxy that doesn't forward the `Content-Disposition` header) and the archive had no separate print name, so the app fell back to a name it built itself and appended `.3mf` unconditionally. The fallback now keeps the existing extension instead of adding a second one.
- **Reprinting from an archive attaches the new run's finish photo, not the old one.** When a print reuses an existing archive record (queued / adopted / re-dispatched prints), its completion notification could carry the finish photo from the previous run. The stale photo is now cleared when the reused record starts a fresh print, so the notification shows the run that just finished.
- **The connection diagnostic no longer fails "external storage" on A1 and A1 Mini.** Those printers have no MicroSD slot, so the external-storage check is now skipped for them (reported as "not applicable") instead of showing a red failure. Printers that do have the slot (X1/P1/P2S/H2 families) still get the real check.
- **The Failure Analysis chart now shows readable reason names.** The stats widget was displaying the raw internal keys (e.g. `firstLayerAdhesion`) instead of the translated labels. It now shows the same human-readable, localised reason names used elsewhere; older records that stored the label directly still display correctly.
- **The System page's boot time is no longer off by your timezone offset.** The server was sending the boot timestamp without a timezone marker, so the browser applied your local offset a second time and showed the wrong time. Timestamps are now sent as UTC and converted once.
- **Quieter logs: routine "file already gone" and "not connected" messages are no longer warnings.** Deleting a print file that the printer had already removed (normal on the A1's self-cleaning storage), and a harmless "printer not connected" status nudge, were logged as warnings and cluttered the log. They're now debug-level; genuine delete failures and connection problems are still logged as warnings.
- **Printing spool labels no longer downloads two copies of the PDF per click.** A window-flags quirk made the "open in new tab" path always fall back to a download as well, so each click produced both a tab and a duplicate `bamdude-labels.pdf`. It now opens a single PDF.
- **Bambu Studio no longer sees a virtual printer's state thin out between status pushes.** When BamDude relays a real printer's state to Bambu Studio through a virtual printer, the printer sends a full snapshot occasionally and tiny partial updates ~once a second. The relay cache used to keep only a hand-picked list of fields across those partial updates, so the ~80 other capability/lifecycle fields drained away — Bambu Studio's device screens (AMS, calibration, firmware state) would grey out until the next full snapshot. The cache now preserves every field the printer has ever reported and merges partial updates on top, so the slicer always sees a complete state. Nested fields (like `vt_tray`) also merge instead of overwriting, so a partial update no longer drops sibling values.
- **Empty AMS slots no longer show up as loaded filament in Bambu Studio.** The virtual-printer relay now applies the printer's own empty-slot bitmap to the slicer-facing state, so a slot the printer reports as empty is presented as empty to Bambu Studio instead of carrying over a stale filament from a previous spool.
- **Open tabs now pick up a new version automatically after an update.** Previously, after upgrading BamDude, an already-open browser tab kept running the old bundle until you manually refreshed. The tab now reloads itself once the new version is installed — and only on a genuine update, never on first install (which could otherwise leave a fresh page stuck on a spinner).
- **The project edit dialog's Save button is now reachable on short screens.** On small displays (e.g. a Raspberry Pi touchscreen) a long project form pushed Save and Cancel off the bottom of the screen with no way to scroll to them. The dialog now caps its height, scrolls the fields, and keeps the action buttons pinned in a sticky footer.
- **The AMS drying popover's Start button is no longer hidden behind Safari's toolbar on iPhone.** On iOS Safari the bottom URL/toolbar overlay clipped the popover's footer, making the Start button unreachable. The popover now measures the actually-visible viewport (and uses `dvh` sizing), so its footer stays above the toolbar.
- **Restoring a backup into a PostgreSQL install no longer deadlocks and rolls back.** During a restore, timer-based background services (the print scheduler, smart-plug snapshot loop, notification digest, and dispatch worker) could wake on their normal cadence and grab row locks just as the restore tried to drop and recreate every table — producing a deadlock that aborted the whole restore. Those services are now paused before the database swap, and the drop step caps its lock wait at 10 seconds so any stray writer surfaces a clear error instead of hanging. (A container restart after restore, already required, brings the services back.)
- **Scheduled local backups now run at the time you enter in your local timezone, not UTC.** The time-of-day picker under Settings → Scheduled Local Backups was interpreted as UTC, so a UTC+3 user who wanted a 21:00 backup had to enter 18:00. It's now interpreted in the container's timezone (the `TZ` env var, the same value the Support page shows), with a `Local time (Europe/Kyiv)` hint next to the field so the zone is unambiguous. UTC is still used when `TZ` is unset. One-time note: if you previously entered a UTC time as a workaround, re-enter it as your local time once after upgrading.
- **System uptime is now correct inside Docker/LXC containers.** The System page read the host's boot time (via `/proc/stat`) on containerised installs, so uptime reflected how long the host had been running, not BamDude. It now uses PID 1's start time, which is the container's start on containers and matches host boot on bare metal.
- **The systemd installer no longer fails to start when installed under `/home`.** The hardened service used `ProtectHome=true`, which hides `/home/*` and broke the service's own executable path when the install directory was under a home folder (`#1685`). The installer now uses `ProtectHome=read-only` for `/home`-rooted installs (and the shipped unit file defaults to read-only, with a comment on how to tighten it if your install lives elsewhere).
- **A1 Mini cloud filament profiles no longer vanish from the AMS slot and Profiles pickers.** Bambu renamed the `@BBL` model suffix on its cloud profiles mid-2026 — an A1 Mini profile that used to read `Bambu PLA Basic @BBL A1 Mini …` now ships as `@BBL A1M`. BamDude compared that suffix against the printer name verbatim, so every renamed cloud profile silently disappeared when you selected an A1 Mini printer. `A1M` is now recognised as A1 Mini everywhere the model filter runs (AMS slot Configure dropdown, Profiles filtering, and 3MF model detection). The match stays narrow on purpose — it won't collapse genuinely different printers like A1 and A1 Mini.
- **Assigning certain Polymaker (and other partner) spools now shows in the slicer immediately.** Some cloud filament presets — notably Polymaker's "(Custom)" Bambu Lab H2D variants — use a `PFCN…` preset id that the assign flow didn't recognise as a cloud id (it only handled `GFS…` and `PFUS…`). The raw id was pushed to the AMS slot, so the slicer's filament dropdown showed "unknown" until you opened Configure. `PFCN…` presets are now resolved to their underlying filament id just like the other cloud shapes, so the slot is recognised right after assigning.
- **Queue prints are attributed to a user even when the item had no owner, and the printer now shows who started them.** Starting a queue item that carried no uploader (e.g. added before auth was configured) left the resulting archive with an empty **User** column; it is now credited to the operator who clicked Start, while items that already have an uploader keep theirs. Queue-started prints also now populate the printer's live "current print user" instead of showing no one mid-print.
- **A print the printer accepts but never actually starts no longer wedges the queue.** When a printer acknowledged a dispatched job (its internal task id advanced) but then stalled before the print began running — reported after a power cycle on older firmware, where the printer re-authenticates cloud + LAN and can hang mid-handshake — the queue item was marked "printing" and stuck there until the container restarted. Dispatch now treats that acknowledgement as only the first phase: it keeps watching for the print to actually reach an active state, and if that never happens it fails the job cleanly so the queue can move on instead of freezing.
- **Background tasks no longer vanish mid-run, and their errors now reach the logs.** Some fire-and-forget background jobs (cooldown power-off, finish-photo notifications, timelapse scans, startup sweeps, and others) could be garbage-collected before they finished — producing an opaque `Task was destroyed but it is pending!` warning and silently swallowing any error they hit. These now run through a single helper that keeps them alive to completion and logs any uncaught error with a full traceback, so failures surface as actionable log entries instead of disappearing.
- **X2D print archives no longer land empty.** On the X2D (firmware 01.01.00.00), the secure FTP handshake BamDude uses to pull each print's `.3mf` at print start was being rejected (`WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER`), so archive cards showed only the print time — no filament weight, layers, MakerWorld link, or thumbnail — and Spoolman usage tracking went silent for that printer. The X2D now caps its FTPS connection to TLS 1.2 (matching what its firmware accepts), the same per-model approach already used for the P2S. Every other model is unchanged. *(This ships as a strong hypothesis based on the P2S fix; if your X2D still shows empty cards, let us know.)*
- **Printing one plate of a multi-plate project no longer over-debits your spools.** When you sent a single plate from a multi-plate `.3mf`, both the built-in filament inventory and the Spoolman integration parsed the whole file and subtracted *every* plate's filament from the spool — so a small lid print could debit the entire project's grey + black totals. Filament tracking is now scoped to the plate that actually ran (using the archive's recorded plate), for queue, reprint, and Send-to-Printer jobs alike.
- **Prints that finish while BamDude is disconnected — or that a printer auto-restarts — no longer get stuck as "printing".** The print reconciliation sweep now re-runs on every reconnect (not just the first one after startup), so a print that completes during a network drop or an overnight outage is closed and its queue advanced once the connection returns. It also now recognises a firmware "ghost replay" — when a printer (often after a smart-plug power-cycle) re-runs the same file under a new internal job id — and closes the superseded print (flagged as outcome-uncertain) instead of leaving it tracked forever.
- **Cancelled prints no longer count as failures in your statistics.** The Stats page bucketed user- or queue-cancelled prints together with genuine failures, which dragged the Success Rate gauge down and inflated the failure rate whenever you stopped a job. Cancelled / stopped prints now have their own **Cancelled** line in the breakdown, the Success Rate gauge divides by completed + failed only, and the failure-rate widget (and its weekly trend) excludes them from both the numerator and the denominator. They still count toward Total Prints.
- **Paused prints no longer inflate hours-based maintenance reminders.** The per-printer runtime counter that drives hours-based maintenance intervals (rod lubrication, belt checks, nozzle cleaning) was accruing time while a print sat paused. A paused printer isn't moving and isn't wearing, so overnight pauses were bringing maintenance warnings forward early. Only actively-printing time now accrues.
- **Maintenance custom-type Documentation URL now saves.** Setting a Documentation URL when creating a custom maintenance type was silently dropped on save; it now persists.
- **Ghost prints from doubled file extensions are cleaned off the SD card.** When a library file's stored name ended up with a doubled `.gcode.3mf` extension, the post-print SD-card cleanup looked for the wrong filename and left the real file behind — which some printers (A1 / P1S) then auto-start as a ghost print on the next power cycle. Cleanup now derives the exact uploaded filename the same way the dispatcher does, so the file is reliably removed.
- **Fallback archives now record which filament was used.** When BamDude can't download the 3MF at print start (P1S / A1 / P2S lock the file mid-print), the archive it creates from live MQTT data now also captures the filament type and colour from the AMS state, so those archives show the right material in inventory and reprint views instead of a blank.
- **Attaching a source 3MF to a cloud / SD-card print no longer errors.** Uploading an original project file to an archive that BamDude created without a downloaded 3MF (cloud, Bambu Handy, or a pre-existing SD-card print) returned a 500 and could leave a stray file outside the data folder. Those archives now store the source under a dedicated location inside the data volume.
- **Quieter logs around STL thumbnails.** Generating STL/OBJ thumbnails no longer floods the log with matplotlib font-cache chatter on first use, and bulk-uploading a ZIP of tiny placeholder STLs no longer produces a warning per file (they're skipped up front as too small to contain a mesh). The matplotlib cache is also kept in the data directory so it survives restarts.
- **Clearer errors when Cloudflare blocks Bambu Cloud or ntfy.** If Bambu Lab's Cloudflare protection intermittently serves a challenge page instead of the API response, Bambu Cloud sign-in now explains that it's a temporary Cloudflare block on Bambu's side (with what to try) instead of a bare "Invalid response". Likewise, testing an **ntfy** server that sits behind a Cloudflare challenge now returns an actionable message — add a Cloudflare security-skip rule, disable Bot Fight Mode, or use Cloudflare Access — instead of dumping the raw challenge HTML. Notification requests also now identify themselves honestly (BamDude user-agent) like the rest of the app's outbound calls.
- **OIDC sign-in now fills in your email even when the username comes from a different claim.** If you configure the **Email Claim** to a non-email identity claim (e.g. `preferred_username` on Authentik) to drive the username, new auto-provisioned accounts now still capture your real email address from the standard `email` claim, instead of leaving the email blank. The account-linking safety checks are unchanged — linking to an existing account still relies only on the configured claim.
- **Filament usage is attributed to the right spool when an AMS has an empty slot.** With a layout like "3 AMS slots loaded + 1 empty + an external spool", the slicer's last filament was being charged to the empty AMS slot instead of the external spool, so the external spool's usage went unrecorded. Empty AMS slots are now skipped when matching the slicer's filament order to physical trays.
- **Camera stream-token timestamps now show in your local timezone.** The "created", "expires", and "last used" times on long-lived camera tokens (Settings → API Keys) were rendered from the server's UTC clock, so they appeared shifted by your timezone offset — and a token could look expired (or not) at the wrong moment. They now display in the browser's local time.
- **A virtual printer with a target now uses the target's access code automatically — no more silent bridge failures from a mismatched code.** Archive / Review / Queue virtual printers that point at a real printer bridge the slicer's connection through to that printer, so the codes on both sides have to match. Previously you could set a different code on the virtual printer, the slicer would connect, and the bridge would then silently fail to reach the real printer. When a target is selected the access-code field is now read-only and shows the target's code (with a reveal toggle); the backend enforces this on save, and a one-time upgrade step corrects any existing virtual printers whose code had drifted. Standalone virtual printers (no target) still have an editable code.
- **Dual-nozzle printers can now control nozzle-offset calibration per print.** On the H2D, H2D Pro, H2C and X2D, the print dialog now shows a "Nozzle Offset Calibration" toggle (on by default, matching Bambu Studio). Previously the calibration was always skipped with no way to enable it — and there was no way to keep it off deliberately, which matters for diamond-nozzle setups that must not run it. The toggle only appears for dual-nozzle printers; single-nozzle machines always skip the calibration regardless. The choice is remembered per queue item and applied on every dispatch path.
- **AMS drying is now available on the H2C (firmware 01.02.00.00 and newer).** The H2C was treated as a model that doesn't support AMS drying at all, so the dry controls never appeared. It's now version-gated like the H2S and P2S: on firmware 01.02.00.00 or later the drying controls show up for compatible AMS units.
- **Empty AMS units no longer send hourly humidity and temperature alarms.** An AMS with no filament loaded still reports ambient humidity/temperature, so anyone running a loaded unit alongside an empty one got useful alarms for the loaded unit and hourly noise for the empty one — and silencing the category killed both. Alarms are now dispatched only for AMS units that actually have filament loaded, checked per unit, so a multi-AMS setup still alarms on the loaded ones. Sensor history keeps recording for every unit, so the System page humidity charts stay continuous.
- **The filament type dropdown now includes carbon-fibre, glass-fibre and specialty materials.** Editing a custom filament profile only offered the base materials, so you couldn't tag a preset as PLA-CF, PLA-GF, PLA Aero, PETG-CF, ABS-GF, ASA-CF/GF, PCTG, PAHT-CF, PA6-CF/GF, or PPS/PPS-CF/GF. All of these are now selectable, grouped by material family. Existing baseline materials are unchanged.
- **AMS slots with a spool loaded but no filament type set now stand out instead of looking empty.** When a spool is physically loaded into an AMS slot but its filament type hasn't been configured yet, the slot used to render the same as a truly empty slot (a plain "-"), so it was easy to miss that it needed setting up. Such slots now show a "?" with an amber-outlined slot circle and a "Spool loaded — slot not configured" hover label, across every AMS view (regular AMS, AMS Lite and AMS HT). Genuinely empty slots keep their plain dashed look.
- **Archive names with an ampersand (or other special characters) now display correctly.** A model titled e.g. "PCB Vise & Solder Station" was stored with the raw XML encoding ("PCB Vise &amp; Solder Station") and then shown with the escaping still visible. Titles embedded in the 3MF are now decoded before saving, so the name reads as intended. The "Archive name source" help text (virtual printer settings) has also been corrected: Bambu Studio replaces the name you type in its "send to printer" dialog with the model's Title whenever one is present, so the "Filename" and "Metadata" modes often produce the same name.
- **Bambu Studio 2.7.x "Send" no longer gets stuck on "Downloading" with a virtual printer.** Newer Bambu Studio uploads the file before sending the MQTT print command; that reordering left the virtual printer reporting "preparing" indefinitely, so the Send dialog never released — even after deleting from the queue or completing a real print. The virtual printer now re-confirms the finished state a moment after the print command, so the dialog closes as expected. Proxy-mode printers are unaffected (the real printer drives their state).
- **Docker bridge-mode setups no longer need a 1000-port range for virtual printer file transfers.** The virtual printer's FTP passive-data range was 50000–51000 (1001 ports); under Docker's default networking every published port spawns a host proxy process, so that range alone pinned around 3.5 GB of host RAM (invisible in `docker stats`, since it's host-level). Each virtual printer now gets its own small 10-port slice allocated by its id (VP 1 → 50000–50009, VP 2 → 50010–50019, …), so the sample `docker-compose.yml` exposes just `50000-50029` for three printers — roughly 210 MB instead of 3.5 GB. Widen the range to add more printers; proxy-mode printers still forward the real printer's full range.
- **A proxy virtual printer's card now shows the target printer's real serial.** In proxy mode the virtual printer advertises the target printer's serial to slicers (over SSDP and in its certificate), but its settings card showed a different, self-generated serial — confusing when you compared the two. The card now displays the same serial the slicer sees. Archive/queue/file-manager virtual printers keep their own generated serial (they never impersonate a real printer), and a proxy pointed at a since-removed printer falls back to the generated serial so the card still renders.
