- **Slicers no longer get disconnected from a virtual printer after sitting idle.** A slicer that connected and then sat idle — some OrcaSlicer builds send no keepalive pings at all — was being disconnected after a short interval, causing "publish failed" errors and forcing a reconnect. The virtual printer now behaves like real Bambu firmware: it keeps idle sessions open and relies on the OS network stack (TCP keepalive) to detect genuinely dead connections, so the slicer stays connected until you actually close it.
- **Bambu Studio's "Send" no longer occasionally refuses a virtual printer over stale state.** The virtual printer now preserves more firmware fields (firmware/upgrade state, camera settings, nozzle info, AMS status, and more) across the frequent lightweight status updates, so the slicer's Send pre-flight reads a complete picture rather than a stripped-down one.
- **Bambu Studio can now "Send" to a virtual printer even while the real target printer is mid-print.** The pre-flight check was reading the real printer's live progress (percentage, current stage, layer numbers) through the virtual printer and treating it as "busy", so it refused the upload. The virtual printer now presents itself as idle across all of those fields, matching how it already reported its print state.
- **Queue / Review / Archive virtual printers now connect on hardened-Linux hosts.** On systems whose crypto policy narrows the default TLS cipher list (Fedora / RHEL with `update-crypto-policies`, hardened Alpine containers), OrcaSlicer / Bambu Studio could fail to connect to a non-proxy virtual printer with a bare `code=-1` right after the network handshake. Every slicer-facing TLS port (the bind/detect port, MQTT, FTPS, and the proxy-mode listener) now explicitly offers the cipher suites real Bambu printers use, so the handshake completes regardless of the host's crypto policy.
- **Slicer "Send" to a virtual printer no longer slips through to the real printer's SD card on a flat LAN.** When a virtual printer had no dedicated bind IP (the common default), it failed to rewrite the printer address it advertises to the slicer, so once the real printer was online the slicer sent straight to it and bypassed BamDude's archive. The virtual printer now auto-detects the right host address to advertise.
- **Virtual printers now work when the real printer was added by hostname instead of an IP address.** If you'd added your printer as something like `p1s.fritz.box` (common on home networks with router-provided DNS), the virtual printer couldn't rewrite the address it advertises to the slicer, so "Send" went straight to the real printer and bypassed the archive. The virtual printer now resolves the hostname to an IP first. If that address rewrite ever fails to take effect, the log now states exactly why (no IP yet, hostname didn't resolve, no matching network interface), so a "Send bypassed the archive" report can be diagnosed from `docker logs` in one pass.
- **Multiple slicers pointed at the same virtual printer no longer see each other's responses.** Each command's reply is now routed back only to the slicer that issued it, instead of being broadcast to every connected slicer.
- **A Proxy-mode virtual printer now follows its target printer to a new IP.** If the real printer's address changes (e.g. after a DHCP lease renewal), the proxy restarts automatically against the new address instead of forwarding to the stale one until you toggle the printer off and on.
- **Slicers keep trusting a virtual printer after its internal certificate authority is renewed.** The virtual printers share a self-signed certificate authority that you import into your slicer once. When that authority was regenerated (e.g. it reached its renewal window), each virtual printer kept serving its old certificate — signed by the now-replaced authority — so the slicer's freshly-imported authority no longer matched and the TLS connection failed. Each virtual printer now detects the mismatch and reissues its certificate under the current authority automatically.
- **Rapidly switching a virtual printer's mode no longer occasionally leaves a port stuck.** Under quick mode changes the proxy's data-transfer listeners could linger for up to a minute holding their port, causing the next start to fail; they're now torn down promptly.
- **The virtual printer's built-in diagnostic now checks both discovery ports.** It previously probed only the secure discovery port (3002) and could report a virtual printer as healthy when its plain discovery port (3000) had failed to start; it now requires both.
- **More headroom for concurrent uploads across many virtual printers.** The FTP passive-data port range widened from 101 ports (50000–50100) to 1001 (50000–51000), so large multi–virtual-printer setups no longer run out of data ports and stall transfers under load. If you run in Docker bridge mode and expose this range, update the mapping to `50000-51000` (see `docker-compose.yml`).
- **Virtual-printer housekeeping.** Two prints sent to a virtual printer's queue back-to-back are now ordered correctly (they previously both landed at position 1); deleting a virtual printer removes its leftover upload files from disk; and the virtual printer no longer momentarily reports itself as "running" before its ports are actually open.
- **The Tailscale status on the virtual printer settings no longer errors out when the Tailscale CLI is slow to respond.** A wedged or unresponsive Tailscale daemon made the status request time out with a server error; it now falls back cleanly to showing Tailscale as unavailable.
- **Illegal print-file names are now rejected when you rename, upload, or queue a file — not hours later at the printer.** Names containing characters the printer's SD card can't store (`< > : " / \ | ? *`, control characters, or a trailing space/dot) previously slipped through and only failed at send time with an obscure `553 Could not create file` error, far from the action that caused it. The rename dialog now flags the offending character instantly (matching Bambu Studio), and the upload/queue/print paths refuse such names up front with a clear message.
- **AMS-detected transparent filament is no longer mislabelled "Black".** A transparent spool (AMS reports it as fully transparent, `00000000`) was having its transparency dropped and its remaining black RGB named "Black" in the filament-mapping dropdown and inventory. Transparent spools now resolve to the name **Clear** and paint a checkerboard swatch end-to-end — AMS detection, the assign-spool picker, the forecast panel, and the label sheet.
- **Assigning a spool no longer nags about a profile-only mismatch.** Assigning a spool whose slicer profile differed from the AMS slot popped a confirmation even though BamDude reconfigures the slot to the spool's profile on every assign anyway — so the warning was noise. Only a genuine *material* mismatch (which the printer firmware can reject) now warns, and that dialog spells out that the slot will be reconfigured.
- **Archive "Hide duplicates" filter and "Duplicates" collection now agree with the reprint badge across differently-patched printers.** Both keyed on `content_hash` — the hash of the *patched* bytes actually sent over FTP — which differs per applied patch (e.g. the `mesh_mode_fast_check` gcode patch applied on one printer but not another). So printing the same sliced file on two printers left both rows visible (and absent from the Duplicates collection) even though the reprint badge already counted them as one. Both now key on the same source-file hash the badge uses (`COALESCE(source_content_hash, content_hash)`). "Hide duplicates" also now collapses within the *current filtered view* (printer, collection, date), so it no longer hides every copy when the earliest sibling sits behind an active filter or in the trash.

## [0.4.6] - 2026-05-29

Stable 0.4.6 release. Image: `ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.6` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.6` (`:latest` tracks this).

Post-0.4.5 polish, led by **inventory**: bulk-edit selected spools, batch-copy, a colour filter, a purchase-location field, and usage-history deletion with weight reclaim — plus the auto-queue gives prints priority over AMS drying, the virtual printer no longer wedges as "busy" in the slicer, three Skip-Objects fixes (incl. correct object positions read from the pick image — thanks @latsss), a Statistics "Records" correction, and a P2S FTPS reliability fix.

### Added

- **Record where each spool was bought.** Spools now have an optional **Purchase location** field (e.g. "AliExpress", a local shop) — separate from *Storage location* (where the spool is kept). It appears on the spool form, in **Bulk edit**, and as an optional inventory table column.

- **Bulk-edit spools.** New **Bulk edit** action on the Filament inventory opens a dialog where you pick which spools to change (defaults to all currently-filtered, deselect any you want to skip) and **tick which fields to apply**: slicer preset, material, brand, subtype, label weight, colour, empty-spool weight (spool type), date of purchase, filament diameter, cost/kg, note, category, low-stock threshold, extra colour stops, visual effect, and storage location. Inputs mirror the single-spool form — preset / effect / diameter / empty-spool are dropdowns; material, brand and subtype autocomplete from everything the system knows (slicer presets + colour catalog + built-ins), not just the filtered spools, so you can switch to any known brand/variant; category and storage autocomplete from your existing inventory; and the colour picker draws from your colour catalog filtered by the brand + material being applied (choosing a name fills the hex, and the list updates when you change brand/material) — rather than plain text. The autocomplete dropdowns are compact and scrollable (matching the spool-edit dialog), and the whole dialog resets each time you reopen it. Each field pre-fills when the selected spools already share one value, otherwise shows *"— varies —"*; only ticked fields are written, the rest are left as they are. **Consumed weight and RFID tags are never touched.** Internal inventory only (not Spoolman mode).
- **Copy a spool in batch.** The **Quick Add (Stock)** toggle — which adds a *Quantity* field to create several identical spools at once — is now available when **copying** a spool, not just when adding a brand-new one. Copying still starts each spool fresh (usage reset to 0, no RFID tag carried over), so you can clone an existing spool's filament/colour into a whole new batch in one go. Edit mode stays single-spool.
- **Filter the Filament inventory by colour.** The inventory toolbar has a new **Colour** dropdown alongside Material / Brand. Its options are the colours you actually have **in stock** — built from your existing (non-archived) spools and grouped by resolved colour *name* (so two near-identical hexes that both read as "Black" filter together, using the same colour catalog as the rest of the app). Picking a colour narrows the list to spools of that colour; it clears with **Clear filters** like the other chips. (Internal inventory only — unaffected by Spoolman mode.)
- **Delete spool usage-history entries and reclaim the weight.** Each row in a spool's **Usage History** now has a small hover **×** that removes just that consumption record. Removing a record (or using bulk **Clear**, which now behaves the same way) **returns its weight to the spool** — the filament counts as unused again, so the spool's remaining weight goes back up — *and* subtracts the same amount from the linked print's recorded filament, so the **Statistics page stays in step with inventory**. For a multi-colour print, removing one colour's record only reclaims that colour's share. Useful for cleaning up a mistaken or test print that shouldn't count against the spool.

### Fixed

- **Statistics "Records" widget corrected.** *Longest / Heaviest / Most Expensive Print* counted **failed, aborted and cancelled prints** — so a print that failed mid-way could show up as your "longest" or "most expensive" print. Records now consider **completed prints only**. The **Success Streak** now reports the **longest** run of consecutive successful prints within the selected period (it previously showed only the *current* trailing streak). Records already follow the page's selected time period.
- **Skip Objects: three fixes.** (1) **Now works for prints started from the slicer.** The object list is now read from the already-downloaded archive 3MF first, falling back to an FTP pull from the printer — previously a slicer-started print (where BamDude's dispatcher never ran) often showed no skippable objects even though the file was sitting in the archive. The Skip Objects **button is now enabled** for these prints too — the "supports object skipping" flag was previously left unset on the slicer-start path, so the button stayed greyed out even once the objects had loaded. It also **stays enabled across the periodic mid-print MQTT reconnect** (every ~5 min for some models), which used to reset printer state and re-grey the button until a restart. (2) **Object ID positions on the preview now match the printer screen.** Positions are read from the slice's pick image (each object instance is painted with its own ID), instead of guessing by object name — which placed every copy in the wrong spot whenever a plate used the slicer's "instances" feature (identical names, one merged bounding box). (3) **Removed the artificial "wait for layer 2+" block** — you can skip objects from the very start of a print. Thanks to **[@latsss](https://github.com/latsss)** for reporting all three and for [bambu-cli](https://github.com/latsss/bambu-cli) — its pick-image (`pick_N.png`) object-id decoding showed the correct way to read per-instance positions and is what fix (2) is built on.
- **Virtual printer no longer gets stuck showing "busy with another print job" in the slicer.** A virtual printer (any mode — File Manager, Queue, Auto-Queue) could wedge so that Bambu Studio / OrcaSlicer refused to send to it, reporting *"The printer is busy with another print job."* — and it stayed that way until BamDude restarted. The cause: when a slicer starts a send the VP flips to a "preparing" state, and that state was only cleared once the file finished uploading **as a `.3mf`**. If the upload was interrupted, failed, timed out, or arrived under a different name, the VP sat in "preparing" forever and every later send saw it as busy. The VP now (1) always returns to a ready state once an upload finishes — success **or** failure, any file type, even if saving the file errors — and (2) never advertises "preparing" to the slicer unless an upload is actually in progress, so a half-finished earlier attempt can't block the next one. Affects both Bambu Studio and OrcaSlicer (both treat "preparing" as busy).
- **Auto-queue now lets prints take priority over AMS drying, the same as directly-queued jobs.** When **Settings → Queue → "Block queue until drying completes"** is **off** (the default — "prints take priority over drying"), a job placed via the **auto-queue** (distributed by printer model) would still wait for an AMS dry cycle to finish on its own, even though a job queued directly to that printer would stop the drying and start printing. The auto-queue now treats a printer that is busy *only* because it's auto-drying as available: it routes the job there and the printer stops drying to begin the print — **but only if no truly-idle printer of the same model is free**, so drying is never interrupted needlessly. With the block setting **on**, drying still holds the queue. (Most relevant on P2S/H2 farms with AMS auto-drying enabled.)
- **Deleting a print archive no longer leaves dangling spool-usage links (and no longer risks failing on PostgreSQL).** A spool's consumption history (`spool_usage_history`) is meant to outlive the archive it came from, but its `archive_id` link wasn't being detached on delete — on SQLite that left the row pointing at a deleted archive, and on PostgreSQL the foreign key could block the deletion entirely. Archive deletion now clears the link first, and a one-time migration NULLs any already-orphaned links left by past deletes. The usage records themselves are always kept — only the stale link is severed.
- **Statistics over-counted filament for failed prints.** A print that failed part-way through still reported its *full slicer estimate* on the Statistics page, even though inventory had only been deducted by the filament actually extruded. Stats and inventory now agree: a finished print that stopped early records the **actual consumed weight** (and its cost is no longer inflated by the un-printed remainder). A one-time migration **backfills existing failed / aborted / cancelled / stopped prints** from the per-spool usage that was already recorded, so historical totals correct themselves automatically. Completed prints are unaffected.
- **P2S "can't send file / SD card" failures during busy farms (FTPS `WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER`).** Bambu printers' FTPS server accepts roughly one session at a time. On print-complete BamDude fired the post-print SD-cleanup **and** the 3MF archive-download at the *same* printer simultaneously (plus multi-path retries) — the overlapping second connection got a plaintext rejection that the implicit-TLS client surfaced as `[SSL: WRONG_VERSION_NUMBER]`, which then got mis-reported as an SD-card/"flash drive" problem even though the card was fine. Every FTP operation is now **serialized per printer** (never more than one connection to a printer at a time), and FTP retries use **exponential backoff** (capped 30s) so a just-finished P2S that's still flushing its SD card gets time to free the session. Especially affects multi-P2S farms. (This is separate from the #1401 TLS-1.2 cap, which addresses a different P2S symptom — data-channel truncation.)
- **The login page logo is no longer broken.** It referenced the old `bambuddy_logo_*` filenames (renamed to `bamdude_logo_*` during the fork), so the logo 404'd on the login screen for everyone (Docker included). Also fixed the sidebar **GitHub** links, which still pointed at the predecessor repo (`kainpl/bambuddy-he`) instead of `kainpl/bamdude`.
- **Leftover "Bambuddy" branding swept out of the running app.** Stale fork-origin names were still showing in user-facing places: the default virtual-printer name + SSDP discovery name, default email sender/subject/body text and notification-template defaults, the support-bundle filename, and various log lines. All now read **BamDude**. (Upstream attribution in docs/comments and legacy database/backup compatibility — `bambuddy.db`, the `bambuddy-backup-*` legacy reader, the import migration — are intentionally left as-is.)

### Changed

- **Default MQTT topic prefix is now `bamdude` (was `bambuddy`).** If you publish to an external MQTT broker and never set **Settings → MQTT → topic prefix** explicitly, your published topics move from `bambuddy/*` to `bamdude/*` — update any Home Assistant / Node-RED subscriptions accordingly (or set the prefix back to `bambuddy` in Settings to keep the old topics). The internal MQTT client IDs were rebranded too (cosmetic; brokers rarely key on them).

## [0.4.5] - 2026-05-26

Stable 0.4.5 release — consolidates the cumulative b1–b5 beta cycle (see the `[0.4.5b5]` and earlier beta sections below for the full per-beta detail) plus the post-b5 work listed here. Image: `ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.5` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.5` (`:latest` now tracks this).

The major user-facing arrivals across the 0.4.5 line: **mass (bulk) firmware updates** across the whole farm — per-model tabs, download-once-then-parallel, skip mid-print, a durable firmware store that survives the vendor pulling a version, and an update log; **anonymized opt-out usage telemetry**; a **self-diagnostics suite** (connection diagnostic, virtual-printer setup checklist + one-click slicer-certificate export, log-health scanner — all folded into the support bundle / bug report); **slicing** gains cross-nozzle-class re-slicing, re-slicing a saved 3MF for a different printer, and printer-aware process/filament profile filtering; an **installable, fully-offline PWA**; **Filament Calibration Wave 2** (Temperature / Retraction / VFA / Max-Volumetric-Speed towers run as real calibration prints); and the close of the upstream Bambuddy v0.2.4.2→0.2.4.3 audit.

### Added

- **Mass firmware updates.** Update firmware across many printers from one page (sidebar → **Firmware**) instead of one modal at a time. Printers are grouped into per-model tabs; pick a version per model — **newer or older**, since rollback is a first-class case — and BamDude downloads each model's firmware **once**, then pushes it to every selected printer's SD card in parallel (with a configurable concurrency cap, default 2, under Settings). Printers that are mid-print are skipped and flagged; a failure on one printer never stops the rest, and every run records a per-printer result. A new **durable firmware store** keeps every downloaded version (indexed by model + version, sha256-verified) so you can still install — or roll back to — a version even after Bambu removes it from their site; such cached-only versions show up in the version list too. You can **pre-download any version into the store without touching a printer**, and the version picker shows at a glance which versions are already in the store vs. not. An **Update log** tab records every firmware run with timestamps and per-printer results — and **single-printer updates from the existing modal are logged there too** (tagged "single" vs "bulk"), so the journal is the one place to see all firmware activity. The existing single-printer firmware flow is otherwise unchanged (it now shares the same store). As with the single-printer update, the final "apply" is a quick step on each printer's own screen — every run finishes by showing the model-specific on-screen instruction for the printers it uploaded to. en + uk.

### Fixed

- **Queue error state is simpler and no longer gets stuck "resumed but not running".** A queue can be halted by two independent things — the operator's pause (`is_paused`) and a fault (`status='error'`) — and they can be set at the same time. The old separate error banner had its own "Resume" button that cleared only the error, leaving the operator pause in place, so the queue still wouldn't dispatch (its only hint a small "paused" pill). The error banner is removed; the error now shows in the queue's status pill alongside idle/printing/paused, and the single Pause/Resume control fully resumes — clearing both the fault and the pause at once.
- **Filament Calibration no longer hides your own process/filament profiles (notably on A1 mini).** The wizard filtered profiles against a *fabricated* printer name built from the hardware short code (`Bambu Lab A1 Mini 0.4 nozzle`), whose casing didn't match the real preset name the slicer records in `compatible_printers` (`Bambu Lab A1 mini`) — so a printer's own cloud profiles were treated as a wrong-printer mismatch and dropped, often leaving a single unrelated profile visible. The wizard now resolves the actually-selected printer preset's real name (the same approach the Slice dialog uses, which was unaffected); your compatible profiles show again while genuinely wrong-printer ones stay filtered. (Regression from the #1325 profile-filtering port.)

## [0.4.5b5] - 2026-05-25

Fifth beta of the 0.4.5 cycle. Adds anonymized, opt-out usage telemetry (Phase 4) and a self-diagnostics suite — a one-click connection diagnostic, a virtual-printer setup checklist with one-click slicer-certificate export, and a log-health scanner that triages your own logs against known-issue signatures, all folded into the support bundle / bug report. Slicing gains cross-nozzle-class re-slicing (single ↔ dual), re-slicing a saved 3MF for a different printer, and printer-aware process/filament profile filtering. Plus an installable, fully-offline PWA (self-hosted Inter font + in-app install button), click-through file print history, one-click SD-card wipe, and K-profiles that auto-link to spools by filament. Closes the upstream Bambuddy v0.2.4.2→0.2.4.3 audit: camera robustness (P2S stream, ffmpeg-version compat), MQTT hardening (malformed-packet survival, serial normalization), Spoolman parity fixes, P1S/P2S FTP fixes, and a flow-dynamics calibration fix. Image: `ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.5b5` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.5b5`. Pin the exact tag — `:latest` still tracks 0.4.4.1.

### Added

- **Support bundles & bug reports now include the diagnostic results.** The connection diagnostic, virtual-printer setup checks, and the log-health scan — previously shown only to you in the UI — are now captured into the support bundle ZIP and the submitted bug report, so a "looks broken" report arrives with actionable signal instead of raw logs alone. The snapshot runs every probe concurrently, is fail-soft per probe (one broken check never sinks the whole bundle) with a ~15 s per-probe cap, and is sanitized with the same name/serial/IP/access-code redaction as the logs. Both flows now show the steps in progress so the (slightly longer) wait isn't opaque. (Upstream Bambuddy 3b9633a1 / #1506 follow-up.)
- **Virtual-printer setup diagnostic + one-click slicer-certificate export.** A stethoscope button on each virtual-printer card runs a pass/fail/warn/skip checklist — VP enabled, services running, bind interface still exists, access code set, target printer (proxy mode), and a live TCP probe of the FTP / MQTT / discovery ports on the bind IP (the only reliable signal that a service actually bound, since start errors are logged and swallowed). And a **Slicer certificate** row on the Virtual Printers page now offers Copy / Download of the shared CA certificate (`bamdude-virtual-printer-ca.crt`) plus its SHA-256 fingerprint — no more `docker exec … cat bbl_ca.crt`; only the public certificate is exposed, the CA private key never leaves the backend. en + uk. (Upstream Bambuddy 6bc6a1d6.)

- **Log Health scanner — self-service triage from your logs.** A new **System Health** section on the System page (and an inline panel in the bug reporter) scans recent logs against a curated catalog of known-issue signatures — rejected access code, FTPS :990 timeout, FTPS TLS handshake failure, flapping MQTT, unreachable camera, SQLite "database is locked" contention — and for each match shows a plain-language cause + fix and a link to the troubleshooting docs, classified as "you can fix this" / environment / please-report. Sample log lines are sanitized (printer names, serials, IPs, access codes, emails redacted) before they leave the process, and noisy individually-benign symptoms only surface after they recur. `GET /system/health`. en + uk. (Upstream Bambuddy e222a0ef.)
- **Add/Edit-Printer setup pre-flight.** Saving a printer now runs the connection diagnostic first and, if a check hard-fails, shows the failing checks inline with a "Save anyway" escape hatch — so you don't add a printer that immediately shows offline (a mistyped IP, wrong access code, or printer not in LAN mode is caught at save time). The diagnostic infrastructure failing never blocks the save. The Add-Printer dialog also gained a manual "Run diagnostic" button for the entered connection. en + uk. (Upstream Bambuddy e222a0ef.)
- **Connection Diagnostic for "printer won't connect / won't print".** A one-click checklist that runs the checks a maintainer would do by hand — TCP reachability of the MQTT (8883), FTPS (990) and camera (322) ports, Docker network mode, printer/host subnet match, MQTT credentials, and LAN Developer Mode — and explains each failure with a concrete fix. Run it from the printer card's action menu, a quick button that appears on an offline printer, or the new **Connection Diagnostic** section on the System page; the in-app bug reporter also scans your printers when the form opens and shows the result inline so a setup problem can be self-fixed before filing a report. en + uk. (Upstream Bambuddy #1465 triage / 76e327f4.)
- **Event-loop stall watchdog.** A frozen asyncio loop used to be invisible — no log line, `/health` hangs, the process may ignore SIGTERM. A heartbeat now re-arms a `faulthandler` timer that dumps every thread's stack to stderr if the loop stalls for 30s, so a hard freeze shows up in `docker compose logs` with the blocked frame instead of nothing. (Upstream Bambuddy #1486 / 774eba73.)

- **Cross-nozzle-class re-slicing (single ↔ dual nozzle).** Re-slicing a 3MF built for a single-nozzle printer onto an H2D/H2C/X2D (or vice-versa) now works: BamDude detects the class crossing and forwards the slicer's `--arrange` so BambuStudio repositions objects for the target bed and reconciles the embedded project settings, instead of failing on the source's coordinate layout. Multi-plate "slice all" across classes slices each plate independently and merges them into one 3MF. Per-slot filament picks for slots a plate doesn't use are normalised so the loaded-filament temperature validator doesn't reject the slice. (Upstream Bambuddy #1493.)
- **Re-slice a saved 3MF for a different printer.** The Slice dialog no longer blocks (or warns) when the 3MF was originally built for another printer model — the slicer re-slices it for whatever printer the chosen profile/bundle targets (bed, kinematics, nozzle count and start-gcode all come from the target). Removed the obsolete "printer mismatch" gate and its now-dead `source_printer_model` plumbing. (Upstream Bambuddy #1325 / ed27b27a.)
- **The Slice dialog filters process & filament profiles to the selected printer.** Picking a printer now narrows the process and filament dropdowns to the profiles compatible with it (by the profile's own `compatible_printers`, then by any uploaded Slicer Bundle, then by the `@BBL <model> <nozzle>` naming convention — nozzle-aware, so a 0.6-nozzle profile won't show for a 0.4 printer). Profiles for other printers drop into a trailing **"Other printers"** group rather than disappearing, and profiles with no printer info are never hidden. The printer and process dropdowns also **default to the profiles the 3MF was prepared with**, and switching printer re-picks any selection left incompatible. The same matcher now drives the **filament-calibration** wizard's profile picker (model **and** nozzle aware, where it was model-only before), there hiding incompatible profiles outright since a wrong-printer calibration print wastes a bed. (Upstream Bambuddy #1325 + follow-ups.) en + uk.
- **Belize Dollars (BZD) added to the cost-currency dropdown** (Settings → Cost). `getCurrencySymbol('BZD')` → `BZ$`. (Upstream Bambuddy #1454.)
- **K-profiles auto-link to spools by filament.** Once a printer is calibrated for a filament, every spool of that filament now picks up the K-profile automatically — no per-spool clicking. Matching is by the spool's slicer preset, resolved to its **base** for custom filaments (e.g. a custom "Generic PETG"-based filament inherits the Generic PETG calibration), across all your printers and nozzles. The chosen profile is auto-activated when none is active yet, manual PA-tab links always win, and each spool card now shows its linked K-profiles with an "auto" marker. Custom filaments also now correctly surface matching K-profiles and colours in the AMS slot dialog and the spool's PA-Profile tab (the base preset is resolved via the cloud `base_id`). en + uk.
- **"Clear SD card" in the printer file browser.** A one-click recursive wipe that deletes every file on the SD card and removes all nested subfolders, while keeping the standard top-level folders (cache, model, timelapse…) the printer expects to exist. Guarded by a danger confirmation, and refused while a print is running or paused (the active job streams from the card). The whole walk runs over a single FTP connection so it stays fast even on a card full of cached models. Requires the `printers:files` permission. en + uk.
- **Click a file name to see its print history.** In the File Manager (grid and list views) and on the MakerWorld → History tab, the file name is now a link that opens the Archive filtered to every print dispatched from that library file, with a dismissible chip showing which file you're drilled into. Backed by a new `library_file_id` filter on the archive list (the link is stamped at dispatch time, so external/manual prints never match). en + uk.
- **Install BamDude as an app, and the UI now works fully offline (#1460).** A new **Install app** button appears in the sidebar footer when the browser offers a PWA install (it captures `beforeinstallprompt` and re-fires it on click) — Chrome for Android dropped the automatic install banner in Chrome 108, so this is the only reliable install path there; the button hides itself once installed or where there's no programmatic install (iOS Safari). The Inter font is now **self-hosted** (variable woff2 served same-origin and cached by the service worker) instead of fetched from `fonts.googleapis.com` at runtime — the cross-origin fetch was CSP-blocked during install on Android and left the UI unstyled offline. The service worker now also passes cross-origin requests straight through (a blocked one no longer comes back as our cached `index.html`), and the CSP drops the now-unused Google Font hosts. en + uk. (Upstream Bambuddy #1460 / 17e39921 + 16da533c.)
- **Anonymized usage telemetry (opt-out).** BamDude now sends a once-a-day anonymized snapshot — app version, OS platform/arch, Docker yes/no, aggregate counts (archives, printers, spools, projects, smart plugs, **users**, **AMS units**), printer **models** (never names/serials), enabled integrations (Spoolman, Obico, Telegram, OIDC, Git backup, slicer API) and daily print counts — to help prioritise development. It is keyed by a random `install_id` generated once at first boot (`DATA_DIR/.install_id`, mode 0600) and **not** linked to any account, email, IP or hardware id. The id is included in **backups** (so a restore keeps the same identity) and in the **support bundle / bug report** (so a report can be correlated with telemetry). **No IP is stored** by the collector (a coarse country is derived from the proxy header, then discarded), and no names, serials, file paths or settings values are ever sent. It is **on by default with disclosure** — a notice on the first-run setup screen, and a toggle under **Settings → General** (en + uk). Turning it off immediately asks the collector to erase this install's data. Disable entirely with `TELEMETRY_DISABLED=true`, or point it at your own collector with `TELEMETRY_RELAY_URL`. The daily send is fail-silent and never disrupts the app. Backend collector + admin dashboard live in the separate `bamdude-telemetry` project.

### Fixed

- **Adding a printer no longer hangs the whole app on a slow P1S (#1445).** The Add-Printer connection test slept a fixed 2 s then tore the probe down synchronously — paho's `loop_stop()` joins its network thread, which on a P1S still mid-TLS-handshake blocked the asyncio event loop and queued every other request behind it. The probe now polls up to 8 s (early-returning the moment the printer connects, so the happy path stays ~1–2 s) and tears the client down off-loop. (Upstream Bambuddy #1445 / 0406487e.)
- **The File Manager no longer hammers the printer over FTPS every 30 s (#1480).** The printer file browser auto-refetched on a 30 s interval, each time opening a fresh FTPS connection (full TLS handshake) — on fragile controllers like the P1S that tipped MQTT, FTP and the camera into simultaneous timeouts while the browser sat open. The list now refreshes on open, directory change, after upload/delete, and via the manual Refresh button — no background poll. STL-thumbnail failures also now log a traceback for support bundles. (Upstream Bambuddy #1480 / 50d19848.)
- **P2S file uploads no longer truncate ("unable to parse 3mf file") (#1401).** The P2S's vsFTPd build doesn't tolerate TLS 1.3's async session-ticket model on the FTPS data channel, so uploads aborted mid-stream (426) and landed truncated. A new per-model FTP profile (mirroring the camera profiles) caps the P2S to TLS 1.2; every other model stays on negotiated TLS 1.3 unchanged. (Upstream Bambuddy #1401 / 6f050708.)
- **Flow-dynamics calibration is no longer silently skipped (#1478).** The print command hardcoded `extrude_cali_flag=0` and integer-encoded the calibration/leveling fields for the H2 family on a mistaken belief — so the printer skipped flow calibration regardless of the toggle (poor corner quality from a stale K value). A real BambuStudio capture confirmed those fields are plain booleans for every model; `extrude_cali_flag` is now `1` (run) / `2` (reuse stored PA) paired with the flow-calibration toggle. `use_ams` stays boolean (the actual #1386 fix). (Upstream Bambuddy #1478 / 379b1c14.)
- **The AMS drying popover no longer renders off-screen with the Start button unreachable (#1447 / #1458).** It now flips above the trigger when it would overflow the bottom of the viewport, and on a short viewport the body scrolls with the Start button pinned to a fixed footer. The drying command + its response are now logged in full for support bundles. (Upstream Bambuddy #1447 / #1458 / 0f680394 + 8cff425c + fbaf2190.)
- **The Local Profiles search bar no longer vanishes when nothing matches (#1470).** Typing a query that matched no presets collapsed the whole view into the "import some profiles" empty state — which also removed the search box, so you couldn't edit or clear the query without a page refresh. The search bar now stays put and a "no presets match your search" message replaces the columns; the empty state is shown only when there genuinely are no presets. en + uk. (Upstream Bambuddy #1470 / 016e0178.)
- **The Obico Failure Detection status panel shows the thresholds for your configured sensitivity (#1469).** The live-status block reported the LOW/HIGH detection thresholds for a hardcoded "medium" regardless of the sensitivity actually set in Settings, so a farm running on "high" or "low" saw numbers that didn't match its behaviour. `get_status()` now derives the thresholds from the configured sensitivity. (Upstream Bambuddy #1469 / 5b3962e3.)
- **Post-restart and queued prints now attach their timelapse again (#1485 / #1403 follow-ups).** When BamDude restarted mid-print, the first MQTT push suppresses the print-start event (so a duplicate archive isn't created) — but that was also where the timelapse baseline was captured, so the completion scan fell back to snapshotting the SD card *after* the new video had already landed and never detected it as new. A restart-recovery hook now captures the baseline the first time RUNNING is observed for a print that began before BamDude came up, and the baseline is also captured on the queue/scheduled (expected-archive) print-start path that was missing it. (Upstream Bambuddy #1485 / ed08ed37 + #1403 / bfd3fc75.)
- **The Print Activity heatmap buckets prints by your local date, not UTC.** An evening print in a behind-UTC timezone (e.g. CDT) was counted on the following day's cell while its label still read today, so the calendar looked off by a day. Bucketing now uses the local-tz `YYYY-MM-DD`. (Upstream Bambuddy #1446 / fcee1a6f.)
- **Spoolman weight tracking now decrements tag-less spools assigned via the UI (#1459).** When a non-RFID spool was bound to a slot through the Assign-Spool UI, its deterministic fallback tag is intentionally kept out of Spoolman's `extra.tag` (so it doesn't pollute Spoolman), so the per-print weight tracker — which matched only by tag — never found it and never decremented its weight. The tracker now falls back to the local `spoolman_slot_assignments` ledger (by printer/AMS/tray) when the tag lookup misses; RFID tag-match still wins when present. (Upstream Bambuddy #1459 / d3f0e9ac.)
- **The "missing spool assignment" notification no longer false-fires on every Spoolman-mode print (#1473).** The check queried only the legacy built-in-inventory assignment table, which is empty in Spoolman mode (bindings live in `spoolman_slot_assignments`), so every correctly-bound tray was reported missing. The assigned-tray set is now the **union** of both tables, and switching back from Spoolman to internal mode now clears the Spoolman slot-assignment ledger (symmetric to the existing clear in the other direction). (Upstream Bambuddy #1473 / b06f8f69 + 305529f4.)
- **OpenSpoolman-tagged spools are assignable again (#1122).** The AMS-slot assign picker hid any Spoolman spool with a non-empty `extra.tag`, but `extra.tag` is only an RFID/NFC matching key (OpenSpoolman writes its own NFC value there) — so OpenSpoolman users' spools became un-assignable even when occupying no slot. Assignability is now decided by the slot-assignment ledger, not `extra.tag`. (Upstream Bambuddy #1122 / e1a236e4.)
- **A reassigned non-RFID slot no longer shows a stale spool in the hover card (#1457).** Non-RFID slots use a deterministic per-slot fallback tag; on re-binding, the previous spool's `extra.tag` was never cleared, so it lingered in the AMS hover card's spool-ID block. Assign and Link now clear that tag from any other spool still claiming it (best-effort), and the hover card's fill-level resolver now prefers the explicit slot assignment over the (possibly stale) tag-link. (Upstream Bambuddy #1457 / 12b0c138.)
- **Archive filament colour now follows the assigned spool, not the slicer's 3MF.** An archive's colour was read verbatim from the print's 3MF (the slicer's per-slot colour, picked independently of the exact hex you curate on the inventory spool), so a print from a `#000000` spool could show the slicer's near-black `#161616` on the card and in the Color Distribution graph. Once usage tracking resolves the print's slots to inventory spools, their curated colours are now authoritative — applied all-or-nothing (only when every used slot matched a coloured spool) for both built-in inventory and Spoolman modes. (Upstream Bambuddy #1494 / ab8e0761.)
- **Grouped inventory rows now show group totals.** A collapsed group of spools displayed one member's remaining weight as the headline; it now sums remaining weight (and, in the table view, used/label/core weight) across every spool in the group. (Upstream Bambuddy #1368 / 7aad3fb3.)
- **FilamentColors.xyz sync identifies honestly.** The colour-catalog sync now sends a `BamDude/<version>` User-Agent instead of leaking httpx's default `python-httpx/x.y`, consistent with every other outbound client. (Upstream Bambuddy 3286ccd7.)
- **P2S camera stream no longer freezes on the first frame.** P2S firmware `01.02.00.00` sends an RTSP stream whose RTP timestamps don't advance, so ffmpeg's constant-frame-rate conversion treated every frame after the first as a duplicate and dropped it (the stream showed one frame, then froze). The P2S camera profile now adds `-use_wallclock_as_timestamps 1` so ffmpeg rebuilds each frame's timestamp from arrival time; X1/H2/A1 are unaffected. (Upstream Bambuddy #1395 / 6d7a92c0.)
- **Camera live-view works across all ffmpeg versions again.** The RTSP socket-timeout flag is now probed from ffmpeg at runtime instead of hard-coded: modern ffmpeg (5/6/7, current Debian/Ubuntu/Homebrew) uses `-timeout`, while transitional builds (Ubuntu 22.04-era) need `-stimeout` — hard-coding either one broke the other half of installs (a wrong `-timeout` there silently implied `-listen` and failed with `EADDRINUSE`). (Upstream Bambuddy #1504 / eae96da5.)
- **The camera diagnostic is now reachable in window mode.** The stethoscope "Diagnose" control (and the Diagnose button in the stream-error state) shipped only in the embedded camera viewer; the standalone camera window (`/camera/{id}`, the default view mode) never got it, so window-mode users couldn't self-diagnose a dead stream. (Upstream Bambuddy #1395 / 51b0d28b.)
- **ffmpeg's reason for a stalled camera now reaches the logs.** A stalled-but-alive ffmpeg (the P2S RTSP failure mode) never closes its stderr, so the previous read-to-EOF discarded the banner + stream-analysis lines it had already printed and the support bundle went dark. ffmpeg stderr is now drained incrementally, and the resolved per-model probesize / full ffmpeg argv (camera URL redacted) are logged — making the next "camera won't start" bundle diagnosable. (Upstream Bambuddy #1395 / 056f06a3.)
- **A wrong-cased printer serial no longer connects to silence.** Bambu serials are uppercase and the MQTT report topic `device/<serial>/report` is case-sensitive, so a serial typed in the wrong case (or with stray whitespace) connected and subscribed fine but never received a single status report — every field stayed "unknown". The serial is now normalised (trimmed + uppercased) on save, and a connection that keeps going stale without ever receiving a report logs a one-shot hint pointing at the serial number instead of looping silently. (Upstream Bambuddy #1465 / 01787eb1.)
- **BamDude behind Cloudflare no longer needs `unsafe-inline` to load.** Cloudflare injects a bot-detection script whose hash rotates per request, so it could never be allowlisted under the strict `script-src 'self'` CSP. The SPA policy now also stamps a fresh per-request `nonce` — Cloudflare clones that nonce onto its injected script so it passes, without weakening the policy (`'self'` stays, no `'unsafe-inline'`). Also `/manifest.json`, `/sw.js` and `/sw-register.js` now answer `HEAD` (uptime probes / `curl -I` were getting 405). (Upstream Bambuddy #1460 follow-up.)
- **Re-slicing an archive for a different printer no longer mislabels it with the source printer.** A cross-model re-slice inherited the source archive's `printer_id`, so the new archive card and reprint modal showed the original printer's name; the id is now cleared on a cross-model re-slice (the card falls back to the sliced-for model). (Upstream Bambuddy c0c07bc5.)
- **"Prefer Lowest Remaining Filament" now uses your inventory weight, not just the printer's RFID counter.** The dispatch sort read only the MQTT `remain` value, which is meaningful only for Bambu RFID spools (non-RFID spools report `-1`, so multiple trays tied and the lowest AMS slot always won) and is the printer's counter rather than BamDude's `label_weight − weight_used` (or Spoolman's `remaining_weight`). When a slot is bound to an inventory spool, that tracked remaining is now the sort signal — inventory-tracked spools are preferred over untracked ones, then by ascending remaining. Works in both internal-inventory and Spoolman modes; un-bound slots keep the old MQTT behaviour. (Upstream Bambuddy #1508.)
- **The 3D preview no longer freezes the page on complex multi-part 3MFs.** Parsing a large, many-component model (e.g. a multi-colour "parted" MakerWorld statue) ran as one long synchronous burst that locked the UI — including the preview's own close button — until it finished. The parser now yields to the browser periodically so the modal stays responsive. (Upstream Bambuddy #1412.)
- **The library 3D preview's slice button now respects the Slicer API setting.** With in-app slicing enabled, the preview opened the *external* slicer while the file-row action used the in-app Slice dialog; the preview now offers the same in-app "Slice" action for sliceable files. (Upstream Bambuddy #1325 follow-up.)
- **Library files now show their filename, not the embedded 3MF title.** A file imported from Bambu Studio or MakerWorld would display its in-app project title (often the generic "Exported 3D Model") on the card, in search and in sort, instead of the filename you saved it as — fixable only by a rename round-trip. All import paths (upload, ZIP, external-folder scan, MakerWorld, slice output) now drop the embedded title on the way in, and a one-time migration clears it from files imported before the fix. Print archives still keep their real print name. (Upstream Bambuddy #1489.)
- **The File Manager "All Files" view can now show files inside subfolders.** Upstream's #1499 fix made "All Files" always recurse into subfolders (a library where every file lived in a subfolder appeared empty before). In BamDude this is now controlled by a new **"Show all files recursively"** toggle (Settings → General → File Manager, default **off**) — off keeps the original root-only listing, on lists every file across all subfolders. (Upstream Bambuddy #1499, made configurable.)
- **AMS drying no longer powers the printer off seconds after it starts.** A tray-only AMS status update (which carries no drying fields) was dropping the remaining `dry_time` during the state merge; the drying-complete detector then read the absent value as 0, saw a false "60 → 0" finish, and — for anyone using the new smart-plug auto-off-after-drying — cut power mid-cycle. The merge now preserves `dry_time`/humidity/temp across tray-only updates, and the completion detector only fires on an explicit `dry_time` reading. (Upstream Bambuddy #1462.)
- **A malformed MQTT PUBLISH from a Bambu printer no longer kills the connection.** The printer's broker occasionally delivers a truncated packet whose declared topic length exceeds the bytes present; paho-mqtt would build a negative `struct` count and crash its network thread with `struct.error: bad char in struct format`, dropping the printer offline until the ~70 s stale-connection watchdog reconnected. BamDude now drops the malformed push (the printer re-pushes its state within seconds), mirroring the guard already in place for malformed SUBACK packets.

### Changed

- **A failed slice now shows a dismissible dialog with the slicer's reason** instead of a toast that auto-vanished before you could read it. (Upstream Bambuddy #1493.)
- **File library multi-select now toggles only when you click the checkbox**, not anywhere on the card or row. Clicking a card/row body no longer flips its selection (which made it easy to select files by accident while scanning the library); the checkbox is the single select affordance, matching the printers page. Applies to both grid and list views.

### Security

- **Pinned `starlette>=1.0.1` and `idna>=3.15`** against PYSEC-2026-161 (Starlette) and CVE-2026-45409 (idna ReDoS) — both are transitive dependencies that were resolving to the vulnerable versions. (Upstream Bambuddy security pins.)

## [0.4.5b4] - 2026-05-21

Fourth beta of the 0.4.5 cycle. Completes the Filament Calibration Wave 2 roadmap — Flow Rate plus the Temperature / Retraction / VFA / Max-Volumetric-Speed towers all run end-to-end as real calibration prints — and folds in a large batch of upstream Bambuddy ports (the v0.2.4→0.2.4.2 audit): smart-plug auto-off after drying, an in-app camera diagnostic, manual LDAP provisioning, Spoolman parity fixes, MQTT / FTP / firmware robustness, and an A1 / A1-mini bed-jog safety fix. Plus archive & statistics polish — consistent failure-status accounting, distinct cancelled/aborted badges, and per-print vs per-object filament averages. Image: `ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.5b4` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.5b4`. Pin the exact tag — `:latest` still tracks 0.4.4.1.

### Added

- **Filament Calibration Wave 2 complete — Flow Rate plus the Temperature, Retraction, VFA and Max-Volumetric-Speed towers all run as real calibration prints.** Each slices through your sidecar, prints on the printer, and saves the picked result; tower tests are recorded as inert per-(filament, printer, nozzle) farm records via the finish-step calculator.
- **Edit Archive dialog: two new failure reasons ("Swap Mode error", "Printer error") and a free-text "Error details" field** for failed / cancelled prints (writes the archive's verbose `error_message`). en + uk.
- **Filament Trends: separate "Avg per print" and "Avg per object" summary cards.** The previous single average divided by object count, under-reporting per-job filament on plates printed in quantity; the two metrics are now distinct. en + uk.
- **Smart-plug auto-off after AMS drying completes** — a per-plug toggle (default 10-minute delay) independent of the print-finish auto-off, fired on any drying cycle (queue / ambient / manual).
- **In-app camera diagnostic.** A "Diagnose" button on the camera viewer runs a staged reachability + first-frame check and shows which stage failed plus a translated remediation hint, instead of leaving you to open a support ticket.
- **Virtual-printer system print-option defaults.** Set per-model fallback flags (timelapse, bed levelling, …) once under Settings → Print → Saved Print Profiles; a slicer that omits a flag inherits the system default instead of the bare column default (an explicit slicer-sent value still wins).
- **Manual LDAP user provisioning.** An "LDAP" tab in the create-user modal lets admins search the directory and onboard users even with auto-provision off.
- **Inventory: "Storage Location" filter chip** to narrow the spool list to a physical location.
- **Inventory: spool database ID shown** in the edit-modal header and the AMS slot hover card.
- **Inventory: "Reset usage to 0"** per-spool and bulk eraser, so the consumed counter reads lifetime-since-reset (internal + Spoolman).
- **Label printing: AMS-holder template split into two correct sizes (74×33 + 75×55 mm)**, plus a "By colour" sort for rainbow-ordered label sheets.
- **Per-printer "queue completed" notification** that fires (and names the printer) the moment one printer drains its queue — on by default across all transports.
- **Self-signed CA certificate support in Docker** via opt-in `USE_SYSTEM_TRUST_STORE` for local HTTPS endpoints (e.g. Home Assistant).
- **Scoped `POST /settings/electricity-price` API endpoint** for Home-Assistant dynamic-tariff integrations (opt-in per API key) instead of granting full settings write.
- **Slice modal: the build-plate choice is applied to the resolved process profile**, fixing "Cool Plate does not support filament" when slicing onto a preset with a different default plate.
- **Sliced library STLs now carry a preview image** (the source STL's render is injected into the resulting 3MF).

### Changed

- **Settings → Spool Catalog renders identically in internal and Spoolman modes** (drops the confusing inline filament editor and a noisy console 400).
- **Single-colour prints auto-map to the spool already loaded in the extruder** instead of forcing an AMS swap to slot 0; filament type still gates, multi-colour keeps full type+colour matching.
- **Print progress bars share one colour scheme everywhere** — green running, amber paused — across the printers, queue and project pages.
- **Paused prints read at a glance** — a pause glyph over a dimmed cover on printer and queue cards, amber progress bar.
- **Pause chip drops the useless "Unknown" label** for an unclassified pause, showing "Paused" instead.
- **A maintenance-due Telegram notification clears itself** once each due item is handled.

### Fixed

- **Statistics count cancelled and stopped prints as failures consistently** — the Success Rate widget, the Failure Analysis widget and the "Failed prints" filter now share one failure set (failed / aborted / cancelled / stopped).
- **Cancelled and aborted prints show distinct status badges** (`aborted` → "aborted" / «перервано»; `cancelled` keeps "cancelled" / «скасовано»).
- **Failure Analysis widget honours the selected timeframe and excludes trashed prints** — "All time" now covers all history (was hard-pinned to the last 30 days) and the empty-state message reflects the actual selection.
- **Stats widgets no longer count prints moved to trash**, so the charts agree with the headline totals.
- **Update checks back off when GitHub rate-limits us** instead of hammering the API every poll.
- **"Scan for timelapse" no longer attaches the wrong video** (matches on print start time, asks when ambiguous), and no longer stays greyed out for queue / reprint prints.
- **Trashing an archive cancels its still-pending queue items** and the queue stops serving a dead thumbnail for them.
- **Archive cards reveal the full print name on hover** when it is clipped.
- **Multi-colour prints no longer report near-zero cost** when only some AMS trays are mapped to inventory spools (untracked filament tops up at the default rate).
- **Raw `.gcode` and non-zip `.3mf` uploads reject at upload time** with a clear message, not 30 s into the print.
- **Support bundle no longer leaks the `mqtt_broker` host**, and now records slicer-sidecar reachability + versions and feature-table diagnostics; bundle import errors log the sidecar's reject reason.
- **"Open in Slicer" URLs work** for spools whose name lacks `.3mf` or contains URL-confusing characters.
- **External-folder scan no longer hangs on large NAS mounts**, and STL thumbnail generation stopped crashing (mesh thumbnails backfill in the background).
- **Docker STL thumbnails stop logging "Permission denied"** and re-scanning fonts every upload (`MPLCONFIGDIR` pinned).
- **Spoolman: non-Bambu spools get weight updates** (per-print 3MF tracking is the weight writer); linking a spool to an AMS-HT (H2C / H2D) slot no longer fails the DB constraint; search by spool ID, the Unassign button and modal stacking all work again; "Print labels…" works in Spoolman mode.
- **Spoolman: external-spool prints no longer debit the AMS-slot-0 spool**; Bambu RFID spools auto-create with Bambu names (not the first competitor match); editing a Color Name saves without minting duplicate filaments.
- **AMS: spool removal applies on X1C firmware that reports `power_on_flag=False` while idle** (no manual Reconnect), and physically-empty slots / "Reset Slot with spool inserted" configure cleanly on A1 Mini BMCU / P1S Standard AMS.
- **Assign Spool: AMS slots configure cleanly for spools with no K-profile**, and local-preset spools resolve to specific filament rows.
- **Add/Edit Spool: hex colour can be typed character-by-character again**; catalog swatches apply gradient + effect; CF / GF subtypes appear in the dropdown; `storage_location` saves and shows.
- **Smart-plug live wattage rounds to a whole number** on the printer card.
- **Plate-detection uses the external camera** when one is configured (no more permanent "Build plate not empty" on A1 + RTSP).
- **Virtual Printer: AMS state no longer disappears on P1S / A1** between full pushalls; clicking Print (not Send) no longer wedges the slicer at "Downloading 0%"; queue mode inherits slicer print options (timelapse, bed levelling, …) instead of model defaults.
- **MQTT: external-spool `ams_filament_setting` uses the global tray id (254)** so the filament identity applies on no-AMS printers.
- **Per-printer queue: an aborted / cancelled print pauses the queue** instead of dispatching the next item.
- **Create User form rejects weak passwords with a useful message** mirroring the real complexity rules.
- **LDAP login no longer wipes manually-assigned groups** outside the configured mapping.
- **Deleting an SSO / 2FA user no longer leaves orphan auth rows** on SQLite.
- **OIDC provider icons render on the login page** (proxied server-side under the strict CSP).
- **Discord provider accepts the legacy `discordapp.com` webhook host.**
- **Firmware update dialog survives Cloudflare-blocked outages** on bambulab.com with an honest message.
- **P2S camera RTSP stream locks** instead of timing out (per-model ffmpeg tuning), and the live stream no longer freezes when Obico or the snapshot endpoint pulls a frame on the same printer.
- **Layer timelapse starts for queue / VP-dispatched prints**, not just slice-and-send.
- **FTP upload no longer treats a 426 as success on truncated files**, while transient 426s on intact files pass (SIZE-verified).
- **H2S with no AMS can start a print again** (firmware format split from dual-nozzle routing).
- **Queue items no longer get stuck in `printing`** when the printer was in FINISH at dispatch time.
- **Restarting BamDude mid-print no longer fires a duplicate print-start**, and a print that finishes while BamDude is stopped is reconciled on restart.
- **Safety: bed-jog Z direction fixed on A1 / A1 Mini** — "Up" no longer drives the toolhead into the bed. If you ran an earlier 0.4.x build, avoid the printer-card bed-jog buttons until upgrading.
- **Git backup refuses to save against a non-private repository** (credentials-leak guard).
- **Home Assistant smart-plug entity dropdown only offers entities the schema accepts**, even while searching.
- **MQTT robustness:** the thread survives a malformed SUBACK (no ~70 s blackout); a print that finishes during an MQTT stale window is recognised as complete; "Clear plate" no longer 400s right after a reconnect; the first dispatch to a stale-prone P1S no longer fails.
- **Telegram "✅ Done", speed-mode and queue-move buttons no longer crash** when redrawing the menu.
- **Queue-completed notification fires when the last queued print finishes**, and "Power off when done" actually powers off after a queued print.
- **Webhook API endpoints fixed** (status reads + the start endpoint that never uploaded the file).
- **Dependencies:** `urllib3` floor-pinned to ≥2.7.0 (CVE fixes); a `JWT_SECRET_KEY` shorter than 32 chars is rejected at startup; `ws` / `brace-expansion` transitive bumps.

---

## [0.4.5b3] - 2026-05-16

Third beta of the 0.4.5 cycle. The Filament Calibration wizard's Wave 1/2 lands — PA Tower, PA Pattern and PA Line now run end-to-end as real calibration prints (Python ports of the BambuStudio generators), with per-mode preset overrides applied through the slicer sidecar. Plus a K-profile data-layer pass (external-spool pre-print bind, in-place sync, BS-parity edit/delete, hard-prune of printer-side deletes), per-printer queue pause that works mid-print, an auto-queue lifecycle + stats cleanup, and honest client identification on every Bambu Lab / MakerWorld call. Image: `ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.5b3` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.5b3`. Pin the exact tag — `:latest` still tracks 0.4.4.1.

### Added

- **Add-to-queue dialog hides printers whose queue is paused.** Paired with the queue pause feature — a paused queue refuses new items, so the "Add to queue" modal's printer picker now drops any printer whose queue is paused (the backend would `409` the add anyway). Only the add-to-queue mode filters; reprint / edit-queue-item are untouched.

- **Per-printer queue pause/resume — works even while a print is running (m067).** A printer's queue could only be paused when idle: the pause/resume control was hidden during a print, and the API rejected pausing a `printing` queue outright. The block was structural — `PrinterQueue.status` is a single field the scheduler reads as the authoritative "printer busy" marker, so it couldn't also hold a pause state. New `PrinterQueue.is_paused` flag is orthogonal to `status`: a queue can be `printing` and `is_paused` at once. Pausing a queue now (a) leaves any running print completely alone — it finishes normally — (b) stops the next item from dispatching, (c) refuses new items being added (manual add returns `409`, and the auto-queue scheduler skips the printer entirely so it can't route work there either). The queue card's pause/resume button is now shown in every state with a "Paused" pill when active; resume also clears a status-level `paused`/`error` state in the same click. Migration m067 adds the column.

- **Auto-queue panel shows archive-backed totals.** The auto-queue panel listed only pending router items — there was no at-a-glance "how have auto-queue prints been doing" the way every per-printer queue card has a `done · failed · cancelled` footer. New `GET /auto-queue/stats` counts `print_archives` flagged `from_auto_queue` and folds `aborted` / `stopped` into `cancelled`, mirroring `get_queue_terminal_counts` exactly. The panel renders a footer line (`done · failed · cancelled`) once at least one auto-queue print has finished. Because the `auto_queue_items` row is deleted when its print finishes, this count comes entirely from the archive flag — the panel and the per-printer queue cards now read their history from the same source.

- **Auto-queue print provenance on archives (`print_archives.from_auto_queue`, m066).** An `auto_queue_items` row is a pre-dispatch *router* — once the `AutoQueueScheduler` copies it into a printer's `print_queue` and that print runs, the auto row carries nothing more. It used to linger forever as a `status='assigned'` row with an `assigned_to_item_id` that dangled the moment the completed `print_queue` item was auto-cleaned (`foreign_key_check` surfaced 7 such orphans). New lifecycle: when a `print_queue` item is deleted, its source `auto_queue_items` row is deleted with it (`detach_print_queue_refs` helper, wired into all three `print_queue` deletion sites — `on_print_complete` auto-cleanup, the manual `DELETE /queue/{id}` route, and per-user bulk delete). The lasting record moves to the archive: every print the scheduler dispatched is now stamped `from_auto_queue=True` at archive-creation time (from the queue item's `source_auto_item_id`). This lets the auto-queue view compute archive-backed completed / failed totals the same way per-printer queues already do via `get_queue_terminal_counts` — a simple stats endpoint + UI can land on top without further schema work. `calibration_session` rows referencing a deleted `print_queue` item get their `print_queue_item_id` nulled instead of deleted — a calibration's session history outlives its queue item. All cleanup is done with explicit `DELETE` / `UPDATE` rather than `ON DELETE` clauses because SQLite runs with `foreign_keys=OFF`. Migration m066 adds the column, backfills `from_auto_queue` for archives produced by an auto-queue print that's still traceable through a surviving non-pending `print_queue` row carrying `source_auto_item_id` (pending rows are skipped — `print_queue.archive_id` only points at the produced archive from dispatch onward; completed auto-prints lost their `print_queue` row on completion and are unrecoverable, so the backfill is best-effort and the flag is authoritative only for prints dispatched under the new code), and deletes the orphaned `auto_queue_items` backlog — rows whose `assigned_to_item_id` dangles because the `print_queue` row is gone. Auto rows whose `print_queue` item still exists are left alone: the operator deleting that queue item triggers the same `detach_print_queue_refs` cleanup, so the one-time migration never removes a row the user can still act on.

- **K-profile name is editable on the Profiles page (Bambu Studio parity).** The edit modal used to lock the profile-name field — operators could fix the K value but not relabel a slot. Field is now editable both on add and on edit. The DB row keeps its identity through the rename because the K-profile cache sync (see Fixed) now matches by combo `(printer, filament_id, nozzle, vol_type, extruder)` and mirrors `name` + `pa_k_value` onto the matched row — so attached notes (m065) stay linked across renames.

- **K-profile delete sends one BS-parity `extrusion_cali_del` shape for every printer.** `delete_kprofile` branched on a serial-prefix dual-nozzle guess: H2D / X2D / H2C got `extruder_id · nozzle_id · filament_id · cali_idx · nozzle_diameter`, while non-dual (X1 / P1 / A1 / P2S) got the same plus a `setting_id` field. BambuStudio's `command_delete_pa_calibration` (DeviceManager.cpp:1905) sends one fixed payload for all models — the dual-nozzle field set, no `setting_id`. BamDude now matches: single shape, no branch, no `setting_id`. (BS additionally appends `nozzle_pos` / `nozzle_sn` when an H2D nozzle rack is mounted — BamDude doesn't wire the nozzle-rack feature anywhere yet, so those stay omitted.)

- **K-profile edit is now a single in-place `extrusion_cali_set`, not delete + add (BS parity).** The non-H2D edit path used to publish `extrusion_cali_del` → `await asyncio.sleep(0.5)` → `extrusion_cali_set` with a fresh `setting_id` — which left a noticeable window where the slot didn't exist on the printer and a fresh `setting_id` broke any reference (e.g. tracked auto-cali history). BambuStudio's actual edit path (CaliHistoryDialog.cpp:474-493 → CalibUtils::set_PA_calib_result → DeviceManager.cpp:1864 `command_set_pa_calibration`) ships a single `extrusion_cali_set` carrying the original `cali_idx` + preserved `setting_id`; firmware on every Bambu printer (X1 / P1 / A1 / A1 Mini / H2D / H2C / X2D) handles it as an in-place tray update via `ExtrusionCalibSetParse` (DevCalib.cpp:151). BamDude now does the same: one `set_kprofile` call regardless of model, with `cali_idx=profile.slot_id` + `setting_id=profile.setting_id`. Faster save round-trip, no transient missing-slot window, no `setting_id` drift. The H2D-only branch and the post-delete sleep are gone.

- **Honest BamDude identification on every Bambu Lab / MakerWorld / Wiki call.** Aligns with Bambu Lab's 2026-05-12 cloud-access statement, which separates modifying AGPL code (allowed) from "impersonating official clients in communication with our cloud infrastructure" (not allowed). The main authenticated cloud path was already honest (it identified as `Bambuddy/1.0` — a leftover from before the BamDude rebrand). All four call sites now consistently send `BamDude/<APP_VERSION> (+https://github.com/kainpl/bamdude)` — the URL in parens makes the source unambiguous if Bambu ever audits the traffic. Specifically: `bambu_cloud.py:_get_headers` rebranded from `Bambuddy/1.0` to BamDude; `bambu_cloud.py:verify_totp` no longer spoofs Chrome UA + `Origin: bambulab.com` + `Referer: bambulab.com/` + `Accept-Language` (verified upstream that plain UA hits HTTP 400 with the expected application-level error, no Cloudflare interstitial); `makerworld.py:_CLIENT_HEADERS` rebranded from Firefox 147 (the `Referer: https://makerworld.com/` header stays — MakerWorld's CSRF / origin-check middleware uses it functionally on some endpoints, that's protocol, not identity); `firmware_check.py:_client` rebranded from Chrome 120 on `wiki.bambulab.com`. The separate `_cf_client` (curl_cffi Chrome120 TLS impersonation for `bambulab.com` Cloudflare-blocked firmware-CDN path) stays — that's a TLS-fingerprint workaround, not a UA spoof. Separately: the `/v1/iot-service/api/slicer/setting` endpoint's `version` query param default moved from `02.04.00.70` (an actual BS 2.4.0.70 release) to a neutral `1.0.0.0` (Bambu's server accepts any value within the `XX.YY.ZZ.WW` format — verified). New `_SLICER_API_VERSION` constant in `bambu_cloud.py` is re-exported into `routes/cloud.py` so the two route defaults stay in sync. Two regression tests rewritten (`test_verify_totp_uses_honest_bamdude_user_agent`, `test_sends_honest_bamdude_user_agent`) to lock in the contract — UA must start with `BamDude/`, must NOT contain Mozilla/Chrome/Firefox/Origin/Referer (Referer-on-MakerWorld stays per spec).

- **Cancel-calibration resets the wizard in place + correct mode label.** Discarding a half-finished session from the Resume banner used to leave the banner visible until a manual refresh (the cancel mutation invalidated the wrong React-Query key — `['calibration', 'awaiting', printerId]` while the query actually lives under `['calibration', 'active', printerId]`). Now the banner clears optimistically (active-sessions query data set to `[]`), the wizard's step + input + saved-rows + error state reset to a clean start, and the same fixed key is used for all four invalidation call sites (start / submit-manual / submit-auto / cancel). Plus the banner itself: it was hardcoded to render every non-flow-rate session as "PA Line" via a literal `bodyPaLine` translation key; now uses a generic `body` template + a new `modeShort` i18n namespace so a half-finished PA Pattern shows "PA Pattern", PA Tower shows "PA Tower", etc.

- **Manual-save dialog for PA Line + PA Pattern: direct K input.** Replaces the row-index dropdown (which silently used BS's hardcoded `0.0 / 0.002 / 50` defaults so any operator-picked sweep range yielded the wrong K). The dialog now shows a single number field — operator reads the K label printed next to the cleanest row (PA Line) or column (PA Pattern) and types it in. PA Tower keeps the measured-height-mm + `K = Start + Step × height` formula path. Backend `ManualResultIn` carries either `pa_k_value` (direct K from the operator's reading) or `best_line_index` (PA Tower's height-to-K formula) — both are accepted.

- **PA Line goes production (W2 Phase 9 complete).** PA Line picks now run end-to-end through BamDude. The full BS pattern (vertical prime line + N rows of slow / fast / slow extrusion segments per stepped K + optional filled glyph box with K-value labels next to every other row) is regenerated on every call via a Python port of `BambuStudio Calib.cpp::CalibPressureAdvanceLine::print_pa_lines` (lines 415-490). Reuses the PA Pattern glyph + line + box primitives — the only PA-Line-specific addition was the `Left_To_Right` digit mode (mirrors BS Calib.cpp:82-101). Pattern centres on the operator's real bed bbox (read from the resolved printer preset's `printable_area` when present, with a printer-model → bbox fallback for the common case of cloud-preset deltas that don't carry the field — so an A1 mini's 180×180 plate gets the pattern at `(38, 62)` instead of the BS-formula's off-centre 256-mm-bed assumption). The 3×3×0.2 mm cube placeholder is anchored to the right edge of the glyph tab at its bottom row, so it tracks the centred layout for every bed size and never collides with the K rows. Per-mode preset overrides (`apply_pa_line_process/printer/filament_overrides`) pin the placeholder cube to 1 wall / 0 shells / 0% infill and mute retract / wipe / resonance-avoidance the same way PA Pattern does (uninterrupted G1 trail = readable K bands). Wizard defaults match PA Pattern (0.0 → 0.08 step 0.005 = 17 K rows) instead of BS's `0.1/0.002` since 0.1 is too aggressive for direct-drive Bambu printers in practice; operator can widen via the wizard inputs. `MODE_STATE[PA_LINE] = PRODUCTION`.

- **PA Pattern goes production (W2 Phase 2 complete).** PA Pattern picks now run end-to-end through BamDude. The comb-and-digit pattern's full G-code (concentric box frame, K-numbered glyph tab, per-pattern V-shaped walls at each K) is regenerated on every call from the operator's `start / end / step` via a faithful Python port of `BambuStudio Calib.cpp::CalibPressureAdvancePattern::generate_custom_gcodes` — all 7-segment glyph drawing (`draw_digit` / `draw_number`), single-line extrusion math (`draw_line` with `Flow::mm3_per_mm` cross-section), concentric perimeters + 45° hatched fill (`draw_box`), pattern orchestration (4 layers, `get_num_patterns = ceil((end - start)/step + 1)` patterns per row, layer-0 layout shrink, K-glyphs on every other pattern at layer 1) ported 1-to-1 from BS source. Math validated against the BS-shipped scaffold (frame `84.08 × 42.43mm`, tab `84.08 × 13.5mm` — matches the shipped XML's G1 endpoints). M900 K commands carry the Bambu `L1000 M10` suffix on Bambu printers (per `GCodeWriter.cpp:354`). Verification diff vs Orca-desktop's saved project: same 17 K values per layer, same K-injection order before each pattern's V walls, same frame + tab dimensions (within float-print noise); minor differences in absolute Z (BS-spec 0.25 first layer vs Orca's preset 0.20) and starting_point coords (Orca uses `handle_spacing=1.2`, BS=2) — semantically equivalent, structurally correct.

- **Per-mode preset overrides applied via sidecar `--load-settings` (PA Tower + PA Pattern).** Previously our process/filament/printer hardcodes were written into the 3MF's `Metadata/project_settings.config` and got overridden by the sidecar's `--load-settings` (operator's preset values won — sliced CONFIG_BLOCK showed preset's `wall_loops=4`, `initial_layer_speed=['50']` instead of our pinned `3` / `30`). New `backend/app/services/calib_preset_overrides.py` patches the preset JSONs in-memory before the sidecar receives them — mirrors what BS desktop's `Plater::_calib_*` does on the active preset bundle. For PA Pattern: `wall_loops=3`, `skirt_loops=0`, `brim_type=no_brim`, `enable_wrapping_detection=0`, `print_sequence=by layer`, `initial_layer_speed=[30]`, `line_width=nozzle*1.125`, `initial_layer_line_width=nozzle*1.4`, `initial_layer_print_height=0.25`, `layer_height=0.2` (process); `filament_retract_when_changing_layer=[0]`, `filament_wipe=[0]` (filament); `wipe=[0]`, `retract_when_changing_layer=[0]`, `resonance_avoidance=0` (printer). For PA Tower: `enable_wrapping_detection=0` (process), `slow_down_layer_time=[1]` (filament). Verified end-to-end via the sliced gcode's CONFIG_BLOCK — every override is in the final gcode the printer runs.

- **PA Pattern cube repositioned (out of pattern V wall zone).** BS-shipped `pa_pattern.3mf` parks the 5×5×0.85mm cube placeholder at bed centre `(82.79, 86.07)` — squarely on top of the pattern's V wall column. Build-item transform now patches the translate to `(51.633841, 83.5, 0.4)` to match what Orca-desktop's PA Pattern wizard saves: cube sits in upper-left of the pattern frame, V walls draw on bare bed without colliding with the cube's perimeters. Scale stays at BS-shipped `(0.278, 0.278, 0.047)` → cube remains 5×5×0.85mm. Per-object overrides for PA Pattern dropped entirely (was 8 keys) — preset's solid-cube defaults (top/bottom_shells=4, infill=40%) match Orca's saved project; pattern's G1 trail printsреально through `Metadata/custom_gcode_per_layer.xml` regardless of cube's own slice settings.

- **`_compose_from_3mf` patched to support targeted overrides without erasing scaffold metadata.** Previously the 3MF pass-through path always overwrote `Metadata/custom_gcode_per_layer.xml` (even when the caller passed an empty list — which would have wiped BS's pre-baked PA Pattern comb), and lacked a way to reposition the cube. Now: skips the custom_gcode rewrite when the caller's list is empty (PA Tower production mode always passes a non-empty M900 K sweep so unchanged there), and accepts `build_transform_scale` + `build_transform_translate` so per-mode builders can position scaffold geometry without rewriting the whole 3D/3dmodel.model.

- **PA Tower flips from verification → production (W2 Phase 1 complete).** Picking PA Tower in the wizard now slices through your sidecar, FTPs the resulting `.gcode.3mf` to the printer, runs it as a real calibration print, and surfaces a manual-save dialog when finished. K-factor is computed via the Orca-wiki formula `K = Start + (Step × measured_height_mm)` — enter the height (in mm) on the tower where the corners look cleanest, BamDude does the arithmetic, saves to `filament_calibration`, fires `extrusion_cali_set` + `extrusion_cali_sel` to write the printer's 16-slot K-profile history and bind it to the AMS slot. Per-object overrides ported 1-to-1 from Orca's saved PA Tower project (hollow tower, `seam_position=back`, `brim_ears` for Orca / `outer_only` for BS, no top/bottom shells, no infill), project-level `enable_wrapping_detection=0` + `slow_down_layer_time=1` mirror BS `Plater.cpp::_calib_pa_tower`. Manual-save UI is mode-aware — PA Line / PA Pattern keep the "pick the cleanest line/corner" picker, PA Tower gets the measured-height number input.
