- **Reprinting a file from the printer screen no longer overwrites prior history.** When a user reprinted a previously-completed file from the printer's own screen (across one or many printers), `on_print_start`'s post-download content-hash adoption block was matching the historical archive by `content_hash` *regardless of status* and flipping it from `completed` (or any terminal status) back to `printing` — clearing `completed_at`, deleting the original print's outcome, and never creating a new archive row for the new run. Doing this on N printers at once destroyed N rows of history. Adoption now requires `status="printing" AND completed_at IS NULL` — terminal rows are left alone, and the new print gets its own fresh archive. Mid-print recovery (BamDude restart while a print was actively running) keeps working because the in-flight row's status is still `printing`.
- **Cross-printer race when multiple printers download the same-named 3MF concurrently.** When a user kicked off the same file on two printers from the slicer at once, both `on_print_start` flows raced into the same shared temp path (`archive_files/temp/<filename>.gcode.3mf`) — both opened it with `"wb"`, truncated each other's output, and the resulting bytes were a sparse / corrupt 3MF. On Linux this typically produced two archives both pointing at garbage; on Windows the second `open` failed with a sharing violation and that printer fell through to the no-3MF fallback. Fix: temp downloads now go into a per-printer subdir (`archive_files/temp/<printer_id>/<filename>.gcode.3mf`) so two printers can't collide on the same path. `archive_download_retry` and the on-demand `try_download_3mf` helper both pick this up automatically — no caller changes needed.
- **Library trash hard-delete + sweeper now explicitly NULL out the FK on related archive rows on SQLite.** `print_archives.library_file_id` is declared `ON DELETE SET NULL` in the model + every migration that touches it, but SQLite only enforces FK actions when `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` is set on each connection — which BamDude deliberately does not (turning it on globally would require a separate audit of every other `ON DELETE` clause in the schema). On Postgres the FK fired correctly; on SQLite installs (the default) two of the four library hard-delete paths — `library_trash_service._sweep` (after retention elapses) and `library_trash_service.hard_delete_now` ("Empty trash" / "Delete now") — left related archive rows with **stale `library_file_id` pointers** to now-deleted library rows. The user-visible symptom is subtle (later joins return NULL, the cleanup library-skip rule still treats the design as "library has it gone" and proceeds correctly), but the inconsistency would have surfaced any time something assumed `library_file_id IS NULL ⇔ never had a library link`. The two route-level library-delete paths (`api/routes/library.py` external-file delete + bulk delete) already did an explicit `UPDATE PrintArchive SET library_file_id = NULL WHERE library_file_id IN (deleted_ids)` before the row delete; the trash sweeper + `hard_delete_now` now do the same. Behaviour now matches across both DBs and across all four entry points: archive rows survive a library hard-delete with the FK column blanked, **never cascade-deleted**.
- **Same-second same-name archive folder collision now appends a suffix.** `archive_print` and `attach_3mf_to_archive` build the archive folder name as `YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS_<stem>` — per-second granularity. In practice a single printer can't run two prints in the same second so this was a theoretical-only risk, but the previous `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` would have silently re-used a colliding folder and overwritten the existing 3MF. New code probes for an existing folder and appends `_2`, `_3`, … to the name until a fresh path is found, then `mkdir(parents=True)` without `exist_ok` so any future race fails loud.

---

## [0.4.1] - 2026-04-28

Polish release on top of `0.4.0.1`. Mostly UI-side date/time formatting consistency and small navigation conveniences.

### 0.4.1b1 — pre-release (2026-04-27)

First beta milestone of the 0.4.1 cycle. Everything listed under `[0.4.1]` is in this image (`ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.1b1` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.1b1`). Intended for early testers — pin the exact tag, don't use `:latest` yet (still points at 0.4.0.1).

### Documentation moved to its own repository

The MkDocs site source (`docs/`, `mkdocs.yml`, `overrides/`) now lives in a dedicated repository at [`kainpl/docs.bamdude.top`](https://github.com/kainpl/docs.bamdude.top) and is published at **<https://docs.bamdude.top/>**. The static-site build deps (`mkdocs`, `mkdocs-material`, `mkdocs-static-i18n`) were dropped from `requirements-dev.txt`. The application repo is now code + tests + Docker + CHANGELOG only — no docs build needed for a normal contributor checkout.

Cross-links in `README.md`, `UPDATING.md`, and `DOCKERHUB.md` now point at the published `docs.bamdude.top/...` URLs. The sidebar logo (was a localhost placeholder) targets **<https://bamdude.top/>** (landing page); the sidebar's "Docs" link targets the docs site directly.

### Date / time formatting respects system settings everywhere

Audit pass — every user-visible date or time value now follows the `time_format` (12h/24h) and `date_format` (system/us/eu/iso) preferences set under Settings → System. Previously many places fell through to the browser locale even when the user explicitly picked one of the explicit formats.

- **`utils/date.ts` helpers** — `formatDateOnly` and `formatDate` gained optional trailing `dateFormat` / `timeFormat` arguments (backward-compatible: existing 1-2-arg callers unchanged). When set to anything other than `'system'`, the explicit `MM/DD/YYYY` / `DD/MM/YYYY` / `YYYY-MM-DD` form is used and any locale-style `options` are ignored. **`formatDateTime`**: explicit `dateFormat` now wins over `options` (previous logic flipped to `toLocaleString(undefined, options)` and silently dropped the user's preference).
- **`QueueCard.tsx`** — current-print ETA now passes `timeFormat` (was rendering in browser-default 12h/24h regardless of setting).
- **`ProjectDetailPage.tsx`** — `CurrentPrintInfoCard` ETA + project due date + activity timeline timestamps all respect settings; timeline `formatTimelineDate` no longer hard-codes `dateFormat='system'`.
- **`PrintersPage.tsx`** — "Last: <name> • <date>" footer line + plate-detection reference timestamp use `dateFormat`. New `dateFormat` prop on `PrinterCard` threaded through both grid + grouped-by-location render paths.
- **`SettingsPage.tsx`** — API key `last_used` uses `dateFormat`.
- **`NotificationProviderCard.tsx`** — "last success" badge uses `dateFormat`.
- **`GitBackupSettings.tsx`** — local `formatDateTime` helper that called `date.toLocaleString()` without settings replaced with the util's settings-aware version. Backup status, scheduled run, log table, and local backup file list timestamps all follow the user's choice.
- **`NotificationLogViewer.tsx`** — log row date + tooltip full-date both threaded with `timeFormat`/`dateFormat`.
- **`LibraryFileNotesPopover.tsx`** — note `updated_at` metadata uses `formatDateTime` with settings (was raw `toLocaleString()`).
- **`CalendarView.tsx`** — per-event time in the day drawer uses `formatTimeOnly(timeFormat)` (was raw `toLocaleTimeString(undefined, ...)`). Day-name + selected-date header intentionally kept locale-driven (Intl handles full weekday/month names better than the explicit numeric forms).
- **`SpoolUsageHistory.tsx`** — usage record timestamps use `formatDateTime` with settings (was hard-coded `'en-GB'`).

Intentionally left alone: chart axis labels (`Queue/TimelineAxis`, `PrintCalendar`, `AMSHistoryModal` X-tick, `StatsPage`, `FilamentTrends`), debug log timestamps (`MQTTDebugModal`, `FailureDetectionSettings` event log) — all locale-only by design.

### Navigation

- **Project badge on archive cards is now clickable** → opens the project's detail page. Hover gets a `brightness-125` accent. `onClick stopPropagation` so the click doesn't bubble to the parent archive card and open the archive detail modal instead.

### Archive 3MF auto-cleanup

New optional retention policy for the archive directory: when a design hasn't been printed for N days, its `.3mf` file is deleted from disk. The archive row itself stays — thumbnails, filament usage, costs, notes, energy data, project links, and all other metadata are preserved. Re-printing an old design after cleanup just shows "3MF unavailable" until you re-upload from your library or slicer (same UX as fallback archives that never had a 3MF).

**Settings → Print → Archive Settings → "Auto-cleanup of stored 3MF files"** — toggle, retention input (default 30 days, minimum 1), live preview of what would be cleaned right now (`X archives in Y groups, Z MB`), last/next run status, and a "Run cleanup now" button with confirmation.

**Behaviour:**

- **Group by design hash, not per-archive row.** Reprints of the same model share a `COALESCE(source_content_hash, content_hash)` group; the cutoff is evaluated against the newest print across the whole group, so reprinting a model 3 days ago keeps every archive copy of that design — even rows from months back. When the design *is* eligible, every copy in the group has its `file_path` blanked together (consistent UI), and the unique physical file on disk is removed once.
- **Skip rules.** Skipped if any row in the group is `status='printing'`, an active queue item references it (pending / printing / paused), or the originating `library_files` row still exists with a matching hash — the library is the operator's deliberate source of truth, no need to wipe an archive copy when it's still on the source path. If any rule fires, the *whole* group is skipped.
- **Daily sweep at server-local midnight.** `archive_cleanup_service.py` runs the loop; the gate is the `archive_3mf_retention_enabled` setting, so toggling on/off takes effect without a restart. Manual "Run now" honours the same skip rules.
- **What's deleted.** The `.3mf` file + its `.gcode.md5` sidecars + the per-archive directory if it ends up empty. The thumbnail PNG and the archive row stay intact.

API: `GET /archives/cleanup/status`, `GET /archives/cleanup/preview`, `POST /archives/cleanup/run` (preview/status need `archives:read`, run needs `archives:delete_all`).

### Per-plate library file gallery

Multi-plate sliced 3MFs (`.gcode.3mf` with several `Metadata/plate_N.gcode` entries) now show a per-plate gallery in the File Manager: thumbnail strip + nav arrows + per-plate info panel (print time, weight, filament-stack breakdown, object count). Card-mode renders the gallery inline below the FileCard summary; list-mode adds a `Layers` button to the row actions that opens the same gallery in a modal. Single-plate files don't render anything extra — the existing main thumbnail already covers that case.

**Storage shape** — both `library_files.file_metadata` and `print_archives.extra_data` gain two top-level keys: `plates[]` (full per-plate breakdown — index, name, objects, object_count, has_thumbnail, print_time_seconds, filament_used_grams, filaments[], printable_objects keyed by `identify_id`, bbox_all, gcode_label_objects, exclude_object) and `is_multi_plate: bool` (computed once at upload + by the m023 backfill so the file-list / archive-list response can gate gallery rendering on the frontend without an extra fetch per single-plate file). Existing top-level fields (`print_name`, `print_time_seconds`, `printable_objects`, etc.) stay unchanged for backward compatibility — they remain a snapshot of the active plate (or plate 1 fallback).

**Endpoint behaviour** — `GET /library/files/{id}/plates` and `GET /archives/{id}/plates` now read from the cached JSON when present (no ZIP open) and only fall back to live ZIP-parse for rows that pre-date the migration. The shared `services/archive.py::parse_plates_from_3mf(zf)` helper is the single source of truth for the parse logic — used by both endpoints, the upload route, archive_print, attach_3mf_to_archive, and the m023 backfill.

**Backfill at upgrade — heads-up: long startup on first boot after this release**, similar to m022. Migration `m023` opens every existing 3MF on disk, runs the same parser, and merges the `plates[]` array + `is_multi_plate` flag into the JSON column. Expect ~50-200 ms per file. The seed step commits in batches of 100 and logs progress every batch (look for `m023 library_files: progress` / `m023 print_archives: progress` lines). Files deleted from disk are skipped silently. Idempotent — re-running a row that already has both keys is a no-op.

**MP badge** — multi-plate files now carry a small cyan `MP` badge next to the file-type badge (in both grid and list modes). Tooltip explains "Multi-plate file — multiple build plates inside" / "Мульті-плейт-файл — кілька плит для друку". Gated on the same `is_multi_plate` flag as the gallery itself.

**Per-plate object count fix** — the old logic computed `object_count` from a name-deduplicated list of `<object>` entries, so a plate with 11 instances of the same STL collapsed to `1`. The new logic prefers `len(printable_objects)` (keyed by `identify_id`, one row per instance — what the firmware actually addresses via `M623`) and only falls back to the unique-name list for source-only / unsliced 3MFs that don't carry identify_ids. Migration m023 already ran with this corrected logic; installs that ran an early build of m023 need to clear `plates` from JSON + `_migrations` row 23 to re-backfill.

### Virtual printer modes — dead-code cleanup

m002 already migrated legacy ``immediate`` / ``review`` / ``queue`` virtual-printer rows to the canonical three modes (``print_queue`` / ``file_manager`` / ``proxy``), but a lot of code paths still accepted those values, defaulted to ``immediate``, or kept dead helper methods around. This release purges the leftovers:

- **`virtual_printer/manager.py`** — dropped `_archive_file()` (the old immediate-archive path) + `_queue_file()` (old PendingUpload writer). `on_file_received` now dispatches only between `print_queue` (queue) and `file_manager` (library). Defaults updated: `configure(mode="file_manager")` and the empty-state status returns `mode="file_manager"`.
- **`api/routes/virtual_printers.py`** — `VirtualPrinterCreate.mode` default flipped to `file_manager`. Both create + update validators now accept only `print_queue` / `file_manager` / `proxy` and reject the legacy values with 400.
- **`api/routes/settings.py`** — global `virtual_printer_mode` setting validator narrowed to the same three values, default flipped to `file_manager`, and the legacy `queue→review` normaliser removed.
- **`schemas/settings.py`** — `virtual_printer_mode` Field default + description updated.
- **`api/client.ts`** — `VirtualPrinterMode` type narrowed to `'print_queue' | 'file_manager' | 'proxy'`. Inline mode types in update payloads use this type instead of duplicating the union literals.
- **Tests** — `test_virtual_printer_api.py` + `test_virtual_printer.py` rewritten: new `test_update_mode_to_file_manager`, new `test_update_mode_legacy_rejected` (asserts 400 for `immediate`/`review`/`queue`), removed assertions against the dropped helper methods, replaced legacy literals across the unit suite.

PendingUpload table + its API (`/api/v1/pending-uploads`) intentionally stay — historic rows already in the wild are still readable / approvable. The bridge from VP into PendingUpload is gone, so no new rows land there from this release onward.

### File Manager list view: subgrid layout + UI polish

The list-mode table got a structural rework so columns align uniformly across all rows + the action buttons no longer clip into adjacent columns when more buttons appear:

- **Subgrid layout.** The outer table container is now itself a CSS Grid with the full column template; the header and every row use `grid-cols-subgrid col-span-full` so they share track widths. The Actions column is therefore sized to the **widest row's** buttons (e.g. a sliced `.gcode.3mf` with project / notes / print / schedule / plate-gallery / 3D / menu = 7 buttons) — all other rows use that same width. Browser support: Chrome 117+, Firefox stable, Safari 16+.
- **Column sizes** — Type / Size / Date / Uploaded By / Actions are all `max-content` (sized to the widest row's content); Name is `minmax(0, 1fr)` (flex, with `truncate` for long names). Old hard-coded `120/100/100/140/160px` widths gone.
- **Header titles centered** in their columns.
- **Actions row right-aligned** (`justify-end`) and grows leftward into the Name column when more buttons appear, instead of overflowing into Date.
- **Layers (plate-gallery) button moved leftmost** in the actions row — most distinctive action sits where the eye scans first.
- **Hover-preview popup positioning** — anchored at the thumbnail's bottom-right 1/3 (popup extends down + to the right of that anchor) using `absolute top-2/3 left-2/3`. The outer table also dropped its `overflow-hidden` so the popup can extend beyond the table border without being clipped.
- **Archive card 3D-preview icon** — was `<Layers />`, now `<Box />`. Aligns with File Manager's 3D-preview icon and frees `Layers` to consistently mean "multi-plate" across pages. The `Layers` icon in the metadata strip (next to `total_layers`) intentionally stays — there it correctly means "print layers", not plates.

### Plate selector + gallery layout rework

Both PrintModal's plate picker (multi-plate 3MF reprint / queue flow) and the File Manager's per-plate gallery now share the same **vertical-paginator + big-card** layout: a column of small numbered buttons on the LEFT switches which plate is visible, the card on the RIGHT holds a 128×128 thumbnail + plate name + stats row (print time / weight / object count) + scrollable per-object list + per-filament breakdown beneath. Replaces the previous grid-of-plate-cards in the selector and the thumbnail-strip-with-arrows in the gallery.

- Card height locks to thumbnail-row + filament-row. Long object lists scroll inside the card body (`overflow-y-auto`) instead of pushing the dialog height open.
- Thumbnails switched from `object-cover` to `object-contain` so source images with off-center framing are no longer cropped toward the bottom.
- `LibraryPlateGallery` lost its `compact` variant — only the modal callsite remained, so it's now a single render path.

### Parallel dispatch with startup-lock

Background dispatch now runs jobs **in parallel** across printers — sending a print to two or more printers at the same time no longer queues them one-after-another. The previous full-serialization gate (one job in flight across the farm) was added to dodge a "database is locked" deadlock, but it serialized the slow FTP / start_print phases too, which on a busy farm meant operators waited several minutes before the second printer even started receiving its file.

**Mechanic** — replaced the global "one at a time" gate in `_dispatcher_loop` with a `self._startup_lock` (asyncio.Lock) that wraps **only** each job's DB-write critical section: `archive_print` (the heavy `INSERT INTO print_archives`) plus queue-item linking, with an explicit `commit()` at the end. The lock releases right after that commit, so the FTP upload + MQTT `start_print` + post-write phases all run concurrently with the next job's setup. This eliminates the original deadlock root cause (an open write-txn left over from `db.flush()` was held throughout FTP, blocking the next job's INSERT for longer than SQLite's 15s busy timeout) without giving up parallelism on the long phases.

**Affected paths** — `_run_reprint_archive` and `_run_print_library_file`. Both wrap their DB-write phase in `acquire()` / `try / finally release()`. Reprint's lock window is just the optional `swap_macro_events_pending` pre-stamp commit; library-file's lock window covers archive INSERT + queue linking + commit. Post-FTP follow-up writes (swap-pending checklist tick, optional library-file cleanup, final commit) stay outside the lock — they're short and serialize naturally via SQLite's WAL + busy-timeout.

### Label-object metadata extraction

Two new fields are now extracted from each 3MF's `Metadata/project_settings.config` and stored alongside the existing print metadata:

- **`gcode_label_objects`** — Orca writes this explicitly; Bambu Studio doesn't (it always emits `label_object` markers in gcode), so a missing field defaults to `True` to match Bambu Studio's behaviour. Source of truth for "does this 3MF carry per-object identifiers usable for skip-objects on the printer".
- **`exclude_object`** — present in both slicers; emitted as-is when interpretable, no fallback when missing.

Both fields land in `library_files.file_metadata` (uploads + re-parses) and `print_archives.extra_data` (new prints + recovered fallback archives). Forward path is automatic — the existing `ThreeMFParser` populates them on every parse.

> **Slicer-side checklist (heads-up for OrcaSlicer users)**
>
> The skip-objects button only lights up when **both** flags are true in the source 3MF's project settings. Bambu Studio enables them by default; OrcaSlicer hides them inside the print profile and ships **with both off**, so an Orca-sliced file shows the print but not the skip-objects button until you flip them.
>
> 1. **Print Settings → Others → "Label objects"** — emits the per-object IDs (`M624`/`M625`) the firmware needs to address each piece. Without this the gcode has no object markers, so even when BamDude knows the object list it has nothing to send to the printer.
> 2. **Print Settings → Others → "Exclude objects"** — turns on the slicer-side metadata BamDude uses to find the printable objects in the first place.
>
> Both must be ticked **before slicing**. Re-sending an old 3MF without these flags won't work — re-slice with both on, re-upload, and the button shows up. Bambu Studio users don't need to do anything; the defaults are already correct there.

**Backfill at upgrade — heads-up: long startup on first boot after this release.** Migration `m022` opens every existing 3MF still on disk, reads one config file from inside the ZIP, and merges the two fields into the JSON column. Expect roughly 50-200 ms per file: an install with thousands of archives can spend several minutes inside the migration step before the API comes up. The seed step commits in batches of 100 and logs progress every batch (look for `m022 library_files: progress` / `m022 print_archives: progress` lines). Files that have been deleted from disk (history rows whose 3MF no longer exists) are skipped silently — those rows just stay without the new fields. Fully one-shot — runs once, then the `_migrations` table prevents re-runs.

### Fixes

- **Frontend recovers correctly from a long-backgrounded tab.** When a browser tab sat in the background for ~30 minutes (other tab focused, or another window in front), the WebSocket connection often stayed open in name only — the OS / browser had silently torn down the underlying socket without firing `onclose`. Bringing the BamDude tab forward then showed stale React-Query cache for several seconds while a new dispatch toast occasionally rendered "live" with already-finished events as the cache caught up. Three reinforcing changes:
  - **Pong-timeout watchdog** — every 30 s ping now arms a 10 s pong-timeout. If the server doesn't reply within 10 s the socket is considered dead and force-closed, kicking the standard reconnect path. Catches silent socket teardown that browsers used to hide.
  - **Visibility-sync** — `document.visibilitychange='visible'` triggers (a) `queryClient.invalidateQueries({ refetchType: 'all' })` so even non-active queries (cached pages the user is about to navigate to) refresh, and (b) an on-demand ping that reuses the same pong-timeout watchdog above. If the socket is alive, the pong arrives, nothing else happens — quick Alt+Tab no longer churns the connection. If the pong doesn't arrive within 10 s, the watchdog fires the standard close→reconnect path. The on-window-focus refetch built into TanStack Query already covered the active queries, but inactive queries (e.g. the Queue cache while you're reading Archives) used to stay stale indefinitely.
  - **"Reconnecting…" toast** — when the WS has been disconnected for >2 s, BamDude shows a persistent warning toast through the standard `ToastProvider` (same look as macro-executed / plate-not-empty / dispatch-progress notifications). 2 s grace prevents flicker during sub-second reconnects. Auto-dismisses on reconnect. Source of truth is a new `ConnectionContext` so any component can watch `isConnected` if needed.
- **`sqlite3.OperationalError: database is locked` on parallel dispatches.** Sending two prints to two different printers back-to-back could fail the second one mid-FTP — both jobs raced on `INSERT INTO print_archives` while one of them held an open write transaction through the entire FTP upload window, so SQLite's single-writer semantics gave up after the busy-timeout. Fixed by the new parallel-dispatch flow with a per-job startup-lock around the DB-write phase (see "Parallel dispatch with startup-lock" above). The interim "one job at a time across the whole farm" gate that landed earlier in the 0.4.1 cycle is gone — parallel sends now work without the deadlock.
- **Skip-objects state no longer leaks between prints.** `_load_objects_from_archive` previously left `printer.state.printable_objects` untouched when the parser returned an empty dict (e.g. Orca-sliced 3MFs where `support_skip_objects` was off — the runtime label_object markers in gcode work, but `slice_info.config` carries no `<object>` entries our parser can use). On the next print of a different file, the stale dict from the previous job remained in state — the skip-objects modal would either show object IDs that don't exist in the new gcode (firmware rejects the resulting `M623`), or silently misreport the count. State is now reset unconditionally as soon as the function is called for a given archive — if parsing yields nothing, the modal stays hidden, which is the truthful UI for "we don't know the object list for this print".
- **Skip-objects button now requires slicer-side support flags.** A 3MF can carry `printable_objects` metadata (so the count looks right) yet still be unusable for per-object skipping if the slicer profile didn't enable it — clicking the button would send `M623` for object IDs the firmware doesn't actually have markers for, and the firmware would reject. The button now also requires `gcode_label_objects=true` AND `exclude_object=true` in the source 3MF's `Metadata/project_settings.config` (extracted by the parser into `archive.extra_data`, surfaced as `skip_objects_supported` on the printer status payload). Affects three call-sites: `PrintersPage.tsx` (header button + disabled-state styling), `EmbeddedCameraViewer.tsx`, `CameraPage.tsx`. Old archives without the m022 backfill (3MF deleted before backfill ran) fail this gate cleanly — button hidden, no firmware-reject surprises.
- **Skip-objects button no longer "dies" 5 minutes into a print.** Per-printer `mqtt_connection_timeout` (default 300 s for printers imported from Bambuddy) periodically swaps `BambuMQTTClient` for a new instance via `ensure_fresh_connection`. The fresh state had `printable_objects={}` / `skip_objects_supported=False` until `on_print_start` re-fired and reloaded — but the duplicate-guard at the top of `on_print_start` (which exists to prevent fallback-archive duplicates on MQTT replays) returned early *before* the reload, so the skip-objects button went dark every ~5 min mid-print. Server restart "fixed" the symptom because it also wiped `_active_prints` (the dict the duplicate guard reads), letting the next on_print_start take the full path. The duplicate-guard branch now also re-runs `_load_objects_from_archive(active_archive)` so the freshly-created MQTT client state is repopulated alongside skipping the duplicate notification path.
- **Skip-objects state for printer-started fallback prints.** When a print starts directly from the printer screen / Send-to-Printer and BamDude can only fetch the 3MF on a later retry hook (`archive_download_retry`), the new `load_objects_from_archive_into_state` helper now runs as part of the successful attach — the skip-objects modal sees the object list as soon as the recovery completes, instead of waiting for the next BamDude restart or staying hidden for the whole print.
- **Dispatch toast progress bar no longer "sticks" past upload phase.** After upload finished `_set_active_upload_progress(job, 1, 1)` left `upload_bytes=upload_total_bytes=1` on the active dispatch state. Subsequent phases (`Running swap start macro…`, `Starting print on…`) updated the toast text but the progress bar kept rendering at 100% because nothing cleared the byte fields — looked like the file was still uploading while the swap macro was actually running. `_set_active_message` now resets both byte counters when a new phase begins; if a new upload phase starts, its first progress callback repopulates them.
- **No more 401 burst in the browser console after a long-idle tab.** Sliding-session refresh is reactive — when an access token expires the next request gets `401 Token has expired`, the client calls `/auth/refresh`, then retries. The user never gets kicked out, but the browser network panel logs every parallel 401 (20-40 of them when 4-5 React-Query keys re-fetch on tab-refocus). `client.ts` now decodes the JWT's `exp` claim and arms a one-shot timer to call `/auth/refresh` ~60 s before expiry, plus a near-expiry pre-flight check at the top of `request()` that awaits the same coalesced refresh promise when a request is about to leave with a near-dead token. Result: in the common "tab sat in background, came back" case the access token is already fresh when the visibility-sync invalidates queries — no 401s leave the browser, console stays clean. The reactive path stays as a fallback for direct-fetch call-sites (binary / streaming endpoints) and for failure modes where the proactive refresh didn't run (browser throttled `setTimeout`, refresh cookie expired). Decode failure (non-JWT or malformed token) safely degrades to the old reactive flow.
- **`print_finished` macro event has a translated label** in the macro settings table. The event landed in 0.4.0 (chamber-light symmetry, see "Auto-light-off superseded by macros" below) but `settings.macroEvents.print_finished` was missing from both `en.ts` and `uk.ts`, so the dropdown rendered the raw key. Fixed in both locales.
- **P2S printer card / archive thumbnail now shows the correct render.** `getPrinterImage()` mapped `p2s` to `/img/printers/p1s.png` because the project hadn't shipped a P2S asset yet — every P2S in the dashboard, queue widget, archive grid, and Telegram-bot status header rendered as a P1S. The 320 × 320 P2S render (sourced from Bambu Lab's wiki) is now bundled at `static/img/printers/p2s.png` and the mapping points at it. Existing P2S printers pick up the new image on next page load with no DB or config touch.
- **Swap `change_table` macro now survives a backend restart mid-print.** `_active_swap_config` was an in-memory dict — restarting BamDude between print-start and print-complete wiped the swap intent, and `on_print_complete` had nothing to act on (no `[SWAP] Running change_table` log entry). The intent is now also persisted to `print_archives.extra_data["swap_macro_events_pending"]` as a real checklist: every event the dispatch plans to fire is added at archive creation, and each event is removed from the list at the moment the corresponding macro fires successfully (`swap_mode_start` → removed by dispatch right after the macro returns; `swap_mode_change_table` → removed by `on_print_complete` right after the macro returns). When the list becomes empty the key drops entirely for clean state. Restart in the middle of a print: `on_print_complete` reads the remaining pending list and only fires what's still in it — a re-trigger of `on_print_complete` (MQTT replay, reconnect flap) finds the event already removed and does nothing. For library-file dispatches the initial marker is folded into `archive_print()`'s INSERT (same statement that creates the row, so no extra writer); for reprint dispatches the existing row is updated in the already-open dispatch session before FTP upload and any runtime-tracker contention. The earlier post-`start_print` standalone UPDATE was scrapped — it raced the runtime-tracker `UPDATE printers …` writer and timed out under SQLite's `busy_timeout`.

## [0.4.0.1] - 2026-04-24

Hotfix on top of `0.4.0`. `:latest` moves forward; `:0.4.0` stays pinned for anyone who wants it.

### Fixes

- **Legacy upgrade path restored** — migrating from BamDude 3.0.1 straight to 0.4.0 crashed at `m005_swap_profiles.seed()` with `no such column: printers.awaiting_plate_clear`. The migration used the ORM `select(Printer)` / `select(Macro)` helpers, which loaded every column from the *current* model — including `awaiting_plate_clear` (added by `m010`) and `action_type`/`mqtt_action`/`delay_seconds` (added by `m017`). Those columns don't exist yet at m005's point in the chain. Rewrote all three seed steps to use raw SQL with explicit column lists, touching only columns that genuinely existed at m005 time. New installs (tables built by `create_all`) keep working; legacy Bambuddy 3.0.1 upgraders now get through m005 cleanly.
- **Archive thumbnail render-thrash during uploads** — `api.getArchiveThumbnail(id)` and `getArchiveTimelapse(id)` built URLs with `?v=${Date.now()}`, so every re-render produced a new URL per `<img>` and the browser re-fetched every thumbnail. Dispatch-progress WebSocket (~5 Hz during FTP upload) turned this into a constant thumbnail refetch loop. Default URL is now stable (no cache-buster param); callers can pass an explicit `version` arg when they need genuine bust after archive media actually changed.
- **CI dedup across refs** — fast-forward merges (`dev → main`) triggered CI + Security twice on the exact same SHA (once per branch). Added `fkirc/skip-duplicate-actions@v5` as a first-job gate with `concurrent_skipping: 'same_content_newer'`, paths_ignore for `*.md` files. Same-SHA duplicate triggers now short-circuit every downstream job.
- **Daily-snapshot cron disabled** — back to explicit beta milestones (`vX.Y.ZbN`) for pre-release testing. Workflow stays in-repo, triggered via `workflow_dispatch` when a one-off daily is needed. Uncomment the `schedule:` block to restore the automation.
- **`CVE-2026-3219` (pip) suppressed in audit** — local-only archive-type confusion in pip itself (CVSS 4.6). BamDude runtime doesn't invoke pip, only image-build does, so blast radius is the build environment. pip upstream hasn't shipped a fix yet; suppressed via `--ignore-vuln CVE-2026-3219` in both CI and weekly security audit to stop the automated issue-bot from re-opening. Remove suppression once pip ships.

## [0.4.0] - 2026-04-24

Stable cut of the 0.4.0 cycle. Collects the upstream Bambuddy v0.2.3 → v0.2.3.2 port chain (three upstream releases in one BamDude cycle), multiple BamDude-native features (queue↔archive refactor `m019`, MFA cluster `m012`, sliding-session refresh tokens, per-project print plan, MQTT-action macros on print start/finish, spool metadata + auto-increment lots, cross-page navigation polish), and safety/security/UX fixes. `:latest` Docker tag now tracks this release. See [`UPDATING.md`](UPDATING.md) for the backup-first upgrade flow.

### Post-b2 polish (folded into 0.4.0)

- **Spool metadata — purchase_date + filament_diameter + lot** (migration `m020`). Purchase date is a separate DATETIME from `created_at` (import timestamp) — the inventory "Added" column prefers it when set. Filament diameter is a dropdown (`1.75` / `2.85`, defaulted + backfilled to `1.75`). Lot is a 1-based bundle position; quick-add mode replaces the number input with an "auto-increment lots" checkbox that tells bulk-create to number generated rows 1..N server-side. All three tokens added to the spool display-name template; `purchase_date` column is visible by default, `added_time` now defaults to hidden.
- **Inventory columns smarter defaults.** New default columns now land at their DEFAULT position (not appended to the end) for existing users, so added-over-time fields like `display_name` are visible immediately without re-arranging. When `rgba` (swatch) + `color_name` are both visible they collapse into one "Колір" column in the table; the visibility/order settings keep both entries separate. The ColumnConfigModal reads an optional `_full` suffixed i18n key for the verbose setting-dialog label (short label stays in the table header).
- **Password complexity relaxed.** Dropped the special-character requirement from create / change / reset paths — NIST SP 800-63B deprecates composition rules beyond length + a basic mix. Still enforced: upper + lower + digit + ≥8 chars.
- **Auto-light-off superseded by macros.** Dropped the `printers.auto_light_off` column (migration `m021`, SQLite copy-drop-rename, PostgreSQL `ALTER DROP COLUMN`). The equivalent chamber-light behaviour is now an mqtt-action macro on the `print_started` event — and for the symmetrical "turn it back on after print" a new `print_finished` event fires from `on_print_complete`. Both carry the full macro filter set (enabled / per-model / swap_mode_only / swap_profile / delay_seconds). Macros beat a single boolean: delay control, on/off symmetry, per-model targeting.
- **Queue/archive refactor polish.** Migration `m019` drops cached terminal counters from `printer_queues` and moves them to `print_archives.queue_id`, recomputed on read. Completed queue items auto-delete once their archive lands. Library-file delete cascade-deletes referencing queue items (or 409s if one is printing). Archive status badges show `error_message` on hover. Dispatch-time archive is born `'printing'` — no more transient "archived" flash.
- **Cross-page navigation polish.** QueueCard footer + printer-card summary row link to `/archives?printer=<id>` (filter pre-applied, then URL param stripped so refresh falls back to localStorage). Queue card headers + project-detail queue cards link printer names to `/#printer-<id>` with a smooth scroll on the printers page. Project-detail queue section now renders info-only "current print" cards per active job (thumbnail + progress bar tinted with project colour + ETA / layer count), sized on the same grid breakpoints as the main queue page.
- **Project-page active-print feed.** `PrintQueueItem.project_id` now exposed in the API response so the project page can filter `status='printing'` items client-side.
- **Error formatting.** Frontend `request()` helper stopped JSON-stringifying Pydantic 422 validation-error arrays — each entry's `msg` is surfaced (with the noisy "Value error, " pydantic v2 prefix stripped), joined by newlines. Toasts now render "Password must contain at least one uppercase letter" instead of `[{"type":"value_error",…}]`.
- **Fresh-install setup flow fixed.** Routes keyed off a legacy `authEnabled` flag (always `true` post-0.4.0) that made `SetupRoute` immediately redirect to `/login`, trapping fresh installs in a setup↔login ping-pong. Gates now key off the authoritative `requiresSetup` from `/auth/status`. `LoginPage` also proactively redirects to `/setup` when the backend still reports no admin, so the login form isn't visible while it can't possibly succeed. `setup_auth` proactively clears orphan `user_groups` rows before creating the new admin (covers the manual-SQL-delete + reset_admin paths).
- **K-profiles tab loading indicator.** Spool form's PA-profiles tab showed a confusing "No printers configured" while the K-profile fetch was in flight; now renders an explicit spinner + "Loading printer K-profiles…" until the per-printer fetch completes.
- **Assign-spool dialog** now displays the synthesised display name from the same template as the inventory table, and the search box does the same tokenised substring match — "SUN Bl" finds "SUNLU PETG Black".
- **Migration m002 tolerates post-m019 fresh installs.** `create_all()` runs first with the current model (no cached queue counters), so the m002 recount UPDATE used to fail with "no such column: completed_count" on every new container. Now guarded with `column_exists`.

### 0.4.0b2 — pre-release (2026-04-24)

Second beta milestone of the 0.4.0 cycle. Bundles every "(post-b1)" section below (printer card, archives UI, queue/dispatch follow-up, projects, library, authentication, macros, etc.) into a single image: `ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.0b2` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.0b2`.

### 0.4.0b1 — pre-release (2026-04-23)

First beta milestone of the 0.4.0 cycle. Everything listed below is in this image (`ghcr.io/kainpl/bamdude:0.4.0b1` / `kainpl/bamdude:0.4.0b1`). Intended for early testers — pin the exact tag, don't use `:latest` yet (still points at 0.3.2).

### Printer card (post-b1)

- **Hide the yellow "Позначити плиту як очищену" button while the green "Очистити стіл і почати наступний" CTA is visible.** Both buttons hit the same `POST /printers/{id}/clear-plate` endpoint; previously, a printer with pending auto-dispatch queue items after a finished print showed them both, which was confusing. The yellow universal status button now auto-hides whenever the `PrinterQueueWidget` is rendering its contextual green CTA (printer state FINISH/FAILED + awaiting_plate_clear + at least one non-manual queue item). Single-print direct flows with no queue still get the yellow button — one button per state, no duplicates. Query for pending queue items piggybacks on the widget's existing query via matching react-query key (zero extra network).

### Archives UI (post-b1)

- **Status badges for every non-completed state**, not only `failed`/`aborted`. `printing` now shows a blue pulsing "Друкується"/"Printing" chip with a spinning loader icon, clickable to jump to the owning printer's page. `archived` (source-hash dedup landed but never printed) shows as a grey chip. `cancelled` / `stopped` previously had no visual indication — now they share the red styling with failed. Happy-path `completed` stays badge-free so the layout doesn't clutter the common case. Applied in both the grid card and the list view so they line up.
- **Calendar view stopped labelling active prints as "Successful"**. The day-drawer used a binary success-else-failed split, which mislabelled anything that wasn't `failed`/`aborted` as "Successful" — including rows currently printing or just-archived. It now uses the same status badge as the grid: `completed` → green "Successful", `printing` → blue "Printing", the failure family → red, the rest → neutral gray with the actual status text. Day/overview counts (`успішно` / `невдало`) continue to track `completed` vs the whole failure family (`failed` / `aborted` / `cancelled` / `stopped`) — `printing` and `archived` are correctly excluded from both tiles.

### Queue / Dispatch (post-b1, follow-up)

- **Queue history moves into archives — live queue only tracks what's unfinished** (migration `m019`). Three new columns on `print_archives`: `queue_id` (FK to `printer_queues.id`, `SET NULL` on delete), `batch_id` (VARCHAR(36)), `error_message` (TEXT, verbose-diagnostic twin of `failure_reason`). The four cached counters on `printer_queues` (`completed_count`/`failed_count`/`cancelled_count`/`total_count`) are dropped — they went stale the moment queue items started auto-cleaning. `GET /printer-queues/` now recomputes them from `print_archives` grouped by `queue_id + status` on every request (one bulk query across all queues for the list endpoint; cached-counter replacement cost). `pending_count` + `skipped_count` stay on `printer_queues` since live pending / skipped items aren't cleaned up. Dispatch fills `archive.queue_id` + `archive.batch_id` at creation time — queue-driven prints copy from the queue item, external / Print-Now / direct-dispatch prints fall back to the printer's default queue so every archive is attributable. Seed pass backfills historical rows from `print_queue.archive_id` links where still present. One knock-on: archive rows for completed queue items now survive the queue item's own cleanup — that's the whole point of the refactor.
- **Completed queue items auto-delete once their archive lands.** `on_print_complete` removes the queue item after the archive transitions to `completed`, so the live queue table stays lean — only pending / skipped / failed / cancelled items remain, the ones operators still care about. Failed + cancelled + skipped keep their queue rows so the existing "Issues" section retry / unskip / remove UI continues to work. Queue history query load shifts to `print_archives`, which is already the source of truth for durable print metadata.
- **Dispatch-time archive is born in `'printing'`, not `'archived'`.** The library-file dispatch path (`background_dispatch._run_print_library_file`) now passes `print_data={"status": "printing"}` to `archive_print`, so the UI no longer flashes a transient "Archived" badge during the FTP + MQTT window between archive creation and the printer's first `on_print_start` tick. If dispatch fails after `archive_print` commits the row (FTP / start-print error), a fresh-session helper (`_mark_dispatch_archive_terminal`) flips the archive to `failed` / `cancelled` with the verbose `error_message` set so the zombie `'printing'` row doesn't sit stuck in the UI forever. Guard `WHERE status = 'printing'` so we don't clobber a legitimate `on_print_complete` transition that raced with the error path.
- **`current_item_id` no longer gets clobbered mid-print.** `set_queue_printing(queue_id, item_id=None)` used to unconditionally overwrite `current_item_id` with whatever was passed in; the `on_print_start` paths that call `mark_queue_printing_for_printer(printer_id)` without a queue-item id were wiping the scheduler's just-set pointer and the UI then lost track of which queued item was live. The helper now preserves the existing value when `item_id` is `None` — callers that legitimately want to clear the pointer (terminal transitions) use `set_queue_idle` instead.
- **Failed-archive hover shows the full error text.** `ArchiveResponse` exposes the new `queue_id` / `batch_id` / `error_message` fields, and the archive card + list status badge on `ArchivesPage` render `archive.error_message` (falling back to the short `failure_reason`) as the `title` tooltip — operators get the verbose diagnostic by hovering, without opening the detail modal.

- **Print-Now with quantity > 1 now routes ALL copies through the queue.** The earlier flow split a quantity=N direct-print into "primary via direct dispatch (no queue item) + N-1 siblings in queue". After a restart the sibling-queue scheduler tick could race with the primary's on_print_start hash-adopt and produce the archive duplicate we cleaned up last session. Fix: for `quantity == 1` direct dispatch stays as before; for `quantity > 1` the endpoint skips the direct path and calls `enqueue_batch_copies(count=quantity)`, so the user sees all copies in the queue from the moment they click "Print" (including reprint from the archive dialog). `BackgroundDispatchResponse.dispatch_job_id`/`dispatch_position` become nullable; the response status is `"queued"` for the N-copies path.
- **Queue items inherit `project_id` from linked library files** on every creation path: `/print-queue/` (PATCH + POST), `/library/files/add-to-queue` bulk add, and `enqueue_batch_copies`. Existing rows backfilled one-time via SQL. Project-page stats ("Завдання друку", "1 в процесі" tile) now count queued items that were added before the folder/file was linked to the project.
- **Library file delete now SET NULL on referring rows instead of CASCADE-wiping them.** `print_queue.library_file_id` FK changed from `ON DELETE CASCADE` to `ON DELETE SET NULL` (migration `m018`). The `delete_file` endpoint additionally nulls the column in-application on both `print_archives` and `print_queue` so SQLite installs (where `PRAGMA foreign_keys` is off by default) get the same behaviour as PostgreSQL. Queue history + archive history survive a source-file deletion with a dangling `library_file_id=NULL`.
- **Library file delete — queue items split into "block" vs "cascade" instead of silent SET NULL.** A `PrintQueueItem` with neither `library_file_id` nor `archive_id` is undispatchable, so the earlier SET-NULL policy left permanently dead rows cluttering the queue. Now `DELETE /library/files/{id}` does two things in-app: (1) if any queue item referencing the file is in `status='printing'`, return **409 `file_in_use`** with the blocking `queue_item_ids` — the operator has to wait for or stop the print first; (2) otherwise, cascade-delete every referencing queue item along with the file. Archives keep the SET NULL behaviour (they own their own 3MF copy in the archive dir, so history survives). `POST /library/bulk-delete` applies the same logic per file: blocked-by-printing files are skipped and counted under the existing `skipped_files` return field instead of the whole bulk aborting.
- **`created_by_id` audit on queue items.** `/library/files/add-to-queue` bulk add and both Telegram "add to queue" handlers now pass the acting user's id into `PrintQueueItem.created_by_id`. VP auto-queue + webhook trigger legitimately leave it `NULL` (no authenticated user to attribute). Direct-add endpoint + `enqueue_batch_copies` already did.
- **Project in-progress tile** on `ProjectDetailPage`: new blue "N в процесі / N in progress" line between the total and the parts-printed lines, driven by the existing `stats.in_progress_prints` counter. Visible only while at least one queue item for the project is in `printing` state.
- **`/projects/{id}/archives` MissingGreenlet crash** on the project archives tab fixed by eager-loading `PrintArchive.created_by` in `list_project_archives`. `archive_to_response` accesses `created_by.username`; the previous query only eager-loaded `project`, so rendering triggered a lazy load against an already-returned async session.
- **`/projects/{id}/timeline` 500 crash** fixed: the timeline handler was accessing `item.print_name` on `PrintQueueItem` rows, but that column only exists on `PrintArchive`. Timeline description now falls back through `archive.print_name → archive.filename → library_file.filename → "(unnamed queue item)"`, with `selectinload` for the archive + library_file relationships so no lazy loads fire.

### Queue / Dispatch (post-b1)

- **Single dispatch layer — kills the double-archive race.** Before: two orchestrators could both call `archive_print()` for the same (printer, library_file) if their timing collided — scheduler's `print_scheduler._dispatch_item` (queue-item branch) and `background_dispatch._run_print_library_file` (direct "Print Now"). Result: two archive rows for one physical print, one carrying `library_file_id`/`project_id`/`created_by_id` but stuck at `status='archived'`, the other getting `status='printing'` from `on_print_start` hash-adoption but with most metadata `NULL`. Now: the scheduler's `_start_print` is a thin wrapper that delegates the full patch → archive → FTP → register_expected → MQTT-start pipeline to `background_dispatch.run_from_queue_item()`. Only one dispatch runs per queue item; its archive row carries all the metadata and is wired to the queue item via `PrintQueueItem.archive_id` in the same transaction as archive creation. Removes ~250 lines of duplicated code from the scheduler. Stagger / is-idle / auto-drying / cancel-handling stays in scheduler — it's still the queue orchestrator, just stops executing prints itself.
- **Race-dup archive cleanup** — `scripts/merge_race_duplicate_archives.py`. Finds archive pairs with same `printer_id + content_hash + file_path` created within 60 s and asymmetric `library_file_id` (the classic race signature), picks a survivor row (queue-referenced first, then furthest along lifecycle, then started_at-set, then newer id), backfills every null-but-present field from the orphan, re-points any dependent `print_queue.archive_id`, deletes the orphan. `--dry-run` by default, `--apply` to commit, `--loose` prints broader candidates (same-hash within 5 min, at least one `archived`) for manual review without touching them.

### Macros (post-b1)

- **MQTT-action macros on `print_started` event.** Macros can now carry `action_type='mqtt_action'` instead of G-code — invoking a named MQTT command (`chamber_light_off`, `chamber_light_on` on MVP) exactly when the printer's `gcode_state` transitions into `RUNNING`. Use case: auto-turn-off chamber lights once printing actually starts. Optional `delay_seconds` field (0-3600) defers firing — 0 = immediate. Fire-and-forget so a long delay doesn't stall the print-start handler. Matcher re-uses the existing (event, printer_model, enabled) filter; `swap_mode_only` + `swap_profile` are hidden in the UI for non-swap events since they don't apply. Migration `m017` adds `action_type`, `mqtt_action`, `delay_seconds` columns with defaults compatible with pre-existing gcode macros. Frontend gains action-type toggle + mqtt-action dropdown + delay input + `MQTT` badge in the macro list (EN/UK i18n).

### Projects (post-b1)

- **Per-project print plan table**. The "Files" section on each project page is now a flat, ordered list of that project's `.3mf` library files with a copies stepper, reorder arrows, per-row totals (filament, objects, time, cost), per-row unlink button, and a grand-totals strip at the bottom (Plates / Objects / Filament / Time / Cost). "Plates" = Σ copies — each copy prints as one build plate. Plan rows auto-appear when a folder is linked to a project, a file is moved into a linked folder, or a file is directly attached; they auto-disappear on unlink or delete. Cost uses the system-wide `default_filament_cost` (same fallback as archives) multiplied by per-row weight × copies. Migration `m016` creates `project_print_plan_items`; existing `library_files.project_id` links backfill as one row per file at copies=1. Linked-folder chips remain as a compact strip so context doesn't disappear — the full folder browser still lives in File Manager.
- **Per-file project link from the File Manager**. Until now, a file could only land in a project via its folder — now each file card (grid) and each list row has its own `Link2` button/chip that opens a project picker (`LinkFileModal`), mirroring the folder-link UX. Linked files show a blue chip with `Briefcase` icon; unlinked files show a hover-revealed gray icon. Per-file unlink happens via the same modal (or the red `Unlink` button on each plan row inside the project page). `FileListResponse` now exposes `project_id` so the UI can render link state without a second round-trip.
- **File → project attachment now cascades into the library**. Linking a folder to a project backfills `project_id` on every file inside; moving a file into a project-linked folder (single `PUT /files/{id}` or bulk `POST /files/move`) inherits the folder's project; explicit per-file `project_id` still wins. Previously the column only filled on fresh uploads through some paths.

### Library (post-b1)

- **File cards show weight + object count**. Both File Manager grid cards and the per-project file list render `Package` filament-weight and `Box` object-count badges (alongside the existing print time and sliced-for model). Extracted from the 3MF on upload; comes for free for virtual-printer uploads too since they use the same parser. `LibraryFile(List)Response` now exposes a flat `object_count` field so the frontend doesn't have to dig into `metadata.printable_objects`.

### Authentication (post-b1)

- **Sliding-session refresh tokens** (§18.14). Access-token TTL drops from 24 h to 1 h, but no one has to re-log in every hour — a new rotating refresh cookie transparently refreshes the access JWT in the background. Leaving a tab open for a week keeps you signed in; closing the browser without the Remember-me checkbox logs you out (12 h DB cap regardless). **Remember me for 30 days** checkbox on the login form opts into persistent sessions — cookie survives browser restarts. Cookie is HttpOnly + SameSite=Lax + Path=/api/v1/auth so XSS can't exfiltrate it and non-auth endpoints never see it. `Secure` attribute auto-detects from request scheme (reverse-proxy `X-Forwarded-Proto` aware) so the same image works on LAN HTTP and HTTPS production without config; `AUTH_REFRESH_COOKIE_SECURE=true|false` env forces the polarity if needed. Refresh-token rotation + reuse detection (OWASP) — a replayed cookie collapses the whole family across devices. Logout / password change / admin 2FA kill all revoke the user's refresh tokens. Frontend retries failed 401s transparently through /auth/refresh, with promise-coalescing so a wave of parallel queries spawns exactly one refresh call. New migration `m015` adds `used_at` + `family_id` to `auth_ephemeral_tokens`.
- **Silent-logout UI redirect** — complementary to the sliding session. If refresh ALSO fails (refresh token expired, revoked, or never issued), a global `bamdude:auth-invalidated` event clears React state and hard-redirects to `/login` instead of sitting on stale UI with every background poll 401'ing into a void. Visibility-change handler triggers proactive `/auth/me` revalidation when a hidden tab regains focus, surfacing an expired session in under a second instead of waiting for a manual action.

### v0.2.3.2 port — safety, queue reliability, AMS UX

### Safety

- **Bed-jog "Home Z" no longer crashes the bed into the toolhead on H2C / H2D / H2S / X1** (upstream #1052). The bed-jog dialog's button sent a bare `G28 Z` over `gcode_line`. On Bambu printers where the Z endstop is at the top (bed moves UP into it), `G28 Z` skips the toolhead-park step that a full `G28` runs first, so the bed rises at full speed with nothing getting out of the way. The reporter only escaped damage because the toolhead happened to be parked on the purge chute. The `/printers/{id}/home-axes` endpoint and `BambuClient.home_axes()` now always send bare `G28` regardless of the `axes` argument, triggering the firmware's safe multi-step routine (park toolhead → home XY → home Z). Frontend button retitled "Auto Home" with dialog copy updated in en + uk so users aren't surprised when X/Y motion happens before Z.
- **"Not homed" warning no longer re-prompts after Auto Home** (upstream #1052 follow-up, `1de4e409`). Clicking "Auto Home" sent the G28 and closed the modal but never set the session-scoped `bamdude.bedJog.warned.<printerId>` flag (only "Move anyway" did), so the next jog click in the same session re-opened the warning — even though the printer was now homed. The `homeAxes` mutation's `onSuccess` handler now flips the same flag. Warning still fires once per printer per session as a safety guard.

### Queue Reliability

- **H2D false-reprint fix** (upstream #1078). The scheduler watchdog reverted queue items to `pending` at 45 s if `gcode_state` hadn't transitioned — on H2D Pro firmware 01.01.00.00 the state stays at `FINISH` for 48–55 s after the printer accepts `project_file` but before `PREPARE`, so the watchdog kept reverting items the printer had already started physically printing, and the next scheduler tick re-dispatched them as a reprint. `_watchdog_print_start` now also exits early when `subtask_id` advances past the pre-dispatch value (the printer echoes the `submission_id` we minted back in its next `push_status` — the signal lands long before `gcode_state` does on slow firmware). Timeout bumped 45 s → 90 s as belt-and-braces for printers that neither flip state nor echo `subtask_id` inside the window.
- **Batch double-dispatch fix** (upstream `950286ad` / v0.2.3.2 re-release). When an ASAP print with `quantity > 1` landed on an H2D or P1-series, the scheduler's next tick saw MQTT `IDLE` (state lagging the accepted command) and dispatched the second batch item onto the already-running printer — surfaced via the "BUG: Multiple queue items in 'printing' status" log. `check_queue` now seeds `busy_printers` from `PrinterQueue.status='printing'` before iterating, so any printer with an outstanding dispatch is excluded regardless of what MQTT currently reports. Also catches external / direct prints that don't have a corresponding queue item row.
- **Clear-Plate button latency fix** (upstream #939 follow-up, `4e86e8cb`). The card-level "Mark plate as cleared" button appeared 30 s – 5 min late because the WebSocket `printer_status` payload didn't carry `awaiting_plate_clear` — the frontend merged the stale `false` until the next 30 s REST poll refreshed it. `printer_state_to_dict` now emits the flag from the authoritative in-memory + DB-backed accessor so the button appears within ~100 ms of the gate being armed.

### Printer UI

- **Per-card plate name for multi-plate prints** (upstream #881, `fa1c46d9`). When two printers run different plates of the same multi-plate 3MF, the Printers page cards now show the actual plate name (or "Plate N" fallback) instead of just the 3MF filename — no more guessing which printer is on which plate. Sourced via `current_archive_id` (resolved from MQTT `subtask_id`) + `current_plate_id` (parsed from `gcode_file` path). Plate transitions reflect within ~100 ms via WebSocket push; single-plate prints stay clean.
- **Queue-card progress no longer flashes stale 100%** (upstream `950286ad`). Right after dispatch, the per-queue-card progress bar briefly showed the previous print's final `mc_percent` before snapping to 0% once the new print started ticking. Progress / ETA / layer fields are now gated on `status.state ∈ {RUNNING, PAUSE}` — in any other state (FINISH from the prior print, IDLE, PREPARE while heating) the bar renders at 0% with no stale ETA or layer count.

### AMS / Filament

- **AMS-HT slot shows saved preset after Configure** (upstream #1053, `87a5aa36`). `GET /printers/{id}/slot-presets` keyed AMS-HT entries at `ams_id * 4 + tray_id = 512`, but frontend lookups use `ams_id` directly for HT (single-slot units share their global tray id with the unit id). Regular AMS worked because all three formulas agreed; HT always fell through to `tray.tray_type` ("Generic PLA") even after a custom preset was saved. Backend now keys via a helper that mirrors frontend `getGlobalTrayId`.
- **Configure AMS slot preserves PFUS preset id** (upstream #1053, `28f80f94`). When Bambu Cloud returned `filament_id=null` for a user preset that inherits from a generic base (e.g. `Sting3D ABS` on `Generic ABS @BBL H2D`), the modal fell back to `convertToTrayInfoIdx(base_id)` — which collapsed the slot's filament identity to the generic's `filament_id`. OrcaSlicer / BambuStudio Sync Filaments then resolved the slot to "Generic ABS" and the custom preset never appeared on the printer LCD. The `base_id` fallback is removed: when the cloud detail has a distinct `filament_id` we still prefer it; otherwise the `setting_id` default (`PFUS…` / `PFSP…`) stands untouched.
- **Assign Spool surfaces manually-added inventory spools** (upstream #1047, `bc895dff`). Three related AMS-UX fixes. (1) Empty-slot hover card lost its Configure / Assign actions after a firmware-reset because a prior gate keyed off `tray?.state === 10`, but real firmware reports state=9 (or omits state entirely) after a user-initiated reset — gate removed (the hover card only renders for empty tray types anyway). (2) The spool filter in Assign Spool required exact `slicer_filament_name` equality, hiding every manually-added inventory spool that doesn't populate the field — filter now prefers exact slicer-profile match when both sides have one, and falls back to partial material overlap either direction ("PLA" spool shows up for a "PLA Basic" slot). (3) The profile-mismatch warning dialog fired on every Generic spool because slicer profile names carry an `@printer` qualifier while the tray stores the bare base name — both the filter and `checkProfileMatch` now strip everything from `@` onward before comparing.
- **Malformed rgba no longer bricks the Filaments page** (upstream #1055, `bf5135cb`). A single legacy spool with a 7-char rgba (`FFFFFFF`, one `F` missing) caused `GET /api/v1/inventory/spools` to 500 with a pydantic validation error, leaving the Filaments page blank and "Add Spool" silently failing. Three layers fixed: (1) the spool update schema now enforces the same `^[0-9A-Fa-f]{8}$` pattern the create schema had, so PATCH can't plant malformed values; (2) the hex input on the spool form normalises to a full 8-char `RRGGBBAA` on every keystroke — 8-char paste passes through, 7-char drops the stray, shorter input right-pads with `0` + `FF` alpha; (3) the response schema drops the pattern so a single bad legacy row renders with a default color instead of 500'ing the whole list endpoint.

### Authentication / Security

- **Spoolman iframe on plain-HTTP LAN hosts** (upstream #1054, `07ef0427`). The strict CSP shipped in 0.4.0 whitelisted only `https:` for `frame-src`, so the Filament tab's Spoolman iframe was blocked on the typical self-host setup where Spoolman runs on plain HTTP on a LAN. Now `frame-src 'self' http: https:` — `frame-ancestors 'none'` still prevents BamDude itself from being framed cross-origin (the defence that actually matters for clickjacking).
- **python-dotenv floor-pinned ≥ 1.2.2 for CVE-2026-28684**. Transitive via `pydantic-settings`; explicit pin with inline comment in `requirements.txt`.
- **Library-print archive tracks the user who clicked Print** (upstream #730 follow-up, `276a1db3`). Previously archives created by the printer-card "Print" button, File Manager prints, and Library-file prints all landed with `created_by_id=NULL` — invisible to the per-user statistics filter, and the post-print user-targeted notification had no recipient. `archive_print()` now receives `created_by_id` through the dispatch job, and `set_current_print_user` is called after `start_print` success so the rest of the pipeline sees the right user.
- **Change Password modal no longer hijacks the Printers search input** (upstream `597d961b`). Opening the sidebar's Change Password modal while on the Printers page rendered the "Search printers" input as a masked password field and kept it that way after closing the modal. Root cause: the modal had three `type=password` inputs with no accompanying username anchor, so password managers (1Password, Bitwarden, browser built-ins) hunted the DOM for a matching text input and latched onto the unlabelled search bar. Layout now has a hidden `autocomplete=username` anchor at the top of the Change Password form; the Printers search input is hardened with `type=search`, `name=printer-search`, `autoComplete=off`, `data-1p-ignore`, and `data-lpignore`.

### Inventory

- **Configurable spool display name** (BamDude-native). Settings → Filament → Spool display name exposes a template with `{brand} {material} {color_name}` as the default, plus 12 other placeholders (subtype, slicer preset, note, label/remaining weight in g / kg / %, colour hex, cost per kg). A new "Name" column (opt-in via Column Config) renders the synthesised name and sorts on it — `localeCompare` with `numeric: true` so digits inside the name sort naturally. The Filaments page search now accepts partial tokens against the synthesised name: typing `SUN Bl` finds `SUNLU PETG Black`; every whitespace-separated token must appear as a case-insensitive substring somewhere in the name. Unknown placeholders stay visible in the live preview so typos surface before save. Template lives in the generic settings table (key `spool_display_template`) — no migration, instant switch without a reload.

### Library / Archives

- **Archive ↔ library-file link + usage stats backfill** (BamDude-native). New `print_archives.library_file_id` column — populated at dispatch time when printing from the Library, fills in retroactively when the 3MF download recovery path attaches a file by hash match. The `library_files.print_count` + `last_printed_at` columns are backfilled from the archive history on migration (completed prints only, matching the live-update policy), and the live-update path now also fires for direct library-file prints, not just queue-driven ones. New migration `m014`.
- **Direct-Print from a printer card no longer pollutes File Manager** (upstream #730 / `1682b695`). Clicking "Print" on a printer card (or drag-drop-onto-card) used to upload the file, persist it as a Library file, dispatch the print, and then leave the Library row + disk file behind — every transient one-off print added a stray File Manager entry the user never asked to save. New `cleanup_library_after_dispatch` flag on the print-library-file request is set only by the Direct-Print flow; when set, `_run_print_library_file` stages `db.delete(lib_file)` in the same transaction as `archive_print()` so a mid-flight failure rolls both back cleanly, commits together, then unlinks the library disk file + thumbnail after commit. File Manager Print and Project Detail Print leave the flag unset — those entries are there by user intent. External library files (`is_external=True`) are never touched.

### Documentation / Install

- **Root-level `UPDATING.md`** added — GitHub-visible landing doc with short-form update flow (backup → pull → verify) pointing at the full guide in `docs/getting-started/upgrading.md`. Covers Docker, scripted native (`install/update.sh`), and manual native paths.
- **Upgrading docs now recommend UI Local Backup** (upstream `66d65f42`). `Settings → Backup → Local Backup → Create Backup` → `Download Backup` is the preferred path on native / self-install — captures the SQLite DB, archives, thumbnails, uploads, and config in one zip. Shell `tar czf` documented as fallback for CI automation. Rollback flow updated to prefer the matching UI Restore.

### Tooling / CI

- **i18n parity gate** (adapted from upstream `c894899b`). New `frontend/scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` — parses `en.ts` and `uk.ts` via the TypeScript compiler API, validates key-set equality, per-leaf `{{placeholder}}` consistency, and plural-suffix integrity. Ukrainian CLDR categories (`_one` / `_few` / `_many` / `_other`) are whitelisted as legitimate additions vs English (`_one` / `_other`) as long as the matching `_one` counterpart exists in the same locale. Wired into `test_frontend.sh` and the `frontend-lint` CI job. Caught one real missing key on the first run (`printers.queue.inQueue_other`) — added.

### Internal

- **Door-open badge scoped to X1-family only**. `home_flag` bit 23 is reliably reverse-engineered on X1, X1C, and X1E; on P1S / P2S / H2-series the same bit is undocumented and observed permanently 0 on some firmwares, so the badge was misleading. Open-frame models (P1P, A1, A1 Mini) never had a door to begin with. A new `DOOR_SENSOR_MODELS` set in `backend/app/utils/printer_models.py` mirrored by a frontend `hasDoorSensor()` helper gates both the MQTT parser and the UI badge; only the three X1 models surface a door state now.

---

### v0.2.3 + v0.2.3.1 port — baseline for 0.4.0

Upstream Bambuddy v0.2.3 sync. Fifteen commits covering queue-reliability (#887/#936/#961/#967/#972), drying gate (#971), FTP salvage & short-circuit, camera snapshot cleanup (#979), SD/door telemetry, and a half-dozen smaller fixes. New migration `m010` adds `subtask_id` (archives) + `awaiting_plate_clear` (printers).

A follow-up cycle pulls upstream **v0.2.3.1** (one-week patch release after v0.2.3) — 14 of 17 content commits ported. Highlights:

- **MQTT task_id int32 cap for P1S** (#1042). `submission_id = epoch_ms % 2_147_483_647` prevents P1S firmware 01.10.00.00 from clamping oversized identifiers and treating every dispatch as a continuation of the previous FAILED job.
- **FTP zombie-thread wait** (#1014). `download_file_async` now waits up to `min(timeout, 30 s)` for the worker thread to finish via a `threading.Event` instead of a fixed 0.5 s sleep. Slow WiFi links overshoot `ftp_timeout` by 10–30 s without being stuck; the old short sleep gave up and started attempt 2 while attempt 1's RETR was still progressing, producing a zombie write race.
- **Obico Status banner clears on next success** (#172 follow-up). One-line `self._last_error = None` after a successful detection cycle so a transient cold-start RTSP failure doesn't leave the banner stuck forever.
- **3MF archive sendfile-truncation guard** (#1032). `archive_print()` and `attach_3mf_to_archive()` replace `shutil.copy2` with an explicit chunked read/write + `fsync`. After the copy, if the source was a valid ZIP but the dest isn't, the archive row is refused and the partial file is removed instead of the silent BadZipFile downstream when the UI later tries to render plates. The silent `except Exception: pass` in `ThreeMFParser.parse()` now logs a WARNING for the same reason.
- **Smart-plug startup MQTT subscriptions** (#1010). Three code paths configuring an MQTT smart plug (startup restore, create route, update route) had drifted; the startup loop short-circuited on `if plug.mqtt_topic:` and silently skipped plugs whose topics were only set in the newer per-type fields (`mqtt_power_topic`/`mqtt_state_topic`/`mqtt_energy_topic`). Extracted a shared `subscribe_plug_to_mqtt()` helper and routed all three through it so the schema can't drift again. Shelly-via-ioBroker setups no longer lose their power/state readings on every restart.
- **`LibraryFile.print_count` + `last_printed_at` written on completion** (#1008). Both fields existed and were displayed in the File Manager but nothing ever wrote to them. New helper `_bump_library_file_usage_if_completed()` runs in `on_print_complete`'s queue-status path, increments count + stamps timestamp on successful completion only (failed/cancelled/aborted don't count). New migration `m013` adds the `print_count` column for installs upgrading from v0.4.0 → next.
- **Firmware wiki regex for P2S/X2D anchors** (#1030). Two regex fixes in `_fetch_all_versions_from_wiki()`: (1) anchor-id dash made optional (P2S/X2D use `h-0102000020260409`, no dash); (2) text-fallback paren-regex accepts both ASCII `()` and full-width `（）` (U+FF08/U+FF09) used by P2S/X2D/A1/A1-mini.
- **Post-login thumbnails race** (upstream 32c0b169). `useStreamTokenSync` now keys the camera-stream-token query on `user.id` so a login/logout invalidates the React Query cache automatically — the old global key cached a 401 from the login page for 50 minutes. New `rewriteMediaSrcWithToken(root, token)` DOM walker retrofits already-rendered `<img>`/`<video>` `/api/v1/` sources when the token transitions null→value; previously the module-level token variable wasn't reactive and pages stayed showing broken thumbnails until reload. `CameraPage` mirrors the user-id-keyed query.
- **Printer-card controls overflow fix** (upstream 66fe4860). `flex-wrap` instead of breakpoint-conditional `max-[550px]:` so controls never escape narrow card widths in Chrome.
- **Plate-clear UI on printer cards** (#939). New `PlateClearedIcon`, plate-status pill (Plate Clear / Plate not Clear / Plate in Use), and a "Mark plate as cleared" button rendered both as a compact icon next to the status line and as a full-width button in expanded view. Visible only when `require_plate_clear` is true on the printer. Updates `PrinterStatus.awaiting_plate_clear` optimistically on success.
- **File Manager "Collapse folders by default" toggle** (#996). New toggle in the sidebar header next to "Wrap" — folder tree opens fully expanded by default; flipping to collapsed re-keys every top-level `FolderTreeItem` so the change takes effect without a page reload. Persists to `localStorage['library-collapse-folders']`.
- **Color-catalog default filter** (#1039). Initial dropdown value flipped from "Bambu Lab" to "All Manufacturers" — backend resolution still prefers Bambu Lab on hex collisions so the user-visible naming stays intact.
- **Dockerfile runtime image bumped to Debian Trixie** (`python:3.13-slim` → `python:3.13-slim-trixie`). Picks up ffmpeg 5→7 and OpenSSL 3.0→3.3.
- **`.trivyignore` extended** with three Debian-postponed CVEs (CVE-2026-6385 ffmpeg DVD subs, CVE-2026-30997 ffmpeg AV1, CVE-2026-6192 openjpeg JPEG2000) — all unreachable in BamDude's actual ffmpeg use (printer-camera RTSP/MJPEG/H.264, no DVD/AV1/JPEG2000 paths).

Skipped: upstream version-bump commit (BamDude carries its own versioning).

### Queue Reliability

- **Signal-based MQTT zombie detection** (#887). The existing `ensure_fresh_connection` was a time-based preflight; it couldn't tell the difference between a quiet but healthy session and a half-broken one where our publishes silently drop. New mechanism: `BambuMQTTClient.force_reconnect_stale_session(reason)` closes the MQTT socket so paho auto-reconnects with a fresh session. Triggered by three signal-based paths: `ams_filament_setting` unanswered 2×10 s, dev-mode probe unanswered 2× (already landed, now routed through the shared helper), and `_verify_print_response` in `background_dispatch` after an unacked `start_print`. The preflight is kept for Telegram-handler UX predictability.
- **`_watchdog_print_start` in print_scheduler** (#967). Queue item marked "printing" after MQTT publish that the printer never processes used to stay stuck forever. The watchdog polls for a state transition and, if none arrives within 45 s, reverts the item to `pending` and force-reconnects the MQTT session. Swap-mode-safe: if `swap_mode_start` already physically moved the plate, the revert is skipped (we don't re-fire the macro on retry) and the operator is asked to intervene instead.
- **`PrintArchive.subtask_id` + resume pre-check** (#972). The printer's `submission_id` (the new unique-per-invocation identifier in `start_print`) is captured as `subtask_id` on `print_archives`. `on_print_start` consults it as a pre-match before the name-based lookup, so a backend restart mid-print adopts the right row even when two archives share a `print_name`. Also backfills `subtask_id` onto rows that matched by name on older runs. Unlike upstream's companion revive-from-cancelled path we deliberately do NOT un-cancel terminal rows — BamDude removed upstream's 4-hour stale-cancel heuristic long ago (it killed legitimate long prints), so there's nothing to revive.
- **`printers.awaiting_plate_clear` persisted column** (#961). The plate-clear gate used to live in an in-memory set; an Auto Off power cycle dropped it and the queue auto-dispatched onto a dirty plate. Now persisted to DB + rehydrated at startup. Semantics inverted: presence means **blocked on user confirmation** (absence = clear to dispatch). Armed at end of `on_print_complete` when `require_plate_clear` is true and no swap path already cleared the plate. Frontend: `plateCleared` prop/field renamed to `awaitingPlateClear`; `clear_plate` endpoint now also accepts `IDLE` state so post-Auto-Off recovery works. Upstream's parallel move of `require_plate_clear` to a global setting is deliberately NOT ported — BamDude keeps the per-printer column with default `true`.
- **`submission_id` on `start_print`** (#1011). Replaces the hardcoded `project_id / subtask_id / task_id = "0"` with a millisecond-timestamp UUID. Third-party MQTT observers (OctoEverywhere etc.) used to treat every reprint as a continuation of the prior task because `task_id` collided.

### MQTT & Telemetry

- **Permissive SD-card parsing** (#899 / #DD349954 / #0D7C0D40). The old `data["sdcard"] is True` check rejected every non-bool form real firmware emits (integer `1`, `"HAS_SDCARD_NORMAL"`, `"normal"`); the flag went False on any firmware that uses the enum form.
